#################### 00_READ_ME_FIRST_MASTER_INDEX.txt ####################

POIETEK / STUDIO DAW STATION (SDS)
MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 1.0

Compiled from:
1) The complete product-development conversation supplied by the creator.
2) Deep review of GitHub repository: jayprophit/Studio-Daw-Station-SDS-
3) Current cross-platform/offline-first architecture recommendations.

WORKING PRODUCT NAME
Poietek is the working ecosystem name discussed in the design conversation.
Studio DAW Station (SDS) is the current repository/product label.
Final public branding should be chosen only after trademark/domain checks.

CORE RULE
One app. One project model. One creative graph. Many workspaces.
Offline first. Cloud optional. Hardware agnostic. Collaboration capable.
Professional depth without forcing beginners to see every advanced control.

LEGAL / CLEAN-ROOM RULE
Competitor products, patents, papers, hardware and interfaces may be studied for
functional principles, workflows and interoperability requirements. Do not copy
proprietary source code, protected assets, branded UI expression, undocumented
confidential protocols, or patented claims without appropriate legal review or
licensing. Compatibility layers should use documented/open interfaces wherever
possible.

===============================================================================

PURPOSE OF THIS BATCH
This ordered document set is designed so a human developer or an AI coding agent
can consume the project in sequence without losing the original vision.

The current GitHub repository is a useful UI/interaction prototype, not yet a
production DAW. The correct strategy is evolutionary:
- preserve useful React UI and workflow ideas;
- replace simulated/demo logic with stable engines and domain services;
- introduce a local-first project model before adding more surface features;
- add platform adapters so desktop, tablet, mobile and web share one project;
- add collaboration, rights, video and community as modules around the same data.

HOW AN AI CODING AGENT SHOULD USE THESE FILES
1. Read 00-05 before changing architecture.
2. Read 06-12 before implementing the relevant subsystem.
3. Use 13 for implementation order.
4. Use 14 as the controlling agent prompt.
5. Use 15 as the definition of done.
6. Never implement a UI label as though the underlying feature exists. A feature
   is not complete until its domain model, persistence, engine behavior, error
   states, tests and platform capability checks exist.

NON-NEGOTIABLE PRODUCT REQUIREMENTS
- Core creation works offline.
- Internet is not required to open/edit a local project.
- Projects can later sync between a user's devices.
- Team projects support asynchronous and live collaboration.
- Audio/video binaries are not stored inside CRDT text structures.
- Every meaningful change is reversible or versioned.
- Hardware mappings are device-agnostic internally.
- Desktop, mobile, tablet and web use the same logical project format.
- Platform capability differences are explicit rather than hidden.
- AI actions are previewable, explainable and reversible.
- Quick Export never forces rights IDs.
- Release/Publish can optionally collect full rights and credit metadata.
- All contributors can be credited: artists, producers, engineers, musicians,
  visual artists, editors, camera crew, labels/publishers and other roles.
- Internal community sharing and external publishing are separate capabilities.
- The app may be free/community-driven; cloud costs can be separated from core
  creation if necessary without locking fundamental creative tools behind packs.

PRODUCT LAYERS
Layer 1 — Creator Studio
Layer 2 — Hardware Bridge
Layer 3 — Local Project/Asset System
Layer 4 — Sync & Collaboration
Layer 5 — AI/Analysis
Layer 6 — Rights/Credits
Layer 7 — Community/Marketplace
Layer 8 — Publishing/Distribution
Layer 9 — Developer Ecosystem

THE SINGLE MOST IMPORTANT FIRST ENGINEERING MOVE
Create a canonical serializable Project domain model and LocalProjectRepository.
Until that exists, adding more UI modules will increase technical debt.

#################### 01_PRODUCT_VISION_PRINCIPLES_USER_STORIES.txt ####################

POIETEK / STUDIO DAW STATION (SDS)
MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 1.0

Compiled from:
1) The complete product-development conversation supplied by the creator.
2) Deep review of GitHub repository: jayprophit/Studio-Daw-Station-SDS-
3) Current cross-platform/offline-first architecture recommendations.

WORKING PRODUCT NAME
Poietek is the working ecosystem name discussed in the design conversation.
Studio DAW Station (SDS) is the current repository/product label.
Final public branding should be chosen only after trademark/domain checks.

CORE RULE
One app. One project model. One creative graph. Many workspaces.
Offline first. Cloud optional. Hardware agnostic. Collaboration capable.
Professional depth without forcing beginners to see every advanced control.

LEGAL / CLEAN-ROOM RULE
Competitor products, patents, papers, hardware and interfaces may be studied for
functional principles, workflows and interoperability requirements. Do not copy
proprietary source code, protected assets, branded UI expression, undocumented
confidential protocols, or patented claims without appropriate legal review or
licensing. Compatibility layers should use documented/open interfaces wherever
possible.

===============================================================================

1. PRODUCT DEFINITION

Poietek/SDS is a universal creative production environment that combines:
- professional DAW workflows;
- sampling and chopping;
- beat and MIDI sequencing;
- physical hardware mapping/control;
- modular racks and routing;
- recording, editing, mixing and mastering;
- video editing and visual effects;
- AI-assisted analysis and creation;
- cross-device local-first projects;
- team collaboration;
- rights/credit/contribution tracking;
- internal creator community;
- publishing/export;
- extensibility through plugins, device profiles, scripts and templates.

The original MPC Sample idea remains important but is no longer the product
centre. The centre is the creator's project. MPC Sample, larger MPC devices,
Roland SP-series samplers, MIDI keyboards, e-drums, DJ controllers, mixing
surfaces, synths, audio interfaces and future devices are endpoints.

2. MISSION

Allow a creator to move from inspiration to finished audio/video and release
without being forced to move between many disconnected applications.

Core flow:
CAPTURE
-> RECORD / IMPORT
-> ANALYSE
-> REPAIR / UNMIX / STEM
-> CHOP / SAMPLE / SOUND DESIGN
-> PADS / DRUMS / MIDI / SCORE
-> COMPOSE / SEQUENCE / ARRANGE
-> RECORD PERFORMERS
-> AUTOMATE
-> MIX
-> MASTER
-> VIDEO / VISUALS
-> CREDIT / RIGHTS
-> COLLABORATE
-> EXPORT / PUBLISH / SHARE

3. PRODUCT VALUES

A. SIMPLE WHEN NEEDED, DEEP WHEN WANTED
A first-time user can follow a guided project. An expert can open routing,
automation, spectral editing, scripting, control-room and advanced MIDI.

B. ONE APP, MANY WORKSPACES
A rack, mixer, piano roll, score editor, sample editor and video timeline may all
be detached or arranged on multiple displays, but they operate on one project.

C. OFFLINE FIRST
Recording, sampling, arranging, MIDI, mixing, local video editing and export must
not require a cloud connection.

D. OPEN ENOUGH TO SURVIVE
Use standard formats and documented protocols where practical. Support import,
export, extensions and device profiles. Do not build a closed island.

E. DEVICE-AGNOSTIC MUSIC
Project musical intent must not depend on one physical controller. Logical pads,
knobs, faders, transports and instruments sit between project and hardware.

F. NON-DESTRUCTIVE
Original media remains preserved. Chops, tuning, effects, AI edits and alternate
arrangements are operations/versions wherever possible.

G. CREDIT PEOPLE WHILE THEY WORK
When a performer, producer, editor or visual artist contributes, that connection
should be recorded at project/track/clip level and be available later for credits
and split discussions.

H. FAIR CREATOR ECONOMY
Quick sharing, tips, sales and subscriptions can be supported internally.
Creators can share free work without being forced into monetisation.

4. MAJOR USER PERSONAS
BEGINNER CREATOR — guided templates, automatic setup, tooltips, complete examples.
BEATMAKER / SAMPLER — stems, chop, pad banks, swing, groove, hardware sync.
SONGWRITER / COMPOSER — chord track, piano roll, score, lyrics, arrangement.
ENGINEER / PRODUCER — low latency, routing, comping, plugins, mix/master.
DJ / LIVE PERFORMER — decks, scenes, setlists, clock, live FX.
FILM / VIDEO CREATOR — picture, captions, VFX, colour, ADR/Foley.
SESSION MUSICIAN — assignments, credits, fee/split status.
VISUAL ARTIST / EDITOR — visual ownership/credit, versions, approvals.
TEAM / LABEL / STUDIO — permissions, rights entities, shared assets, release signoff.
COMMUNITY CREATOR — profile, DMs, follows, comments, remix/fork, marketplace.

5. EXPERIENCE LEVELS
LEARN — guided builder.
CREATE — everyday production.
PRO — advanced routing/editing/monitoring.
LAB — modular/scripting/DSP.
LIVE — touch-first performance.

6. SUCCESS CRITERIA
- start on phone, continue tablet, finish desktop;
- work offline then sync;
- map unknown MIDI hardware without coding;
- change controllers without breaking project content;
- collaborate without emailing ZIPs;
- inspect who changed what;
- credit all contributors;
- create audio and visual deliverables in one project;
- quick-export without publishing paperwork;
- optional structured release workflow.

#################### 02_CURRENT_REPOSITORY_DEEP_AUDIT.txt ####################

POIETEK / STUDIO DAW STATION (SDS)
MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 1.0

Compiled from:
1) The complete product-development conversation supplied by the creator.
2) Deep review of GitHub repository: jayprophit/Studio-Daw-Station-SDS-
3) Current cross-platform/offline-first architecture recommendations.

WORKING PRODUCT NAME
Poietek is the working ecosystem name discussed in the design conversation.
Studio DAW Station (SDS) is the current repository/product label.
Final public branding should be chosen only after trademark/domain checks.

CORE RULE
One app. One project model. One creative graph. Many workspaces.
Offline first. Cloud optional. Hardware agnostic. Collaboration capable.
Professional depth without forcing beginners to see every advanced control.

LEGAL / CLEAN-ROOM RULE
Competitor products, patents, papers, hardware and interfaces may be studied for
functional principles, workflows and interoperability requirements. Do not copy
proprietary source code, protected assets, branded UI expression, undocumented
confidential protocols, or patented claims without appropriate legal review or
licensing. Compatibility layers should use documented/open interfaces wherever
possible.

===============================================================================

REPOSITORY
jayprophit/Studio-Daw-Station-SDS-
Default branch: main
Audited scaffold commit: 6451cb187158a591501fa23bcfbeba19ecd12428

EXECUTIVE ASSESSMENT
The repository is a strong concept/UI prototype produced from Google AI Studio
vibe coding. It demonstrates the intended product shape well: modular rack,
multiple hardware workspaces, visual mapping, step sequencing, basic Web Audio,
Web MIDI input, a pitch-editor mock-up, cascading DAW menus, templates, floating
workspaces and beginner walkthroughs.

It is NOT yet a production DAW or collaboration platform. Most advanced labels
represent UI simulations. Preserve the useful interaction design while replacing
demo state with real domain/engine services.

CURRENT FILE/FOLDER INVENTORY

ROOT / CONFIGURATION

- .gitignore
- assets/.aistudio/.gitignore
- index.html
- metadata.json
- package.json
- tsconfig.json
- vite.config.ts
- README.md was removed by the scaffold commit.

- src/index.css

NAVIGATION / DAW UI

HARDWARE WORKSPACES

- src/components/workspaces/EDrumWorkspace.tsx
- src/components/workspaces/KeyboardWorkspace.tsx
- src/components/workspaces/MPCWorkspace.tsx
- src/components/workspaces/MixerWorkspace.tsx
- src/components/workspaces/PatchBayWorkspace.tsx
- src/components/workspaces/SP404Workspace.tsx

A. ROOT / BUILD
package.json:
- React/Vite/TypeScript foundation is useful.
- package name still "react-example".
- no test runner, PWA, persistence, CRDT, native wrapper, or plugin host deps.
- @google/genai is installed but application code does not actually use it.
- Express/dotenv exist without a complete backend architecture.

tsconfig:
- strict mode not enabled.
- lint is only `tsc --noEmit`.
- likely existing type mismatches should be fixed before feature growth.

vite:
- plain React/Vite.
- no service worker/PWA.
- no native wrapper target.
- contains AI-Studio-specific HMR behavior and a text-encoding artifact.

README:
- removed in current scaffold commit; restore it.
Also add LICENSE, CONTRIBUTING, SECURITY and architecture docs.

B. App.tsx
GOOD:
- one-app/many-modules concept.
- 128 A-H pad model.
- floating workspace idea.
- rack undo/redo.
- templates/walkthrough.
GAPS:
- too much canonical state in React component.
- reload loses project state.
- undo/redo only tracks rack arrangement.
- no Project, Asset, Sync, Collaboration, Video, Rights or Community model.
- transport/metronome scheduling uses JavaScript timers.
- navigation can conceptually add rack modules as a side effect.
- current MasterState construction appears inconsistent with required fields.
- MIDI simulation callback typing appears inconsistent through RackStackManager.

C. types/index.ts
Useful start for hardware abstraction, but missing Project, Asset, Clip, Timeline,
Revision, Snapshot, Team, Rights, Contribution, Video, Plugin, Sync and Conflict.
Critical serialization issue: SamplePad stores a live AudioBuffer. Replace it
with assetId/hash and use a runtime AudioAssetCache.

D. audio/engine.ts
REAL:
- AudioContext, pad playback, simple synth/drums, gain/pan/EQ, mic capture.
PROTOTYPE:
- pitch uses playbackRate, so time changes too.
- no real stretch/warp.
- no AudioWorklet/custom realtime backend.
- no sample-accurate scheduler.
- no professional multichannel interface routing.
- no take lanes/comping.
- sends/buses/plugins/automation incomplete.
- no offline render graph.
- “roundtrip latency” is timer-derived, not measured.
- missing sample can fall back to kick and hide an asset failure.

E. midi/manager.ts
REAL:
- Web MIDI input, note/CC/pitch/aftertouch logging, basic learn.
PROTOTYPE:
- input ports only; no physical MIDI outputs.
- activeMappings not used as a real mapping execution graph.
- keyword device matching.
- simulator devices always added.
- only some declared MIDI processors work.
- no clock/MTC/Link.
- no persisted profile/mapping registry.

F. deviceProfiles.ts
Correct direction but “OFFICIAL” is too strong without verification.
Profiles combine families/models with potentially different mappings.
Missing vendor/product IDs, firmware range, output bindings, transfer/storage
capabilities and verification provenance.
Rename to BUILTIN_DEVICE_PROFILES and add profileVersion + verificationLevel.

G. DAW MENU / NAVIGATION
One of the best parts of the prototype:
- cascading menus;
- burger compact navigation;
- detached workspaces;
- rack front/rear concept;
- templates and guided help.
Before public release, remove competitor/trademark names from component/public
feature labels. Keep principles, use original names.

H. Waveform Sequencer
- static demo tracks/regions.
- waveform canvas is procedural, not audio-derived.
- playhead is UI timer.
- Add Track makes a fake recording region.
- M/S/REC are not a real timeline engine.
- scissors is largely visual.
Replace with TimelineEngine + ClipRepository + WaveformPeakCache + Scheduler.

I. Pitch Editor
Code uses simulated pitch blobs. Sliders do not process audio.
Implement monophonic pitch analysis first; polyphonic later.

J. ChopLab
- synthetic waveform.
- equal slices only.
- reverse does not affect audio.
- status text can disagree with >16 mapping.
- no real transient/zero-crossing/linked-stem behavior.
Implement real sample source selection and up to capability-driven pad counts.

K. AI Groove
- setTimeout and keyword BPM rules, not AI.
- Apply mainly changes BPM.
Introduce AICommandService returning structured, previewable project commands.

L. Hardware Mapper
Strong UI concept, but mappings are hard-coded/demo.
Learn wizard does not persist completed profile.
Advertised conflict resolver not implemented.
Keep UI; connect it to MappingService.

M. Visual Device Editor
Can export JSON, but no import/schema validation/drag-resize/learn/community
versioning.

N. MIDI Routing Matrix
UI-only processor chain. Not connected to MIDI engine.
Scale selector and several processor types are incomplete.

O. Device Health
UI claims measured latency and verified profiles, while current backend does not
justify those claims. Until implemented, label Estimated/Configured and Built-in.

P. Mixer
React state changes do not yet represent a complete mixer/render graph.
Build MixerGraph and bind UI to engine state.

Q. Hardware Workspaces
MPC/SP/keyboard/e-drum/DJ/patchbay/drum-machine views are valuable.
Refactor them to be capability-driven rather than hardcoded simulations.

R. MISSING PRODUCT AREAS
- durable project storage;
- offline PWA;
- native desktop/mobile;
- sync;
- team collaboration;
- rights/credits;
- internal community/DM;
- video/VFX;
- plugin sandbox;
- actual AI provider integration;
- CI/test/release pipeline.

AUDIT VERDICT
Product/UX prototype: strong.
Production audio/MIDI: early.
Durable project system: not yet.
Offline-first: not yet.
Cross-device sync: not yet.
Team collaboration: not yet.
Video/VFX: not yet.
Rights/community: not yet.

Do not add more “advanced feature” UI until the domain/project layer exists.

#################### 03_CANONICAL_DOMAIN_MODEL_UNIVERSAL_PROJECT_FORMAT.txt ####################

POIETEK / STUDIO DAW STATION (SDS)
MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 1.0

Compiled from:
1) The complete product-development conversation supplied by the creator.
2) Deep review of GitHub repository: jayprophit/Studio-Daw-Station-SDS-
3) Current cross-platform/offline-first architecture recommendations.

WORKING PRODUCT NAME
Poietek is the working ecosystem name discussed in the design conversation.
Studio DAW Station (SDS) is the current repository/product label.
Final public branding should be chosen only after trademark/domain checks.

CORE RULE
One app. One project model. One creative graph. Many workspaces.
Offline first. Cloud optional. Hardware agnostic. Collaboration capable.
Professional depth without forcing beginners to see every advanced control.

LEGAL / CLEAN-ROOM RULE
Competitor products, patents, papers, hardware and interfaces may be studied for
functional principles, workflows and interoperability requirements. Do not copy
proprietary source code, protected assets, branded UI expression, undocumented
confidential protocols, or patented claims without appropriate legal review or
licensing. Compatibility layers should use documented/open interfaces wherever
possible.

===============================================================================

FOUNDATION
Canonical project data must be independent of React and hardware.

Project {
 id, schemaVersion, title, ownerId, teamId,
 createdAt, updatedAt,
 tempoMap, timeSignatureMap, keyMap,
 tracks, buses, scenes, rackGraph,
 deviceAssignments, mappings, assets,
 comments, contributors, rights, releases,
 uiWorkspaceReferences
}

Do NOT serialize AudioBuffer, MediaStream, DOM objects, MIDIPort or plugin process
handles.

ASSET
Asset {
 id, contentHash, mediaType, originalName, mimeType, byteLength,
 duration, channels, sampleRate, bitDepth, width, height, frameRate,
 bpm, key, tags, provenance, license, versions
}

TRACK
Track {
 id, type, name, color, order,
 clips, routing, mixer, plugins, automation, contributorAssignments
}

CLIP
BaseClip:
id, trackId, timelineStart, timelineDuration, sourceOffset, sourceDuration,
loop, muted, gain, fades, operations, assetVersionId.

Audio adds warp/pitch/stretch.
MIDI adds notes/controllers/per-note expression/groove.
Video adds transform/speed/color/effects.

MIDI NOTE
id, startTick, durationTicks, note, velocity, releaseVelocity,
probability, repeat, microTiming, pitch/pressure/timbre/articulation.

UNIVERSAL HARDWARE
Project uses LogicalControl, not raw “MPC Pad A01”.
LogicalControl -> PhysicalBinding -> DeviceProfile.

DEVICE PROFILE
id, schemaVersion, manufacturer, model, hardwareRevision, firmwareRange,
vendor/product IDs, categories, connection methods, capabilities,
inputBindings, outputBindings, transport, clock, storageTransfer, audioIO,
provenance, verificationLevel, profileVersion.

PRODUCTION GRAPH
Operation {
 id, commandType, actorId, timestamp,
 inputIds, outputIds, parameters, parentRevision, reversible
}
This traces original -> stem -> chop -> pitch -> pad -> pattern -> arrangement ->
mix -> master.

CONTRIBUTORS
Contributor assignments exist at project, track, clip and asset level.
ContributionEvent records actor/object/action/time/revision.
Activity can inform split discussion but must not automatically determine legal
ownership.

RIGHTS
Store work/master parties, performer/writer/producer credits, labels/publishers,
society/member identifiers, sample licences, visual rights, agreement references,
release signoffs and optional blockchain proof hashes.

QUICK EXPORT vs PUBLISH
Quick Export: no rights IDs required.
Publish: optional structured metadata validation and approval.

SCHEMA VERSIONING
Every project has a schemaVersion and tested migrations.

PROJECT ARCHIVE
project.json
manifest.json
assets/
previews/
waveforms/
proxies/
licenses/
documents/
optional-render-cache/

COMMAND MODEL
All meaningful edits become commands:
AddTrack, MoveClip, SplitClip, AssignPad, SetMixerParameter, CreateMapping,
ApplyWarp, AddComment, AssignContributor, AcceptSplit, etc.

Commands power undo/redo, activity history, collaboration, automation and AI.

VERSION 1.3 CROSS-REFERENCE
PluginInstance, native-module state, modulation bindings and freeze-render references should use the serialization/asset rules in this document together with Document 18.

VERSION 1.4 CROSS-REFERENCE
Document 19 defines AssetReplica and provider-independent asset location. Canonical Asset IDs must never depend on Google Drive, TeraBox, GitHub, OneDrive or any other provider path.

#################### 04_CROSS_PLATFORM_APPLICATION_ARCHITECTURE.txt ####################

POIETEK / STUDIO DAW STATION (SDS)
MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 1.0

Compiled from:
1) The complete product-development conversation supplied by the creator.
2) Deep review of GitHub repository: jayprophit/Studio-Daw-Station-SDS-
3) Current cross-platform/offline-first architecture recommendations.

WORKING PRODUCT NAME
Poietek is the working ecosystem name discussed in the design conversation.
Studio DAW Station (SDS) is the current repository/product label.
Final public branding should be chosen only after trademark/domain checks.

CORE RULE
One app. One project model. One creative graph. Many workspaces.
Offline first. Cloud optional. Hardware agnostic. Collaboration capable.
Professional depth without forcing beginners to see every advanced control.

LEGAL / CLEAN-ROOM RULE
Competitor products, patents, papers, hardware and interfaces may be studied for
functional principles, workflows and interoperability requirements. Do not copy
proprietary source code, protected assets, branded UI expression, undocumented
confidential protocols, or patented claims without appropriate legal review or
licensing. Compatibility layers should use documented/open interfaces wherever
possible.

===============================================================================

TARGETS
Web/PWA, Windows, macOS, Linux, iPadOS/iOS, Android tablet/phone.

RECOMMENDED STRATEGY
Keep React/TypeScript UI.
Web: Vite PWA.
Native: Tauri 2 shell using the same front end, with Rust/native plugins for
filesystem, SQLite, hardware, low-latency audio and platform integrations.

Do not claim identical capability across browser/native/mobile. Preserve one
project format and expose platform capability differences.

MONOREPO
/apps/studio-web
/apps/studio-native
/packages/domain
/packages/commands
/packages/project
/packages/assets
/packages/timeline
/packages/midi
/packages/device-profiles
/packages/mapping
/packages/sync
/packages/collaboration
/packages/rights
/packages/community-client
/packages/ui
/packages/design-system
/packages/ai-contracts
/native/audio-host
/native/midi-host
/native/plugin-host
/server/api
/server/sync-relay
/server/media
/server/community
/server/jobs
/docs
/tests

PLATFORM ADAPTERS
StorageAdapter
AudioBackend
MidiBackend
FileBackend
PluginBackend
ShareBackend
CapabilityRegistry

WEB
Service Worker for app shell/offline.
IndexedDB for structured local state.
large-media cache suited to browser capabilities.
Web Audio + AudioWorklet.
Web MIDI where supported.
Web Workers/WASM for analysis/non-realtime processing.

NATIVE
same UI plus:
SQLite, filesystem, native audio/MIDI, background transfer, secure storage,
plugin host, better large-media handling.

PARITY RULE
Project format identical. Capability may differ.
If a desktop plugin is unavailable on web/mobile, preserve plugin state and play
a freeze/bounce proxy instead of deleting it.

MOBILE
Touch-optimized creation/capture, not merely a viewer.
Heavy desktop-only plugins can remain frozen while project stays editable.

RESPONSIVE
Phone: one focused workspace + drawers.
Tablet: split views.
Desktop: multi-panel/multi-monitor.
Web: capability-aware.

STARTUP
1 local app shell
2 local DB
3 restore project
4 initialize audio/MIDI
5 show UI
6 sync only if network/auth available

Network login must not block local creation.

#################### 05_OFFLINE_SYNC_COLLABORATION_VERSIONING.txt ####################

POIETEK / STUDIO DAW STATION (SDS)
MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 1.0

Compiled from:
1) The complete product-development conversation supplied by the creator.
2) Deep review of GitHub repository: jayprophit/Studio-Daw-Station-SDS-
3) Current cross-platform/offline-first architecture recommendations.

WORKING PRODUCT NAME
Poietek is the working ecosystem name discussed in the design conversation.
Studio DAW Station (SDS) is the current repository/product label.
Final public branding should be chosen only after trademark/domain checks.

CORE RULE
One app. One project model. One creative graph. Many workspaces.
Offline first. Cloud optional. Hardware agnostic. Collaboration capable.
Professional depth without forcing beginners to see every advanced control.

LEGAL / CLEAN-ROOM RULE
Competitor products, patents, papers, hardware and interfaces may be studied for
functional principles, workflows and interoperability requirements. Do not copy
proprietary source code, protected assets, branded UI expression, undocumented
confidential protocols, or patented claims without appropriate legal review or
licensing. Compatibility layers should use documented/open interfaces wherever
possible.

===============================================================================

LOCAL-FIRST
Every edit commits durably to the local device before cloud acknowledgement.

STATE SPLIT
Structured collaborative state:
tracks, clip references, MIDI, mappings, mixer values, automation, comments,
contributors, rights drafts.

Large binaries:
WAV, video, stems, images, freeze renders.
Store separately by stable ID/content hash. Do not put binaries in CRDT docs.

CONTENT ADDRESSING
Use SHA-256 or equivalent content hash to deduplicate and verify media.

LOCAL STORAGE
Web: IndexedDB + service worker app shell + persistent media cache.
Native: SQLite + content-addressed filesystem + transaction journal.

SYNC PIPELINE
local command
-> local transaction
-> revision
-> sync queue
-> upload missing media chunks
-> send structured update
-> ack
-> synced.

OFFLINE EDIT
Users can create/edit cached content, record new media, MIDI, mix, arrange,
comment, map hardware and assign credits with no network.

CRDT
Yjs is a strong candidate for structured collaborative state.
Partition documents; do not put the whole application in one giant shared doc.

COLLABORATION
Presence: online members, cursor/selection/playhead/editing indicator.
Shared edits: arrangement, MIDI, comments, markers, selected mixer metadata.
Audio itself syncs as media assets.

COLLAB PHASES
A async collaboration.
B rapid proxy/full asset sync.
C later live jam with low-latency streaming and clock drift handling.

VERSIONING
undo/redo + named snapshots + collaboration activity.
Support alternate branches/forks and selective merge.

CONFLICTS
CRDT structured data merges.
Binary media conflicts preserve variants.
Rights/split disagreements require human resolution.

TEAM ROLES
Owner, Admin, Producer, Editor, Performer, Mixer, Mastering, Video Editor,
Visual Artist, Reviewer, Viewer, Guest.

CONTRIBUTION FOOTPRINT
Every command has actorId.
Recordings can be assigned performer/instrument/engineer.
Activity dashboard shows who contributed what and when.

PROJECT GOVERNANCE
Optional votes for release/artwork/creative decisions.
Legal agreements require explicit assent, not casual majority vote.

BACKUP
Autosave is not backup.
Provide local snapshots + optional cloud backup + exportable project archive.

CORE ACCEPTANCE
Device A edits offline. Reconnects. Device B receives merged structured state and
missing assets. No IDs collide, no data is lost, both can reopen offline.

#################### 06_AUDIO_MIDI_HARDWARE_PLUGIN_ARCHITECTURE.txt ####################

POIETEK / STUDIO DAW STATION (SDS)
MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 1.0

Compiled from:
1) The complete product-development conversation supplied by the creator.
2) Deep review of GitHub repository: jayprophit/Studio-Daw-Station-SDS-
3) Current cross-platform/offline-first architecture recommendations.

WORKING PRODUCT NAME
Poietek is the working ecosystem name discussed in the design conversation.
Studio DAW Station (SDS) is the current repository/product label.
Final public branding should be chosen only after trademark/domain checks.

CORE RULE
One app. One project model. One creative graph. Many workspaces.
Offline first. Cloud optional. Hardware agnostic. Collaboration capable.
Professional depth without forcing beginners to see every advanced control.

LEGAL / CLEAN-ROOM RULE
Competitor products, patents, papers, hardware and interfaces may be studied for
functional principles, workflows and interoperability requirements. Do not copy
proprietary source code, protected assets, branded UI expression, undocumented
confidential protocols, or patented claims without appropriate legal review or
licensing. Compatibility layers should use documented/open interfaces wherever
possible.

===============================================================================

AUDIO ENGINE
Introduce IAudioEngine. The current WebAudio engine becomes one backend.

Responsibilities:
device configuration, transport clock, sample scheduling, recording, routing,
effects, automation, metering, offline render and analysis feeds.

CLOCK
Replace setInterval musical timing with audio-clock scheduling/lookahead.
Transport tracks sample position, bar/beat/tick, tempo map, signature map, loop
and punch ranges.

PITCH/TIME
Separate resampling, pitch shifting, time stretching and warp mapping.
Modes: beats, monophonic, polyphonic, voice, complex, sound design.

RECORDING
Input/channel selection, mono/stereo/multichannel, monitoring, count-in, punch,
loop/takes, comping, latency compensation and performer assignment.

MIXER GRAPH
Pre gain -> inserts -> EQ/dynamics -> fader/pan -> sends -> output.
Tracks, pads, stems, buses, returns, groups, master and cue buses.

CONTROL ROOM
Main monitors, headphones, performer cues, speaker A/B, mono, dim, talkback,
reference level and calibration.

MIDI
IMidiService:
enumerate inputs/outputs, open/close, timestamped send, subscribe, clock, MTC,
SysEx permission, learn, mapping, processors and device state.

Internal values should be capable of future higher resolution rather than being
hardwired to MIDI 1 7-bit assumptions.

MIDI PROCESSORS
transpose, scale, chord, arpeggiator, velocity, humanize, quantize, random,
probability, note/CC/channel maps, delay, LFO, envelope, Euclidean and conditions.

LOGICAL MAPPING
Physical event -> profile -> logical control -> transform -> app command/parameter.

DEVICE LEARN
Unknown controller wizard creates a reusable profile through real MIDI input.

HOT SWAP
Disconnect one controller without breaking project content. Map a replacement
device to the same logical roles.

PROFILE SAFETY
Verification levels:
unverified, user-tested, community-tested, manufacturer-documented, official.
Never call a mapping official without evidence.

HARDWARE WORKSPACES
MPC, SP sampler, keyboard, e-drum, DJ, mixer, generic sampler/MIDI and DIY views
all sit over the same DeviceService.

LATENCY
Remove timer-based fake measurement.
Real loopback calibration sends a known signal, records it and correlates the
return. If impossible, clearly label API-reported/estimated latency.

PLUGINS
Desktop: VST3/CLAP, AU on macOS when implemented, optional ARA-class integration.
Web: WebAudio/WASM extension format, not fake VST hosting.
Mobile: supported native/in-app modules; preserve unavailable plugin state and
use freeze/bounce proxies.

PLUGIN SANDBOX
scan, quarantine, crash isolation where practical, state restore, missing-plugin
placeholder and compatibility database.

RACK
Turn current rack into a real typed graph:
DeviceNode, AudioPort, MidiPort, ControlPort, ModulationPort.
Front panel = performance. Rear panel = routing.
Combinator = devices + macros + mappings.

PERFORMANCE
No blocking I/O or allocation on realtime audio callback.
Analysis, waveform generation, stems, AI and transcode run off the audio thread.

HARDWARE EXPORT
Each device family gets a capability-specific exporter. Never claim internal bank
or project transfer unless the device exposes/documented it.

VERSION 1.2 CROSS-REFERENCE
Document 17 extends hardware architecture with DeckEngine, ScratchTransport, DVS calibration, high-resolution jog/HID capabilities, DJ mixer routing and controller feedback.

VERSION 1.3 CROSS-REFERENCE
Document 18 is now the detailed plugin-host and native sound-engine specification, including scan isolation, PDC, sidechains, modulation, instruments, effects and mastering. Use it as the implementation source for the plugin subsection here.

#################### 07_DAW_SAMPLING_COMPOSITION_MIXING_MASTERING.txt ####################

POIETEK / STUDIO DAW STATION (SDS)
MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 1.0

Compiled from:
1) The complete product-development conversation supplied by the creator.
2) Deep review of GitHub repository: jayprophit/Studio-Daw-Station-SDS-
3) Current cross-platform/offline-first architecture recommendations.

WORKING PRODUCT NAME
Poietek is the working ecosystem name discussed in the design conversation.
Studio DAW Station (SDS) is the current repository/product label.
Final public branding should be chosen only after trademark/domain checks.

CORE RULE
One app. One project model. One creative graph. Many workspaces.
Offline first. Cloud optional. Hardware agnostic. Collaboration capable.
Professional depth without forcing beginners to see every advanced control.

LEGAL / CLEAN-ROOM RULE
Competitor products, patents, papers, hardware and interfaces may be studied for
functional principles, workflows and interoperability requirements. Do not copy
proprietary source code, protected assets, branded UI expression, undocumented
confidential protocols, or patented claims without appropriate legal review or
licensing. Compatibility layers should use documented/open interfaces wherever
possible.

===============================================================================

STANDARD PROFESSIONAL CORE
Projects, templates, audio/MIDI/instrument tracks, folders, groups, buses, VCAs,
sends, sidechains, recording, comping, editing, automation, tempo maps, mixer,
plugins, freeze/bounce, stems, undo, autosave, shortcuts, drag/drop and rendering.

SAMPLER / CHOP
Waveform/spectrogram/turntable views.
Manual, lazy, transient, equal, beat/bar, silence, AI and zero-crossing chops.
Per-slice start/end, loop, pitch, tune, gain, pan, reverse, fade, filter,
envelope, stretch and choke.
Hardware banks are export profiles, not app limits.

STEMS
Vocals/drums/bass/music as compatibility baseline, optional finer models.
Linked chop markers across stems, stem mixer and stem-to-pad mapping.

PAD BUILDER
Logical pad grid, classification, velocity layers, round robin, choke, gate/loop,
root notes, filters, envelopes, colors and hardware adaptation.

DRUM LAB
Step grid, velocity, probability, ratchets, flam, ghost notes, microtiming,
note repeat, swing, groove, polymeter, Euclidean/conditional tools.

GROOVE
Extract timing/velocity from audio/MIDI, apply by percentage and save templates.

PIANO ROLL
Ghost notes, chord stamps, scale highlighting, MPE/per-note expression,
microtiming, probability, articulations, strum/flam/arp/humanize.

SCORE
Staff/percussion notation, lyrics, chord symbols, articulations, tuplets, tab and
MusicXML-style interchange where supported. Keep score and MIDI synchronized.

HARMONY
Global chord track, key/scale maps, chord pads, scale assistant, detection,
substitution, transpose and alternative tunings.

SESSION PLAYERS
Editable drummer/bassist/keys/guitar/percussion/strings/brass performances
following chord track and user-defined complexity/groove.

CLIP/SCENE
Launch clips/patterns/stems and record performance into Arrangement.

ARRANGEMENT
Real audio/MIDI/video ruler, split/trim/slip/fade/stretch/loop/consolidate,
sections, markers and alternate scratch area.

RETROSPECTIVE CAPTURE
MIDI high priority. Optional rolling audio with privacy/memory controls.

VOCAL/NOTE AUDIO
Monophonic pitch/timing first: pitch curve, drift, vibrato, formant, timing,
sibilance and gain. Polyphonic editing later research target.

ALIGNMENT
Guide vs doubles: timing and optional pitch, loose through tight.

SPECTRAL/REPAIR
Spectrogram, selection, harmonic tools, heal/attenuate, de-click, de-hum,
de-noise, de-clip, de-reverb, de-bleed and voice isolation.

SOUND DESIGN
Sampler, synths, granular/wavetable later, modulation, resampling and macros.

AUTOMATION
Audio, MIDI, plugins, mixer, tempo, video and show-control parameters.

MIX
Buses, groups, parallel containers, snapshots, track versions, reference tracks,
gain-matched A/B, metering and phase/stereo/loudness tools.

MASTER
EQ/dynamic EQ, compression/multiband, saturation, image, clipper, limiter,
resonance control, LUFS/true peak, reference assistance.

EXPORT
Audio master, stems, loops, samples, MIDI, kit/program packages and project
archive. Settings for rate, depth, dither, range/tails and realtime/offline.
Device-specific exports only where verified.

VERSION 1.1 CROSS-REFERENCE
For the detailed pitch/time/vocal subsystem, including Live Tune, Note Edit, Guide/Dub alignment, polyphonic research and audio-to-MIDI, see Document 16.

VERSION 1.2 CROSS-REFERENCE
Document 17 connects the DAW's clips, stems, sampler and arrangement to live DJ decks/scenes so performances can be recorded back into Arrangement.

VERSION 1.3 CROSS-REFERENCE
Document 18 defines the built-in instrument/effect/mastering suite and external plug-in host required for the production workflows in this document.

POIETEK / STUDIO DAW STATION (SDS)

Compiled from:

Professional depth without forcing beginners to see every advanced control.

LEGAL / CLEAN-ROOM RULE
Competitor products, patents, papers, hardware and interfaces may be studied for
functional principles, workflows and interoperability requirements. Do not copy

confidential protocols, or patented claims without appropriate legal review or

===============================================================================

CURRENT REPO GAP
Video/VFX is not yet a real subsystem. Add it as a first-class project domain.

UNIFIED TIMELINE
Audio, MIDI, video, still images, text/captions, motion graphics, automation,
markers, comments, lighting/show-control events.

VIDEO EDITING
Import, trim, split, slip, roll, ripple, duplicate, speed, reverse, freeze,
crop, transform, opacity, blend, keyframes, transitions, proxy and render cache.

AUDIO POST
linked/unlinked A/V, dialogue/music/FX buses, timecode, frame grid, ADR, Foley,
ambience and sync markers.

COLOUR
Exposure, contrast, white balance, curves, hue/saturation, LUT support where
legal/technical, waveform/vectorscope/histogram.

VFX
Layer compositing first; node graph later.
Blur, glow, key, mask, tracking, stabilization, transform, distortion, lighting,
particles, fog/smoke/rain/snow, grain, lens/glitch/stylization.

MOTION GRAPHICS
Titles, lyrics, captions, lower thirds, logos, shapes, keyframes and templates.

AUDIO-REACTIVE
Spectrum, waveform, beat/onset triggers, frequency bands, MIDI-reactive graphics.
Examples: kick -> flash, bass -> particle scale, MIDI note -> scene trigger.

LIGHTING / SHOW CONTROL
General control timeline prepared for MIDI/OSC and future lighting adapters.

VISUAL ASSETS
Video, image, font references/licenses, LUT, template, 3D model, shader, overlay.

3D LATER
Objects, camera, lights, materials, particles and GPU shaders after core editor.

MOBILE VIDEO
Touch-first trim, captions, transitions, beat sync, filters and overlays.
Heavy processing can use desktop/native background or optional cloud services.

DELIVERY
Master archive, landscape, vertical, square, transparent graphics where codec
allows, audio-only and stems. Social presets must be data-driven.

THIRD-PARTY VFX
Desktop extension architecture can later support standard/native video effects.
Internal SDK: frame processor, generator, transition, analyzer and metadata tool.

RIGHTS
Visual contributors and licences use the same contribution/rights system as
audio contributors.

ACCEPTANCE
Import music + video, edit to beats, add captions/effects, credit all creators
and export a finished music video inside one project.

VERSION 1.2 CROSS-REFERENCE
Document 17 defines the DJ/VJ layer: video decks, video crossfader, live visualizers, beat-reactive visuals and performance-scene integration.

#################### 09_AI_AUTOMATION_CREATIVE_INTELLIGENCE.txt ####################

POIETEK / STUDIO DAW STATION (SDS)

Compiled from:

3) Current cross-platform/offline-first architecture recommendations.

WORKING PRODUCT NAME

Studio DAW Station (SDS) is the current repository/product label.
Final public branding should be chosen only after trademark/domain checks.

CORE RULE
One app. One project model. One creative graph. Many workspaces.
Offline first. Cloud optional. Hardware agnostic. Collaboration capable.
Professional depth without forcing beginners to see every advanced control.

LEGAL / CLEAN-ROOM RULE
Competitor products, patents, papers, hardware and interfaces may be studied for
functional principles, workflows and interoperability requirements. Do not copy
proprietary source code, protected assets, branded UI expression, undocumented
confidential protocols, or patented claims without appropriate legal review or
licensing. Compatibility layers should use documented/open interfaces wherever
possible.

===============================================================================

AI MUST RETURN EDITABLE PROJECT OPERATIONS, NOT HIDDEN MAGIC.

AI COMMAND OUTPUT
explanation
proposedCommands
confidence
warnings
previewAssets
rightsFlags

User chooses Preview / Accept / Modify / Reject.

PRODUCTION AI
chop suggestions, patterns, MIDI variations, chords, arrangement, session players.

AUDIO ANALYSIS
BPM/key/chords, classification, transient/loop, loudness, masking, clipping,
phase, stereo and noise/problem detection.

MIX/MASTER
Recommendations with explanations and editable parameter changes.

VIDEO
cut/pacing suggestions, beat markers, captions, color/VFX ideas, storyboard help.

LEARNING
contextual teaching and “show me” flows.

PROJECT MANAGER
missing media, unsynced assets, missing credits, pending approvals, backup health.

RIGHTS ASSISTANT
Classify evidence as original/licensed/royalty-free/public-domain candidate/
unknown. Warn, never automatically determine legality.

INSPIRED-BY MODE
Generate original material from descriptive characteristics. AI is not a legal
bypass for recreating protected recordings.

CREATIVE MEMORY
Opt-in user preferences for devices, layouts, chains and export defaults.

NATURAL LANGUAGE COMMAND PALETTE
“Open mixer.”
“Chop this into transients.”
“Show Alex's changes.”
“Export a private preview.”
“Prepare release metadata but do not publish.”

AUTOMATION BUILDER
Import -> analyze -> BPM -> transients -> pad map -> backup -> notify.

MODEL ABSTRACTION
AIProvider interface. Do not hardcode product to Gemini.
Support cloud and future local providers. Core app works with AI disabled.

OFFLINE AI
Prioritize local algorithms for BPM/key/transient/waveform/basic classification.

PROVENANCE
AI-derived assets can record model/provider, operation, input IDs, timestamp and
human acceptance.

NON-DESTRUCTIVE GUARANTEE
AI MIDI stays MIDI; chops stay markers; mix stays parameters; arrangements stay
clips; video edits stay timeline edits; originals remain recoverable.

VERSION 1.1 CROSS-REFERENCE
Pitch/vocal AI must produce the structured operations defined in Document 16 rather than destructive hidden PCM edits.

VERSION 1.3 CROSS-REFERENCE
AI sound-design/mastering commands should target the structured native-module, macro, modulation and plugin-parameter model in Document 18, with previews and undo.

confidential protocols, or patented claims without appropriate legal review or

INTERNAL CREATOR COMMUNITY

PROFILE
bio, skills, genres, portfolio, public/private projects, follows, collaboration
availability, optional verified identity/equipment/region.

FEED
audio, video, image, project preview, template, device profile, tutorial, remix.
Interactions: like, comment, share, save, follow, report, remix/fork if allowed.

DIRECT MESSAGING
1:1, project/group chat, attachments, private previews, collaboration invites.
Include blocking, reporting, requests and spam controls.

COLLAB DISCOVERY
Search by role, genre, instrument, equipment, availability, language and portfolio.

CONTRIBUTOR FOOTPRINT
Audio and visual:
artist, vocalist, instrumentalist, producer, songwriter, composer, engineer,
mix/master, session musician, DJ, editor, director, camera, VFX, motion, graphic
design and business entities.

RECORDING WORKFLOW
Project setup can preload team.
When recording an unassigned track, prompt for performer, instrument/role,
engineer and session date. Guests can later claim profiles.

CONTRIBUTION IS NOT AUTOMATIC OWNERSHIP
AI can suggest a discussion split, but humans approve.

RIGHTS ENTITIES
People, labels, publishers, management, PRO/CMO, distributors, promoters,
visual-production entities and other contractual parties.

QUICK EXPORT
No ISRC/PRO requirement. Draft/private/master/stems according to permissions.

PUBLISH MODE
Optional validation for title, artists, writers, performers, producer, visual
credits, splits, labels/publishers, licences, identifiers, artwork and release
settings.

SAMPLE RIGHTS
Store source/licence. Before monetized/public release, flag unknown items and
offer attach/replace/seek-permission/original-alternative workflows. Do not claim
AI recreation makes an unlicensed protected work automatically safe.

BLOCKCHAIN
Optional content hash, timestamp, wallet and contract reference. Do not claim
blockchain replaces copyright/contract systems.

MONETISATION
Tips, direct sales, subscriptions/fan support, marketplace, commissions, services
and courses. Payment/KYC/tax/legal requirements are separate infrastructure.

CREATOR LICENCE SETTINGS
listen only, remix allowed, attribution, noncommercial, commercial available,
custom terms.

GOVERNANCE
Platform roadmap voting is separate from project legal approvals.

SHARING
Internal publishing is first-class. External platforms use connector-based
ShareService adapters and each platform's rules/APIs.

VISIBILITY
local only, private cloud, team, unlisted, community/followers, public.
Visibility and monetisation are separate choices.

verification.

OBSERVABILITY
crash/performance/sync/plugin errors without silently uploading private media.

convergence, asset resume, E2E, accessibility.

CI

LEGAL / CLEAN-ROOM RULE
Competitor products, patents, papers, hardware and interfaces may be studied for

PHASE 14 — MONETISATION
tips, sales, subscriptions, marketplace, payout/ledger/legal.

PHASE 15 — EXTENSIONS
SDK, scripts, community profiles, themes and marketplace review.

1 Project type/repository interface.
2 SamplePad assetId instead of AudioBuffer.
3 ProjectStore instead of App-owned canonical state.
4 Command bus + undo.
5 IndexedDB autosave.
6 real asset ingest.
7 waveform peak cache.
8 AudioBackend interface.
9 audio-clock scheduler.
10 MIDI output.
11 persistent mappings/profiles.
12 service worker/PWA.

DO NOT DO FIRST
blockchain, social feed, marketplace, polyphonic pitch DSP, 3D VFX, live remote
jam, every hardware model or every plugin format.

#################### 14_MASTER_AI_BUILDER_PROMPT.txt ####################

POIETEK / STUDIO DAW STATION (SDS)
MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 1.0

Compiled from:
1) The complete product-development conversation supplied by the creator.
2) Deep review of GitHub repository: jayprophit/Studio-Daw-Station-SDS-
3) Current cross-platform/offline-first architecture recommendations.

WORKING PRODUCT NAME
Poietek is the working ecosystem name discussed in the design conversation.
Studio DAW Station (SDS) is the current repository/product label.
Final public branding should be chosen only after trademark/domain checks.

CORE RULE
One app. One project model. One creative graph. Many workspaces.
Offline first. Cloud optional. Hardware agnostic. Collaboration capable.
Professional depth without forcing beginners to see every advanced control.

LEGAL / CLEAN-ROOM RULE
Competitor products, patents, papers, hardware and interfaces may be studied for
functional principles, workflows and interoperability requirements. Do not copy
proprietary source code, protected assets, branded UI expression, undocumented
confidential protocols, or patented claims without appropriate legal review or
licensing. Compatibility layers should use documented/open interfaces wherever
possible.

===============================================================================

COPY THE FOLLOWING INTO A CODING AGENT:

You are the principal software architect and lead implementation engineer for
Poietek / Studio DAW Station (SDS), a local-first cross-platform creative
production environment.

Treat the Poietek/SDS Master Development Library as source of truth. Do not
reduce the product to an MPC app. MPC, SP samplers, MIDI keyboards, e-drums,
DJ controllers, mixers and future devices are endpoints of a universal hardware
mapping layer.

PRIMARY GOAL
Evolve the React/Vite prototype into a production-quality offline-first
audio/video platform without discarding useful interface work.

NON-NEGOTIABLES
1. React/TypeScript stays shared UI unless a documented reason requires native.
2. UI does not own canonical project state.
3. Project state is serializable and schema-versioned.
4. Do not serialize AudioBuffer/MediaStream/MIDIPort/DOM/plugin handles.
5. Large media is separate, referenced by asset ID/content hash.
6. Edits use commands/transactions for undo/audit/collaboration.
7. Core editing works without Internet.
8. Cloud is optional sync/collaboration.
9. CRDT structured state; large media uses object/chunk storage.
10. Hardware uses logical controls/capability profiles.
11. Capability differences are explicit per platform.
12. AI changes are previewable/editable/reversible.
13. Quick Export never requires publishing IDs.
14. Legal splits/ownership require explicit human approval.
15. Do not copy proprietary code/assets/UI/private protocols.

CURRENT REALITY
The SDS repository is a concept prototype. WebAudio and WebMIDI contain useful
early code, but many timeline/waveform/pitch/chop/AI behaviors are simulations.
Persistence, sync, collaboration, video, rights and community are not complete.

DO NOT LIE THROUGH UI
If a capability is not real, label it Preview/Prototype. Do not call it measured,
verified or AI-generated unless it is.

FIRST MILESTONE
Make a real project survive reload/restart and play a real imported audio asset.

ORDER
A strict types/tests
B domain
C command bus
D local repository
E assets
F timeline/audio scheduler
G MIDI output/logical mapping
H real chop/sample
I PWA/native adapter
J sync
K collaboration
Then follow the roadmap.

SERVICE INTERFACES
ProjectRepository, AssetStore, CommandBus, UndoManager, AudioBackend,
TransportClock, MidiBackend, DeviceProfileRepository, MappingService,
SyncService, CollaborationService, RightsService, ExportService, AIProvider,
VideoBackend, PluginBackend.

PRACTICE
small commits, tests, migrations, no god components, dependency injection,
UUID/ULID durable IDs rather than Date.now+Math.random, structured errors,
capability checks, accessibility, profiling and documentation.

FOR EACH FEATURE RETURN
current state, files changed, architecture, code, tests, migration, capability
limits and remaining gaps.

DEFINITION OF DONE
Real behavior, persisted, undoable where appropriate, sync-safe when
collaborative, documented, tested, accessible, and handles offline/error states.

START
Audit Phase 0/1 and implement one vertical slice:
Create Project -> import audio -> persist locally -> reopen -> play on real
timeline -> export a test/master render.
Do not start social/blockchain/marketplace/advanced AI until this is reliable.

#################### 16_PITCH_TIME_VOCAL_ALIGNMENT_AUDIO_NOTE_RESEARCH.txt ####################

POIETEK / STUDIO DAW STATION (SDS)
MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 1.1 ADDENDUM

16 — PITCH, TIME, VOCAL ALIGNMENT & AUDIO-NOTE EDITING RESEARCH
==============================================================

PURPOSE

This document converts functional ideas from established pitch/timing products
into an original Poietek/SDS architecture. It is a clean-room product
specification: study the workflow principles and published capabilities, but do
not copy proprietary source code, patented algorithms, product artwork, UI
layouts, branding, or undocumented behavior.

The SDS repository currently contains a visual pitch-editor prototype with
simulated note blobs. This addendum defines the real subsystem that should
eventually sit behind that view.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. REFERENCE CAPABILITY FAMILIES
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

A. NOTE-LEVEL / MUSICAL AUDIO EDITING
Reference principle:
Detect musical events in audio and expose editable note-like objects rather than
forcing the user to edit raw waveforms only.

Useful published capabilities found in current Melodyne documentation include:
- monophonic and polyphonic analysis modes;
- editing pitch, timing, duration, formants, level and sibilant-related behavior;
- chord/key context;
- multitrack note editing;
- audio-to-MIDI;
- polyphonic note access for suitable instruments.

Poietek/SDS interpretation:
Create an original `AudioNoteLayer` derived from an immutable audio asset.

AudioNote {
  id
  sourceClipId
  startSeconds
  durationSeconds
  detectedMidiPitch
  pitchCurveCents[]
  confidence
  amplitudeEnvelope[]
  voicedProbability[]
  formantEstimate?
  sibilantRegions[]
  transientClass?
}

Edits are separate operations:
PitchCenterOperation
PitchDriftOperation
PitchCurveOperation
TimeMoveOperation
TimeStretchOperation
FormantOperation
GainOperation
VibratoOperation
SibilanceOperation
NoteSplitOperation
NoteMergeOperation

B. REAL-TIME AUTOMATIC PITCH CORRECTION
Reference principle:
A low-latency path is different from a deep offline/manual editor.

Published current AutoTune Pro information describes:
- real-time automatic mode;
- graph/manual editing mode;
- formant/vibrato controls;
- scale/key targeting;
- MIDI mapping/harmony functions.

Waves Tune Real-Time documentation similarly demonstrates the need for a
performance/monitoring path with extremely low or near-zero plugin latency,
formant handling, scale control and MIDI control.

Poietek/SDS interpretation:
Implement two separate processing tiers.

1) LIVE TUNE
- designed for monitoring/tracking/live performance;
- bounded lookahead;
- low-latency F0 detection;
- key/scale;
- correction speed;
- tolerance/dead-band;
- humanize;
- vibrato preservation;
- formant preserve/shift;
- MIDI note/scale target;
- creative hard-tune mode;
- automatic bypass when detection confidence is low.

2) EDIT TUNE
- rendered/previewed high-quality analysis;
- note segmentation;
- per-note curves;
- unlimited undo;
- higher-quality offline render;
- detailed timing/formant/gain control.

Do not make Live Tune and Edit Tune one algorithm with one quality setting.
Their latency/quality constraints differ.

C. GUIDE / DOUBLE / HARMONY ALIGNMENT
Reference principle:
One performance can guide timing/pitch characteristics of one or more alternate
performances.

Published Revoice Pro documentation describes Audio Performance Transfer (APT):
timing relationships are derived between a Guide and Dub; timing, pitch,
vibrato and level characteristics can be transferred with adjustable tightness.
It also supports manual time/pitch warping and workflows for multiple doubles.

Poietek/SDS interpretation:
Create `PerformanceAlignmentService`.

AlignmentJob {
  guideClipId
  targetClipIds[]
  dimensions {
    timing
    pitchCenter
    pitchModulation
    level
  }
  strength
  tolerance
  protectedRanges[]
  mode: music | dialog | rhythm
}

Output remains non-destructive:
AlignmentMap + preview render.

Use cases:
- lead vocal -> doubles;
- lead -> harmony timing only;
- dialogue -> ADR;
- guitar double alignment;
- backing-vocal stack cleanup;
- drum layer timing where material is suitable.

Critical UX:
Natural differences can be retained by tolerance controls.
Users can protect regions that must not be altered.

D. DAW-INTEGRATED NOTE EDITING
Reference principle:
Pitch editing is most useful when it feels like part of the project rather than
a separate file-transfer application.

Current Apple Logic documentation shows note-style pitch/timing editing inside
the audio editor, including note position/length, split/merge, pitch drift,
vibrato, gain, fine pitch and formant controls.

Current Steinberg VariAudio documentation describes analysis/segmentation of
monophonic vocals into note representations for pitch and timing editing, with
undo and audio-to-MIDI-related workflows.

Poietek/SDS interpretation:
The Note Editor must be a native project workspace:
- double-click audio clip -> Note Edit;
- edits belong to the source clip/version;
- no manual capture/transfer step;
- same transport and timeline;
- notes can optionally be shown over waveform/spectrogram;
- inspector changes the selected note(s);
- A/B original/processed;
- render/freeze only when requested.

E. HOST/PLUGIN DEEP INTEGRATION
Reference principle:
ARA-style integrations avoid real-time “capture into the plugin” workflows and
let compatible hosts/plugins share musical/audio context.

Celemony states the ARA SDK has been available as open-source software under
Apache 2.0, and describes direct audio access plus exchange of musical
information such as notes, tempo and chords.

Poietek/SDS interpretation:
- research ARA hosting for desktop plugin interoperability;
- keep Poietek's own internal clip-analysis API independent of ARA;
- use ARA only as an external compatibility adapter, not as the project model.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. PRODUCT MODES
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The pitch/time system should expose six user-facing modes.

MODE 1 — QUICK TUNE
One control strip for creators who simply want transparent correction.
Controls:
Key/Scale
Amount
Speed
Humanize
Formant Preserve
Natural <-> Effect

MODE 2 — LIVE TUNE
For input monitoring and live performance.
Controls:
Input
Key/Scale
MIDI target
Retune speed
Tolerance
Vibrato preservation
Formant
Latency/quality mode
Confidence meter

MODE 3 — NOTE EDIT
Detailed detected-note editing.
Tools:
Select
Pitch
Fine pitch
Pitch curve
Drift
Timing
Duration
Split
Join
Gain
Vibrato
Formant
Sibilance
Reset
Audition

MODE 4 — ALIGN
Guide/Dub workflow.
Timing, pitch and level transfer with independent strengths and protected areas.

MODE 5 — POLY NOTE LAB
Research/advanced mode for suitable polyphonic sources.
This must be treated as a separate R&D tier, because decomposing a chordal
recording into reliable editable note objects is materially harder than
monophonic pitch correction.

MODE 6 — CREATIVE PITCH / FORMANT
Not correction-centric.
Robot/hard tune
scale quantization
harmonization
formant character
octave layers
microtonal/alternative tuning
pitch-follow modulation
MIDI-controlled pitch targets

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3. ENGINE ARCHITECTURE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

PitchTimeEngine
  AnalysisService
  NoteSegmentationService
  F0Estimator
  MultiPitchEstimator?          // later R&D
  TimeStretchBackend
  PitchShiftBackend
  FormantBackend
  AlignmentService
  AudioToMidiService
  PitchRenderService
  LiveTuneProcessor
  AnalysisCache
  PreviewCache

Separation of concerns is mandatory:
Pitch detection does not own rendering.
Note segmentation does not own the UI.
Time stretching does not own project state.
The project stores edit operations, not processor-private memory.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4. MONOPHONIC IMPLEMENTATION PATH — BUILD THIS FIRST
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Phase A
- resample/normalize analysis input;
- detect voiced/unvoiced frames;
- estimate F0;
- smooth with confidence-aware filtering;
- detect note boundaries from pitch + energy + onset cues;
- compute median/target pitch per note;
- draw pitch curve.

Phase B
- semitone/fine-pitch movement;
- pitch correction amount;
- scale quantization;
- note split/join;
- time movement/duration;
- A/B preview.

Phase C
- formant preservation;
- vibrato amount;
- drift;
- sibilance-aware processing;
- audio-to-MIDI;
- batch macros.

Phase D
- multitrack editing;
- reference/guide alignment;
- local/offline AI suggestions.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
5. TIME STRETCH / PITCH SHIFT IMPLEMENTATION OPTIONS
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The present SDS engine uses AudioBufferSourceNode.playbackRate, which changes
pitch and duration together. That remains useful only as a deliberate resampling
effect.

The production engine needs independent pitch and time.

Candidate engineering paths:
A. Clean-room DSP implementation using published time-domain/phase-vocoder
   literature.
B. Licensed third-party DSP library.
C. Platform/native DSP where legally and technically suitable.
D. Future ML/neural resynthesis for selected offline modes.

A practical current library to evaluate is Rubber Band Library, which supports
independent time stretching/pitch shifting, real-time and offline modes, and
formant-related options. IMPORTANT: its official site states it is GPL for open
source use and requires a commercial licence for proprietary distribution.
Licensing must be decided before integration.

aubio is useful as a research/reference implementation for pitch/onset/tempo
analysis, but its official repository is GPL-3.0. Do not copy/integrate it into a
non-GPL product without satisfying its licence.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
6. REAL-TIME TUNING DESIGN
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Signal path:
Input
-> optional noise/gate preparation
-> F0 detector
-> target note resolver
-> correction trajectory generator
-> pitch shifter
-> formant handling
-> wet/dry
-> output

Target Resolver priorities:
1. explicit MIDI target
2. chord-track allowed tones
3. active key/scale
4. chromatic fallback

Naturalness controls:
- correction speed;
- note-transition speed;
- tolerance around target;
- preserve attacks;
- preserve vibrato;
- preserve unvoiced/sibilant segments;
- confidence-based bypass;
- formant preservation.

The engine must display processing latency honestly.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
7. NOTE EDITOR UX — ORIGINAL DESIGN
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Use an original visual language rather than reproducing Melodyne, Flex Pitch or
VariAudio layouts.

Suggested Poietek visual:
- waveform/spectrogram underlay;
- “note ribbons” rather than proprietary-style blobs;
- center line = detected/target pitch;
- thin trace = actual pitch curve;
- colored confidence halo;
- onset/transient flag;
- sibilant/noise region overlay;
- handles appear only on selection;
- side inspector for numerical values.

Gestures:
Drag vertically = note pitch.
Alt/Option-drag = fine cents.
Drag body horizontally = timing.
Drag left/right edge = duration.
Draw tool = pitch contour.
Split at cursor.
Join selected notes.
Double-click property = reset to original.

Accessibility:
All operations also available in inspector and keyboard commands.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
8. POLYPHONIC AUDIO RESEARCH
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Goal:
Detect/edit meaningful simultaneous note events in suitable sources such as
piano/guitar, without marketing the capability until objective quality gates are
met.

Research components:
- harmonic/percussive separation;
- constant-Q/spectral representations;
- multi-pitch estimation;
- onset/offset tracking;
- harmonic assignment;
- source/timbre consistency;
- note-specific resynthesis;
- confidence and ambiguity visualization.

Fallback:
If confidence is insufficient, do not pretend individual-note editability.
Offer:
- stem/source separation;
- harmonic-region transpose;
- chord detection;
- spectral editing;
- convert/re-record suggestion.

Patent/legal review is mandatory before implementing any method derived from
patented commercial techniques. Prefer published research and clean-room
implementation.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9. VOCAL STACK WORKFLOW
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Example:
Lead vocal recorded.
Three doubles and two harmonies recorded.

1. Analyze Lead.
2. User optionally tunes Lead manually.
3. Select doubles.
4. Align timing to Lead at 70%.
5. Keep pitch identity but correct target notes at 45%.
6. Preserve protected breaths/ad-libs.
7. Apply level matching lightly.
8. Render previews.
9. Accept creates AlignmentOperations, not destructive replacements.
10. Harmonies can use timing guide but preserve their harmonic interval.

This reproduces the useful *workflow principle* of alignment products without
copying their implementation.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
10. DIALOGUE / ADR MODE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Video/VFX project integration:
Guide = production dialogue.
Target = ADR recording.

Prioritize:
word/onset timing
phrase duration
consonant/transient alignment
level consistency

Pitch transfer should be optional/minimal for dialogue.

Store alignment map with the linked video scene/take.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
11. AUDIO-TO-MIDI
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Monophonic:
detected note -> MIDI pitch/start/duration/velocity/confidence.

Options:
quantize timing
retain microtiming
pitch bend from actual contour
vibrato to pitch bend
expression from level envelope

Polyphonic audio-to-MIDI is a later research mode and should expose uncertainty.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
12. CHORD / SCALE CONTEXT
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The pitch editor should not own its own unrelated scale system.
Consume the project's:
ChordTrack
KeyMap
ScaleMap
TuningSystem

Correction target modes:
Nearest Chromatic
Project Scale
Chord Tones
Chord + Allowed Extensions
MIDI Guide
Reference Melody
Custom Microtonal Scale

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
13. AI ASSISTANCE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

AI does not directly rewrite PCM without a preview/operation record.

Possible commands:
AnalyzeVocalPitch
SuggestPitchCorrections
SuggestNoteSplits
SuggestVocalAlignment
DetectHarmonyParts
CreateHarmonyMidi
SuggestNaturalnessSettings

AI must preserve the user's expressive intent by default.
Offer:
Transparent
Tight
Effect
Custom.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
14. OFFLINE / CROSS-DEVICE BEHAVIOR
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Store:
analysis version
note segmentation data
edit operations
render settings

Do not require recomputation after every device switch if compatible cached
analysis exists.

If device cannot run high-quality render:
- edit operations remain editable;
- play a cached proxy/freeze;
- render later on capable device.

Core monophonic edit should remain available offline once media and analysis
models/code are local.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
15. COLLABORATION
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Pitch edits are collaborative structured operations:
user A moves note 42 +14 cents.
user B changes vibrato on note 42.

Different parameters can merge.
Conflicting edits to the same parameter use revision history/conflict UI.

Comments can anchor to:
note
time range
word/phrase
audio clip

Activity example:
“Jordan adjusted pitch center of 8 notes on Lead Vocal.”
“Sam aligned BGV Double 2 to Lead at 65% timing strength.”

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
16. TESTING / QUALITY GATES
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

PIT-001 Monophonic pitch detector reports cents error against synthetic fixtures.
PIT-002 Voiced/unvoiced detection does not tune breaths/silence.
PIT-003 Scale correction is deterministic.
PIT-004 Fine-pitch edits survive save/reopen.
PIT-005 Edits are reversible.
PIT-006 Time movement does not alter target pitch unintentionally.
PIT-007 Pitch movement preserves duration in Edit Tune.
PIT-008 Formant preserve mode has measurable expected behavior.
PIT-009 Original/processed A/B gain is matched within tolerance.
PIT-010 Live Tune latency is measured/reported.
PIT-011 Low-confidence frames fail safely.
PIT-012 Alignment protected regions remain unchanged.
PIT-013 Guide/Dub timing strength interpolates predictably.
PIT-014 Audio-to-MIDI timestamps align to detected notes.
PIT-015 Collaboration converges on non-conflicting note parameters.
PIT-016 Polyphonic mode stays hidden/experimental until benchmark thresholds pass.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
17. CLEAN-ROOM / BRAND RULE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Internal research notes may cite:
Celemony Melodyne
Antares AutoTune
Synchro Arts Revoice Pro
Waves Tune
Apple Logic Flex Pitch
Steinberg VariAudio
Auburn Sounds Graillon

Public Poietek features should use original names such as:
Quick Tune
Live Tune
Note Edit
Align
Poly Note Lab
Creative Pitch

Do not use competitors' trademarks as Poietek feature names.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
