#################### 17_DJ_DVS_LIVE_REMIX_SET_PREPARATION_PERFORMANCE_ARCHITECTURE.txt ####################

POIETEK / STUDIO DAW STATION (SDS)
MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 1.2 ADDENDUM

17 — DJ, DVS, LIVE REMIX, SET PREPARATION & PERFORMANCE ARCHITECTURE
=====================================================================

PURPOSE

This document converts the creator-supplied DJ-software comparison and verified
first-party product capabilities into an original Poietek/SDS subsystem.

The goal is NOT to clone Serato, rekordbox, Traktor, VirtualDJ, djay, Mixxx or
Ableton Live. The goal is to understand the functional principles that successful
DJ/performance tools solve, then implement original Poietek/SDS workflows using
the same canonical project, asset, hardware and collaboration architecture.

The current SDS repository already contains a DJ workspace prototype. Treat that
as an interface sketch. This document specifies the engine and data architecture
required to make DJ mode a real performance system.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. CORE PRODUCT IDEA
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

DJ functionality should not be a disconnected mini-application.

A DJ Set is another project mode operating on the same:
Asset Store
Audio Engine
MIDI / HID / Hardware Mapper
Stem Engine
Video Engine
Rights / Metadata Layer
Community / Publishing Layer
Sync / Offline Layer

A creator can move between:
Production Project
DJ Set
Live Performance Set
Video/VJ Set

without duplicating source media or losing metadata.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. REFERENCE CAPABILITY FAMILIES
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

A. LIBRARY PREPARATION / CLUB-MEDIA EXPORT

Current rekordbox documentation demonstrates the value of separating music
preparation from live performance:
- track analysis;
- playlists;
- cue/beatgrid data;
- USB export to supported players;
- device-library backup;
- library sync across devices;
- DVS, video and stems in performance-oriented workflows.

Poietek/SDS interpretation:
Create `PerformanceLibraryService` and `PerformanceMediaExporter`.

TrackPerformanceMetadata {
  assetId
  bpm
  variableTempoMap?
  beatGrid
  phraseMarkers[]
  hotCues[]
  memoryCues[]
  loops[]
  key
  energy?
  color?
  rating?
  tags[]
  comments?
  stemAnalysisId?
  waveformPeakId
  deckPreferences?
}

PerformanceExportProfile {
  id
  destinationType
  targetHardwareFamily?
  supportedAudioFormats[]
  supportsCueMetadata
  supportsBeatgridMetadata
  supportsPlaylistMetadata
  supportsArtwork
  supportsStems
  supportsVideo
  verificationLevel
}

Critical rule:
Never pretend SDS can write a manufacturer's proprietary media-library format
unless the format is documented/licensed and the exporter has been tested.

Generic fallback:
Export ordinary folders/playlists/audio plus an SDS metadata sidecar.

B. DECK PERFORMANCE

Current Serato, Traktor, VirtualDJ and djay materials all reinforce a core live
deck model:
- multiple decks;
- waveform view;
- pitch/tempo;
- cue;
- loops;
- hot cues;
- sync;
- effects;
- sampler;
- stems;
- hardware control.

Poietek/SDS should define a universal `DeckEngine`.

DeckState {
  deckId
  loadedAssetId?
  playState
  positionSamples
  tempoRatio
  pitchPercent
  keyLock
  syncMode
  beatgridId?
  quantize
  slipMode
  loop?
  activeHotCues[]
  stemMix
  effectsRackId?
  outputBusId
  controlSource
}

Deck counts are capability-driven:
2-deck compact
4-deck professional
optional auxiliary/sample/stem/live-input decks

Do not hardcode the entire application to two decks.

C. BEATGRIDS / VARIABLE TEMPO

Serato documentation shows the importance of editable beatgrids, especially for
material with changing tempo, and Traktor Pro 4 currently advertises flexible
beatgrids designed to follow tempo changes.

Poietek/SDS needs `TempoMapAnalyzer`.

BeatGrid {
  downbeat
  beatMarkers[]
  barMarkers[]
  tempoSegments[]
  confidence
  locked
}

Modes:
Fixed BPM
Variable Tempo
Free / No Grid
Manual Grid
Imported Grid

Grid operations:
Set downbeat
Move marker
Add tempo marker
Stretch region
Reset section
Lock
Analyze selected region

Sync must use the tempo map rather than only an average BPM.

D. SYNC

Implement multiple behaviors rather than one magic Sync button.

OFF
No sync.

TEMPO SYNC
Match tempo but leave phrase/bar position under performer control.

BEAT SYNC
Match tempo and beat phase.

PHRASE ASSIST
Optional suggested drop/transition points using phrase analysis, not forced.

LINK / EXTERNAL CLOCK
Support external synchronization adapters such as MIDI Clock and Ableton Link
where platform/API support exists.

Manual beatmatching remains fully supported.

E. DVS / TURNTABLE CONTROL

Serato, Traktor, VirtualDJ, Mixxx and djay all demonstrate the continuing value
of Digital Vinyl Systems.

Create `DvsEngine`.

Responsibilities:
- receive stereo timecode input;
- calibrate signal;
- detect speed;
- direction;
- relative/absolute position when control signal supports it;
- needle/drop behavior;
- scratch movement;
- input-level/quality display;
- dropout/noise resilience;
- latency compensation;
- deck routing.

DvsCalibration {
  inputDevice
  inputChannels
  signalType
  mode
  noiseFloor
  signalStrength
  channelBalance
  polarity
  calibrationQuality
  latencyOffset
}

User-facing modes:
ABSOLUTE
RELATIVE
INTERNAL

Important clean-room/legal rule:
Timecode formats/protocols may be proprietary.
Support only documented/open/licensed formats or user-authorized generic
compatible signal modes after legal review.

Never copy proprietary decoder code.

F. SCRATCH / JOG ENGINE

DVS and platter/jog control require a different quality bar from ordinary
timeline scrubbing.

Requirements:
- very low control-to-audio latency;
- stable reverse;
- rapid acceleration/deceleration;
- key-lock behavior option;
- vinyl brake/start curves;
- slip mode;
- backspin;
- needle search;
- motorized platter feedback adapters where documented.

Add a `ScratchTransport` layer that converts hardware motion into sample-position
changes with interpolation and no audible discontinuity.

G. STEM DJING / LIVE REMIX

Current Serato, rekordbox, Traktor, VirtualDJ and djay all support stem-oriented
DJ workflows in differing forms.

Poietek/SDS should reuse the central Stem Engine rather than implementing a
second DJ-only separator.

Standard performance stem buses:
Vocals
Drums
Bass
Music / Instruments

Optional expanded models:
Kick
Snare
Hi-hat/Percussion
Lead
Harmony
Other

Per deck:
mute/solo
level
EQ/filter
FX send
crossfader assignment
performance pad trigger
stem-aware loop
stem-aware cue
stem swap
stem capture

Important:
Realtime separation may be device-dependent.
If live separation is too expensive:
use pre-analyzed/pre-rendered stems from set preparation.

H. STEM CROSSFADER / MORPHING

Original Poietek concept:
A normal crossfader can be switched into a multi-layer transition surface.

Examples:
Deck A vocal -> Deck B vocal
Deck A drums -> Deck B drums
Bass held from A while music changes to B
Vocals removed during transition

Represent crossfade as automation curves over stem buses, not destructive audio.

I. REMIX / SAMPLE DECKS

Traktor Remix Decks, Serato Sampler, VirtualDJ sampler decks and Ableton clip
launching all reinforce live-triggered content.

Poietek/SDS implementation:
`PerformanceGrid`

Slots can contain:
Audio loop
One-shot
Stem
MIDI clip
Video loop
Lighting cue
Macro scene

Grid supports:
quantized launch
legato launch
gate
toggle
one-shot
loop
follow action
scene launch
record-to-arrangement
MIDI/hardware feedback

This integrates the existing SDS sampler/pad architecture with DJ performance.

J. CLIP / SCENE PERFORMANCE

Ableton Live's Session/clip model demonstrates the value of nonlinear performance
beside a linear arrangement.

Poietek/SDS Live Set should support:
Decks + PerformanceGrid + Scenes.

A scene can recall:
clips
stem states
FX
mixer snapshot
video scene
lighting scene
tempo range
hardware mappings

A DJ can perform the set and optionally record the performance into the standard
Poietek Arrangement timeline.

K. VIDEO MIXING / VJ

VirtualDJ and rekordbox both document video performance features, while djay has
video-oriented workflows on mobile.

Poietek/SDS DJ mode should connect directly to Document 08 Video/VFX.

Video Deck:
video clip linked to audio track
standalone video loop
camera input
visualizer
text/lyrics layer

Controls:
video crossfader
transition
opacity
blend
VFX
beat sync
stem-reactive visuals
camera switching
output display selection

A music-only deck can automatically drive visualizers without inventing fake
video media.

L. CONTROLLER UNIVERSALITY

VirtualDJ and Mixxx strongly demonstrate the value of broad/custom controller
mapping; djay demonstrates class-compliant, cross-platform controller workflows.

Poietek/SDS must keep the universal mapper at the center.

PhysicalControl
-> DeviceProfile
-> LogicalDJControl
-> Deck/Performance command.

LogicalDJControl examples:
deckA.play
deckA.cue
deckA.jog
deckA.pitch
deckA.hotcue.1
deckA.loop.length
mixer.crossfader
mixer.ch1.eq.low
stems.deckA.vocal
performance.slot.1
video.crossfader

Profiles can be:
built-in
manufacturer-documented
community-tested
user-created

M. MIDI / HID / NETWORK

Support architecture should allow:
MIDI
HID
OSC
network control
documented vendor protocols
timecode/DVS audio input

Do not force high-resolution jog wheels through ordinary 7-bit MIDI when a
documented higher-resolution protocol exists.

N. MOBILE / TABLET DJ

djay's published cross-platform hardware guidance and iOS product show that a
serious DJ workflow can be touch-first.

Poietek mobile/tablet DJ mode:
large deck controls
waveforms
jog/scratch
hot cues
loops
stems
performance pads
mixer
pre-cue
external class-compliant controller support where platform permits
Bluetooth MIDI where supported
DVS where audio I/O/platform permits
4-deck layout on sufficiently large displays

Phone mode prioritizes two decks.
Tablet can expose 4 decks, performance grid and mixer simultaneously.

O. PRE-CUE / AUDIO ROUTING

DJ mode requires:
Master
Booth
Headphones / Cue
Deck sends
Sampler
Microphone
Aux / Live Input

Headphone:
cue mix
cue/master blend
split cue
level

Native desktop should support multichannel interfaces.
Mobile supports class-compliant devices where available.

P. FX

Per-deck
send
insert
master
stem-specific
performance macro

FX timing can lock to beatgrid or run free.

Macros can combine:
filter + echo + reverb + stem mute + video transition.

Q. LIBRARY / CRATES / SET PREP

Performance Browser:
Collection
Playlists
Crates/Tags
Setlists
History
Recently Added
Analyzed
Needs Analysis
Missing Files
Streaming Sources
Cloud/Team Library
Remix Assets

Track analysis:
BPM
variable tempo
key
waveform
loudness
phrases
vocal position
stem availability
quality/problem warnings

Never silently modify original media metadata if user chooses project-only
metadata.

R. SET HISTORY

Every performance can create:
PlayedTrackEvent {
  assetId
  loadedAt
  playedAt
  durationPlayed
  deckId
  transitionFrom?
  cueStart?
  recordingTimeRange?
}

Benefits:
set history
credits
radio/broadcast reporting
playlist export
community posting
royalty-reporting integration later where applicable

S. RECORD / BROADCAST

Record:
master mix
individual decks optional
mic
stems
performance events
video output

Broadcast adapters later:
audio stream
video stream
external encoder
community live room

Streaming-service restrictions can prohibit recording/export of protected
streamed tracks. The application must enforce provider policy and clearly
distinguish local-owned/licensed files from streams.

T. MUSIC STREAMING CONNECTORS

Streaming services are optional connector adapters.
Do not make core DJ operation depend on them.

StreamingTrack {
 provider
 providerTrackId
 offlineRights
 recordingAllowed
 stemProcessingAllowed
 cachedUntil?
 territory?
}

Never assume a provider permits:
offline caching
recording
stem separation
export
commercial use.

U. SET PROJECT / PERFORMANCE OBJECTS

DJSetProject extends the canonical Project through composition, not a separate
file format.

PerformanceSet {
 id
 title
 setlist[]
 scenes[]
 deckLayout
 performanceGrid
 mixerLayout
 preferredMappings[]
 videoLayout?
 lightingLayout?
 recordingSettings
 exportTargets[]
}

Track preparation metadata stays reusable across multiple sets.

V. DJ ASSISTANT / AI

AI may:
suggest compatible next tracks
detect phrases
detect variable tempo
suggest cue points
suggest transition regions
prepare stems
identify key clashes
suggest loops
propose set flow
create practice challenges
analyze a recorded set

AI must NOT:
auto-publish copyrighted mixes;
pretend streamed media can legally be recorded;
replace the performer's choices without an explicit Automix mode.

W. AUTOMIX

Optional:
party/background mode
practice set audition
crossfade preview

Parameters:
transition length
beat/phrase aware
energy curve
key compatibility
stem transition style
exclude tracks
manual override

Manual live performance remains the main professional workflow.

X. PERFORMANCE RELIABILITY

DJ/live mode must have a stricter runtime policy than ordinary editing.

Before performance:
preload critical media
precompute waveform/beatgrid/stems where possible
verify audio device
verify controller
verify sample rate
verify mapping
disable sleep
show battery/power
show missing/offline assets
show storage
show CPU/GPU
show network only if required

Performance Guard:
prevents background project migrations/large downloads/AI jobs from causing
audio dropouts during a live set.

Y. OFFLINE-FIRST DJ

Once a set and its permitted media are stored locally:
- open set offline;
- analyze local files offline;
- use decks;
- use DVS;
- use local stems/models;
- use controller mappings;
- record set;
- video/VFX from local assets;
- export history.

Cloud sync and streaming are enhancements, not requirements.

Z. CROSS-DEVICE SYNC

Sync:
playlists
setlists
hot cues
beatgrids
loops
tags
device mappings
performance layouts
project scenes
analysis results where portable

Large source media sync uses AssetStore policies.

Per-device:
desktop layout
tablet performance layout
phone compact layout.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3. ORIGINAL POIETEK DJ WORKSPACES
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Suggested public names:

SET PREP
Library, analysis, cue/loop/beatgrid editing, setlist building.

DECKS
Classic multi-deck mixing.

VINYL
DVS/scratch calibration and performance.

STEM MIX
Stem-focused live remixing.

PERFORMANCE GRID
Samples, loops, clips and scenes.

LIVE MIXER
DJ mixer, cues, mic, booth and FX.

VISUAL DECKS
Video/VJ and audio-reactive visuals.

SET RECORDER
Audio/video capture, set history and metadata.

HARDWARE
Controller mapping and diagnostics.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4. CURRENT SDS DJWORKSPACE MIGRATION
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Keep:
- recognizable two-deck performance metaphor;
- physical-hardware orientation;
- faders/jog/FX concepts.

Replace:
- simulated deck state with DeckEngine;
- fake local UI timing with audio clock;
- hardcoded controller assumptions with MappingService;
- one-screen-only DJ state with PerformanceSet domain objects;
- visual-only FX with real AudioGraph nodes;
- fake waveform with asset-derived peaks;
- fake sync with BeatGrid/SyncEngine;
- fake scratch with ScratchTransport.

The existing component should eventually be renamed from a generic demo
`DJWorkspace` implementation into a view layer over the performance services.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
7. DVS IMPLEMENTATION PHASES
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

DVS PHASE 1
Generic calibration UI + internal scratch transport using mouse/touch/jog.

DVS PHASE 2
Support a legally/documented generic or licensed timecode path.
Relative mode first.

DVS PHASE 3
Absolute-position capable control where protocol permits.

DVS PHASE 4
Stems + DVS and advanced motorized-platter integrations where reliable.

Do not let DVS delay core DeckEngine.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------

pad LEDs
VU meters

track title

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9. PERFORMANCE GRID + DAW CONVERGENCE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

One of Poietek's strongest differentiators should be that a beatmaker does not
have to export a song to a separate DJ program just to perform it.

Production Arrangement
-> Publish to Performance Scene
-> stem groups automatically available
-> loops/hot cues suggested
-> performance pads generated
-> DJ version can be remixed live
-> live performance recorded back into Arrangement.

This connects the DAW, sampler, stems and DJ system directly.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
10. VIDEO / MUSIC VIDEO PERFORMANCE CONVERGENCE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

A finished music-video project can also generate a live set package:

Audio master
stems
video
alternate loops
lyrics/title overlays
lighting cues
hot cues
transition scenes

The creator can then perform the release live using the same source project.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
11. COLLABORATION
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Team set preparation:
DJ A edits setlist.
Producer changes stems.
Visual artist adds visuals.
Lighting designer adjusts cues.

Structured changes sync locally-first.

During live performance:
collaboration mode becomes conservative.
Remote collaborators cannot unexpectedly change the currently playing deck
unless performer explicitly enables remote-control permission.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
12. RIGHTS / SET HISTORY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Set history can reference:
recording/work identifiers
artist
title
source
time played

Future radio/venue reporting systems can consume this history through explicit
connectors.

Do not promise automatic royalty payment merely because a track was detected.
Payment/reporting depends on territory, broadcaster/venue, PRO/CMO, agreements
and supported integrations.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
13. CLEAN-ROOM / LICENSING NOTES
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Mixxx is open-source and currently states GPLv2 licensing in its official GitHub
repository. Study its architecture and user-facing principles, but integrating
GPL code into Poietek has licensing consequences. Do not copy code unless the
entire intended distribution model is compatible or legal counsel approves.

Other referenced products are proprietary. Use only published capabilities and
documented/open interoperability methods.

Avoid public feature names that imply affiliation:
Serato Mode
rekordbox Mode
Traktor Deck
VirtualDJ Mixer
etc.

Use original Poietek terms.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
14. ACCEPTANCE TESTS
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

DJ-001 Load two real tracks and perform uninterrupted crossfade.
DJ-002 Four-deck mode works on capable target.
DJ-003 Real waveform peaks follow source media.
DJ-004 Cue points persist after restart.
DJ-005 Saved loops persist.
DJ-006 Beatgrid can represent changing tempo.
DJ-007 Tempo Sync and Beat Sync behave differently.
DJ-008 Manual pitch/jog control remains available with Sync off.
DJ-009 Quantized hot cue lands within defined timing tolerance.
DJ-010 Slip mode returns to expected timeline position.
DJ-011 Stem mute/solo operates per deck.
DJ-012 Stem analysis can be prepared offline.
DJ-013 PerformanceGrid launches samples/loops in sync.
DJ-014 Scene launch can trigger audio + visual states.
DJ-015 Hardware mapping persists and provides feedback where supported.
DJ-016 Controller disconnect does not crash audio.
DJ-017 Replacement controller can map to same logical deck controls.
DJ-018 Pre-cue is independent of master output.
DJ-019 Multichannel output mapping persists.
DJ-020 DVS calibration clearly shows signal quality.
DJ-021 DVS scratch direction/speed follows test control signal.
DJ-022 DVS failure falls back safely without exploding playback.
DJ-023 Set Recorder captures allowed local media mix.
DJ-024 Streaming policy can disable recording where provider forbids it.
DJ-025 Set history records tracks/times accurately.
DJ-026 Video crossfader does not disturb audio timing.
DJ-027 Local set opens and performs with network disabled.
DJ-028 Set metadata syncs to a second device.
DJ-029 Performance Guard prevents heavy background jobs from causing dropouts.
DJ-030 Variable-tempo track remains synchronized using editable tempo map.
DJ-031 Live performance changes can be recorded back to Arrangement.
DJ-032 DJ project can reuse DAW stems without duplicating the media asset.
DJ-033 Rights/set history export remains metadata-only unless user invokes an
       authorized reporting connector.
DJ-034 Hardware-export adapter refuses unsupported proprietary destination
       rather than fabricating compatibility.
DJ-035 Mobile/tablet layout exposes only controls that fit the current platform.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
15. IMPLEMENTATION ROADMAP
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

DJ PHASE A — REAL DECKS
DeckEngine, waveform, cue, loop, pitch, crossfader, pre-cue.

DJ PHASE B — BEATGRID/SYNC
Analysis, manual grid, variable tempo, Tempo/Beat Sync, quantize.

DJ PHASE C — HARDWARE
Real controller mapping, output feedback, jog resolution, audio routing.

DJ PHASE D — STEMS
Prepared stems first, realtime separation second.

DJ PHASE E — PERFORMANCE GRID
Samples, loops, clips, scenes, record to arrangement.

DJ PHASE F — DVS
Calibration + legally supportable timecode path.

DJ PHASE G — VIDEO
Video deck + crossfader + visuals.

DJ PHASE H — SET PREP / EXPORT
Device/hardware export adapters with strict verification.

DJ PHASE I — MOBILE
Touch and class-compliant hardware.

DJ PHASE J — STREAMING / COMMUNITY LIVE
Only after provider/legal/latency architecture is stable.

#################### 18_PLUGIN_HOST_SYNTHESIS_MODULATION_EFFECTS_MASTERING_ARCHITECTURE.txt ####################

POIETEK / STUDIO DAW STATION (SDS)
MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 1.3 ADDENDUM

18 — PLUGIN HOST, SYNTHESIS, MODULATION, EFFECTS & MASTERING ARCHITECTURE
=======================================================================

PURPOSE

This document converts the creator-supplied research on virtual instruments,
modulators and effects into an original Poietek/SDS architecture.

The supplied material grouped virtual studio tools into three useful creative
families:
1. Synthesizers / sound generators.
2. Modulators / movement creators.
3. Effects / sound processors.

That framing is retained because it is useful for users, but one architectural
correction is required:

PLUGIN ROLE and PLUGIN FORMAT are separate dimensions.

Examples:
- "Instrument" is a role.
- "Effect" is a role.
- "Modulator" is a role.
- VST3, AU, CLAP, LV2 and AAX are formats/APIs/ecosystems.

A third-party wavetable instrument may be VST3, CLAP or AU.
A native Poietek instrument may use no external plug-in format at all.
A Poietek modulator may drive both native and hosted parameters.

The goal is therefore TWO connected systems:

A. POIETEK NATIVE SOUND PLATFORM
Built-in synths, samplers, modulators, processors, analyzers and mastering tools.

B. THIRD-PARTY PLUGIN HOST
Loads external plug-ins where the operating system, licence, security model and
plug-in format permit.

Do not clone proprietary DSP or interfaces. Study published capabilities,
workflow principles and open standards, then implement original designs.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. RESEARCH UPDATE — CURRENT VERSIONS / VERIFIED DIRECTIONS
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The creator-supplied comparison referenced several older product generations.
As of August 2026, first-party sources show important evolution:

Xfer Serum:
The current product is Serum 2. It now combines wavetable, multisample, ordinary
sample, granular and spectral oscillator types. Its current page also describes
sample slicing, real-time score extraction, spectral resynthesis and multiple
warp/modulation options.

Vital:
Current first-party material still emphasizes spectral-warping wavetable
synthesis, highly visual feedback, drag-and-drop modulation, editable LFOs,
audio-rate modulation, MPE and microtonal support. Vital's current Basic tier
states that the complete synthesizer feature set is available, with paid tiers
adding content/services.

Spectrasonics Omnisphere:
Current first-party pages emphasize a broad hybrid architecture: sample +
real-time synthesis, wavetable/granular/FM/ring modulation, deep modulation,
layering, user-audio import, effects and extensive physical-hardware integration.
Current product pages also emphasize a much broader hardware-profile system than
the older comparison material.

Arturia V Collection:
The current V Collection 11 Pro page describes a large instrument family
spanning modeled analog/digital instruments, sampled/acoustic hybrids, unified
preset browsing, hardware mapping and tutorials. The clean-room lesson is not
"copy classic synth panels"; it is "many different engines can share one
consistent browser, macro and controller layer."

Cableguys ShaperBox:
Current first-party material describes a multi-effect modulation environment
containing Reverb, Time, Drive, Noise, Liquid, Filter, Crush, Volume, Pan and
Width shaping, with waveform editing and multiband/rhythmic processing.

Xfer LFOTool:
Current first-party page describes customizable point/tension LFO curves,
multiple simultaneous graphs, BPM/Hz operation, sample-accurate sync and MIDI CC
output. The key transferable principle is that a modulation source can be a
first-class reusable control signal rather than a feature hidden inside a synth.

Soundtoys Tremolator:
Current first-party material describes tempo-synchronized tremolo/auto-gating,
programmable rhythmic patterns and dynamic response. The transferable principle
is musically synchronized modulation with custom curves and envelope-following.

FabFilter Pro-Q:
The creator supplied Pro-Q 3; the current official product is Pro-Q 4. Current
documentation includes static EQ, dynamic EQ, spectral dynamics, linear phase,
mid/side-related workflows and highly contextual controls. The clean-room lesson
is progressive disclosure: advanced controls appear when the user needs them.

Soundtoys 5:
Current first-party material describes a unified effects collection and Effect
Rack combining saturation, delay, pitch, modulation, filter and spatial effects.
The transferable principle is an effects-container workflow where creative chains
can be saved as a single reusable unit.

Valhalla VintageVerb:
Current first-party material describes a collection of distinct algorithmic
"modes" with different sonic characters. The clean-room lesson is to expose
meaningful high-level algorithm choices while keeping a compact control surface.

iZotope Ozone:
The creator supplied Ozone 11; current first-party material is Ozone 12.
Current Ozone 12 materials describe a modular mastering hub, editable individual
modules, track/reference workflows, AI-assisted mastering, dynamic EQ, limiting,
imaging, excitation, stabilizing, stem-aware tools and explicit user control.
The transferable lesson is "assistant proposes a chain, engineer remains in
control."

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. PLUGIN ROLE TAXONOMY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Poietek should expose a simple top-level browser but use richer internal roles.

CREATIVE TOP LEVEL

INSTRUMENTS
- Synthesizer
- Sampler
- Drum instrument
- Physical-model instrument
- Hybrid instrument
- ROM/sample-library instrument
- Generative instrument

MODULATORS
- LFO
- Envelope
- Step modulator
- Curve/shape modulator
- Envelope follower
- Random source
- Sample & hold
- Macro
- Note-expression mapper
- MIDI/CC generator
- Audio-rate modulator where safe/supported

EFFECTS
- EQ/filter
- Dynamics
- Saturation/distortion
- Delay/echo
- Reverb
- Modulation effect
- Pitch/time
- Stereo/spatial
- Spectral
- Restoration
- Creative multi-FX
- Mastering
- Meter/analyzer
- Utility

ADDITIONAL INTERNAL ROLES

MIDI / NOTE EFFECT
Changes note/event streams before an instrument.

ANALYZER
Produces metering/feature data but may not alter audio.

GENERATOR
Produces audio, MIDI, automation or modulation without ordinary note input.

CONTAINER
Hosts multiple instruments/effects/modulators as one reusable rack.

BRIDGE
External hardware or network processor.

VIDEO / CONTROL EFFECT
Not an audio plug-in role, but uses the same extension registry concepts.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3. PLUGIN FORMAT POLICY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

3.1 VST3

Priority: HIGH for desktop.

Current Steinberg first-party developer material states that VST technology /
the current VST3 SDK is open source under the MIT licence, while the VST name and
logo remain Steinberg trademarks with separate usage rules.

Poietek implications:
- Implement VST3 hosting on Windows/macOS/Linux desktop where technically stable.
- Preserve Steinberg copyright/licence notices as required.
- Follow trademark/logo usage rules if displaying official branding.
- Create automated compatibility tests.
- Do not support abandoned VST2 by default.

3.2 CLAP

Priority: HIGH for desktop.

The current CLAP reference repository describes:
- stable ABI;
- plug-in/host objects;
- state;
- parameters;
- audio/note ports;
- real-time/offline rendering;
- latency/tail reporting;
- GUI;
- voice information;
- threading support;
- preset discovery/load;
- host-provided track context;
- remote controls;
- modulation/note-expression-oriented extensions.

The reference implementation is MIT licensed.

Poietek implications:
CLAP should be a first-class host target alongside VST3 because its architecture
fits Poietek's modulation-heavy, MPE and future-extension goals.

3.3 AUDIO UNIT

Priority:
HIGH on macOS.
HIGH for AUv3 interoperability on Apple mobile where practical.

Apple currently documents AUv3 hosting for macOS and iOS and recommends AUv3 for
new development, while AUv2 is in maintenance mode.

Poietek implications:
- macOS: host appropriate AU/AUv3 plug-ins.
- iOS/iPadOS: support AUv3 extensions where platform rules permit.
- isolate platform-specific state from canonical Project state.
- store component identity + version + serialized state + freeze fallback.

3.4 LV2

Priority:
MEDIUM, especially Linux/open-source ecosystem.

Retain as a planned format from the broader Poietek specification.
Before implementation, verify current LV2 SDK/API/licensing and create a dedicated
format adapter and validation suite.

3.5 AAX

Priority as HOST: LOW / NOT INITIAL TARGET.

Avid's current developer site describes the AAX SDK as a development kit for
creating AAX plug-ins for Pro Tools, VENUE S6L and Media Composer, with separate
commercialization/signing requirements.

Poietek should NOT casually promise to "host AAX plug-ins."
Treat AAX as:
- a possible future export/build target if Poietek later ships its own plug-ins;
- subject to Avid licensing, signing and developer-program rules.

Do not display AAX in Poietek's "supported host formats" until an actual legal
and technical host path exists.

3.6 INTERNAL POIETEK PLUGIN API

Required for:
Web/PWA
portable native modules
community extensions
safe built-in modules
future cloud render workers

This API should be capability-based and may have:
- native Rust/C++ backend implementation;
- WASM implementation for portable processing;
- JavaScript/TypeScript only for non-realtime UI/control extensions.

Do not run arbitrary JavaScript on the audio thread.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4. PLUGIN HOST SERVICE ARCHITECTURE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

PluginRegistry
PluginScanner
PluginMetadataDatabase
PluginHost
PluginProcessManager
PluginStateStore
PluginPresetIndex
PluginLatencyManager
PluginAutomationBridge
PluginMidiBridge
PluginModulationBridge
PluginGuiHost
PluginSandbox
PluginCrashReporter
PluginQuarantine
PluginCompatibilityService
PluginFreezeService

Canonical identity:

PluginDescriptor {
  stableId
  format
  vendor
  productName
  version
  role[]
  architecture
  binaryPath
  capabilities
  ioConfigurations[]
  parameterSummary
  hasEditor
  latencyMode
  stateVersion?
  scanStatus
  trustStatus
  lastKnownGoodVersion?
}

PluginInstance {
  instanceId
  descriptorId
  stateBlobRef
  parameterOverrides
  bypass
  wetDry
  routing
  automationBindings
  modulationBindings
  latencySamples
  tailSamples
  frozenRenderId?
  missingReason?
}

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
5. PLUGIN SCANNING / FAILURE CONTAINMENT
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Never scan third-party plug-ins inside the main DAW process if avoidable.

Scanner process:
1. discover candidate binaries/bundles;
2. launch isolated scanner;
3. load one plug-in;
4. inspect metadata/I/O;
5. open/close editor where safe;
6. serialize test state;
7. perform short render;
8. record crash/hang;
9. save compatibility result;
10. unload.

States:
UNSCANNED
SCANNING
VALID
WARNING
QUARANTINED
BLOCKED
MISSING
VERSION_CHANGED

A plug-in crash during scanning must not crash the studio.

A plug-in that crashes three times in the same project can automatically reopen
disabled while preserving its state.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
6. PLUGIN SANDBOX
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Desktop:
Prefer a separate process or process pool for untrusted third-party plug-ins.

Tradeoffs:
- stronger crash isolation;
- IPC overhead;
- GUI embedding complexity;
- real-time scheduling design;
- shared-memory audio buffers required.

Modes:
SAFE
NORMAL
LOW-LATENCY TRUSTED

User can override with warnings.

Never silently give plug-ins unrestricted:
filesystem
network
microphone
camera
project database
user credentials.

Legacy plug-in APIs may not support capability sandboxing perfectly, so Poietek
must distinguish "OS-process isolated" from "fully permission sandboxed."

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
7. STATE / PORTABILITY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

A project stores:
plugin identity
format
vendor/product
version
serialized plug-in state
host parameter mappings
automation
modulation
routing
optional freeze render

If plug-in unavailable:
Show "Missing plug-in" placeholder.
Keep state.
Keep automation.
Play freeze/bounce if available.
Never delete unknown state.

Cross-device example:
Desktop VST3 synth -> freeze render exists.
Open iPad:
- hear freeze;
- edit rest of song;
- MIDI remains;
- plug-in shown unavailable.
Return desktop:
- restore live plug-in.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
8. PLUGIN DELAY COMPENSATION
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Every hosted processor must report or be measured/configured for:
latencySamples
tailSamples

Graph computes path latency.
Delay compensation aligns:
dry tracks
parallel buses
sidechains
returns
master paths

Changes in latency can trigger graph realignment outside unsafe callback points.

Low-Latency Record mode can bypass selected high-latency processors temporarily.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9. SIDECHAIN / MULTI-BUS / MULTICHANNEL
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Host model must support more than stereo in/out.

AudioBus {
  role: main | sidechain | aux | analysis | surround | ambisonic
  channels
  layout
}

Support:
mono
stereo
multi-mono
surround
immersive later
sidechain buses
multiple outputs from instruments
multi-output drum instruments.

Do not hardcode every plug-in instance to one stereo input and one stereo output.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
10. AUTOMATION AND MODULATION ARE DIFFERENT
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

AUTOMATION
Timeline-owned deterministic parameter values.

MODULATION
Continuous relationship between a source and destination.

Example:
Filter cutoff automation = producer drew a curve over bars.
Filter cutoff modulation = LFO moves cutoff around that automation baseline.

EffectiveValue =
Base
+ Automation
+ ModulationSum
then clamped/transformed according to parameter semantics.

Plug-in formats differ in how deeply they support modulation. The host adapter
must translate gracefully.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
11. POIETEK UNIVERSAL MODULATION SYSTEM
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

This should become a major Poietek differentiator.

Every eligible native parameter can expose a modulation socket.

ModulationSource types:
LFO
Envelope
Step
Curve
Random
Perlin/noise
Sample-and-hold
Envelope follower
Audio band follower
Transient trigger
Pitch follower
MIDI velocity
Aftertouch
MPE pressure/timbre/pitch
Note number
Macro
Sidechain level
Beat/bar phase
Video motion data later
AI control signal only when explicitly enabled

ModulationBinding {
  sourceId
  destinationId
  amount
  polarity
  curve
  smoothing
  range
  transform
  stereoMode
  sampleAccurateRequired
}

UX:
Drag modulation source onto destination.
Destination shows a range halo.
Hover reveals source amounts.
Right-click opens routing matrix.
Rear Rack view displays advanced virtual cables.

This takes the strong general workflow principles of Serum/Vital-style
drag-and-drop modulation and integrates them into the entire Poietek ecosystem.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
12. MODULATION RESOLUTION
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

CONTROL RATE
Suitable for ordinary UI movement.

BLOCK RATE
Efficient for many effects.

SAMPLE ACCURATE
Required for selected timing-sensitive modulation.

AUDIO RATE
Only for native modules and external formats that safely support it.

Do not promise audio-rate modulation of arbitrary legacy hosted parameters.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
13. SHAPE / MOTION MODULATOR
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Inspired by the general capability class represented by ShaperBox, LFOTool and
Tremolator, but built as an original universal Poietek module.

`MotionShaper`

Curve modes:
Bezier
points/tension
freehand
step
Euclidean
generated
audio-derived groove

Clock:
Hz
note division
bars
one-shot
retrigger
free-running
MIDI trigger
audio transient

Targets:
Volume
Pan
Width
Filter
Pitch
Time/read-head
Drive
Bit depth/rate
Delay feedback
Reverb send
Plugin/native parameters
Video/VFX parameters later

Multiband mode:
split input into configurable bands.
Each band can have independent depth/curve.

Sidechain source:
host beat clock
external audio transient
MIDI note
manual.

Preset:
stores curves separate from destination so a shape can be reused elsewhere.

14. NATIVE INSTRUMENT FAMILY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Poietek should not rely on third-party instruments to feel complete.

14.3 ANALOG / CLASSIC MODEL LAB

Investigate SFZ because Serum 2 currently highlights it as an open,
human-readable multisample standard. Do not make SFZ the only internal format.

14.6 DRUM INSTRUMENT

Pads + multilayer sample engine + synthesis layers.
Integrates with the logical pad model and hardware profiles already defined.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
24. PLUGIN CONTAINERS / CHAINS
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Every native/hosted processor can be placed in:

INSERT CHAIN
straight series.

PARALLEL RACK
multiple branches.

MULTIBAND RACK
frequency-split branches.

M/S RACK
mid/side branches.

TRANSIENT/SUSTAIN RACK
analysis-dependent branches later.

STEM RACK
vocals/drums/bass/music branches where a stem representation exists.

MACRO RACK
container with exposed controls.

Save chain as reusable preset.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
25. ROUTING / FEEDBACK SAFETY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Modular routing is powerful but can explode numerically or sonically.

Rules:
detect direct zero-delay feedback loops.
Require explicit feedback node.
Clamp/soft-limit safety path optionally.
Display feedback warning.
Mute on NaN/Inf.
Protect user speakers.

Provide a global Panic command:
stop transport
all notes off
reset feedback
mute plugin processes if necessary.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
26. OVERSAMPLING / QUALITY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Native nonlinear processors expose:
Eco
Normal
High
Render Ultra

Oversampling may be:
2x
4x
8x
16x depending on module.

Real-time mode can differ from offline render quality but must preserve sonic
intent and avoid surprising automation changes.

Project records render quality preset.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
27. MPE / NOTE EXPRESSION / MICROTONALITY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The Vital/modern-synth and CLAP research reinforce first-class expressive control.

Native instruments:
per-note pitch
pressure
timbre
release velocity
poly aftertouch
MPE zones
microtonal tunings

Project tuning:
12-TET default
Scala-compatible scale import
keyboard mapping
custom frequency map later.

Host adapters pass the richest expression supported by each format.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
28. HARDWARE SYNTH INTEGRATION
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The current Omnisphere product demonstrates a strong transferable principle:
a hardware profile can do more than generic MIDI Learn.

Poietek already has a universal device profile system.
Extend it to instrument-aware "Performance Mappings."

A hardware synth/controller profile may define:
physical knob -> logical macro
physical section -> UI page
button -> layer select
encoder -> wavetable position
aftertouch -> modulation source
display feedback where documented
preset category shortcuts.

Crucial:
Poietek profiles must be original mapping definitions created from documented
MIDI/HID behavior or user learning. Do not copy proprietary profile data.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
29. WEB / MOBILE PLUGIN REALITY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Web cannot simply host arbitrary desktop VST3/AU binaries.

Web:
- Poietek native WebAudio/WASM modules.
- future portable community WASM modules.
- no claim of VST3/AU hosting.

iOS/iPadOS:
- native Poietek modules.
- AUv3 where Apple permits.
- no desktop VST3 binaries.

Android:
- native Poietek modules.
- portable Poietek extensions.
- third-party plugin ecosystem only if a robust Android-compatible API strategy
  is explicitly developed.

Desktop:
- VST3.
- CLAP.
- AU on macOS.
- LV2 later where implemented.
- internal Poietek modules.

One project preserves unsupported plugin state via freeze/render fallback.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
30. POIETEK PORTABLE MODULE FORMAT
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Future community format:
PXM — Poietek eXtension Module (working internal name only).

Manifest:
id
version

license
roles
capabilities
audioIO
midiIO
parameters
modulation targets
UI

permissions
minimumHostVersion
signature
checksum

Portable DSP:
WASM where performance permits.

Native high-performance:
signed platform binaries with stricter review.

Do not ship this format until its security/update/version strategy is stable.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
32. PLUGIN LICENSING INVENTORY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Every dependency used to implement Poietek DSP must enter a licence register:

DependencyRecord {
  name
  version
  source
  licence
  linkingMode
  modified
  noticeRequirements
  sourceDisclosureRequirements
  commercialRestrictions
  patentNotes
  approvedBy
}

Do not confuse:
"product can host VST3"
with
"we can copy code from a commercial VST3 plugin."

Hosting a format and reproducing a product's DSP are entirely different legal
questions.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
33. AAX / PRO TOOLS INTEROPERABILITY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Near-term practical interoperability:
- audio/stem export;
- MIDI export;
- AAF/other project interchange where supported;
- rendered tracks;
- future Poietek plug-ins can potentially be built in AAX after Avid agreement.

Do not make "host AAX" a launch promise.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
34. AI SOUND DESIGN
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

AI assistant can:
describe a synth patch
create editable oscillator/filter/modulation commands
suggest modulation
search presets
match a reference characteristic at a high level
create an effect chain
explain why chain is proposed
suggest mastering chain

Example:
"Make this bass wider above 180 Hz but keep sub mono."

AI proposes:
- mid/side split;
- high-band width processor;
- mono low-band constraint.

Every change remains ordinary native parameters/routing.

For third-party plugins:
AI may use exposed parameters but must not assume semantic meaning when parameter
metadata is insufficient.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
35. MODULATION + AUTOMATION + HARDWARE UNIFICATION
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

This is a central Poietek idea:

One parameter can have:
Base value
Automation lane
One or more modulators
MIDI/hardware assignment
Macro mapping
AI proposal
Snapshot value

The inspector shows all contributors to final value.

Example:
Filter Cutoff = 2.2 kHz base
+ automation
+ LFO ± 20%
+ aftertouch +10%
+ macro range
= current effective value.

This avoids mysterious parameter movement.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
39. CPU / MEMORY MANAGEMENT
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Plugin DSP can be the dominant project workload.

Host monitors:
CPU average/peak
audio callback deadline misses
memory
GPU UI load
disk streaming
plugin latency
plugin process health

Per-instance:
sleep when silent if safe
smart bypass
freeze
render in place
reduce oversampling in live mode
disable GUI rendering when closed.

Performance Guard from Document 17 can force a stable low-risk configuration.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
40. PRESET / SAMPLE DEPENDENCY PORTABILITY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

A plug-in state may depend on external:
samples
wavetables
IRs
presets

Poietek cannot legally copy arbitrary third-party content into the project
archive without licence/permission.

Plugin dependency policy:
EXTERNAL
User must have library installed.

EMBED_ALLOWED
User/plugin declares media may be copied.

PROJECT_OWNED
Asset originated from user/project.

Freeze render provides portable audible fallback without illegally repackaging
a vendor's source library.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
43. ACCEPTANCE TESTS — PLUGIN HOST
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

PLG-001 VST3 scan failure does not crash studio.
PLG-002 Valid VST3 instrument receives MIDI and renders audio.
PLG-003 VST3 effect processes stereo audio.
PLG-004 CLAP instrument state saves/reloads.
PLG-005 CLAP note expression maps correctly when supported.
PLG-006 AUv3 loads on supported Apple target.
PLG-007 unsupported format is not advertised.
PLG-008 AAX is not listed as host capability unless actually licensed/implemented.
PLG-009 sidechain routing works.
PLG-010 multi-output instrument exposes outputs.
PLG-011 plugin-reported latency enters PDC.
PLG-012 plugin tail remains audible on offline render.
PLG-013 missing plugin placeholder preserves state.
PLG-014 freeze fallback plays on unsupported device.
PLG-015 unfreeze restores live plugin on capable device.
PLG-016 quarantined plugin can be reopened disabled.
PLG-017 plugin crash does not corrupt project.
PLG-018 project closes without orphan plugin processes.
PLG-019 preset index survives rescan.
PLG-020 plug-in update does not silently discard old state.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
44. ACCEPTANCE TESTS — MODULATION
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

MOD-001 LFO can modulate native filter cutoff.
MOD-002 modulation range is visually displayed.
MOD-003 automation + modulation combine deterministically.
MOD-004 bipolar/unipolar transforms work.
MOD-005 smoothing prevents zippering for control-rate source.
MOD-006 sample-accurate source meets timing tolerance.
MOD-007 one shape can target multiple destinations.
MOD-008 destination can receive multiple sources.
MOD-009 modulation routing saves/reopens.
MOD-010 hardware macro control coexists with modulation.
MOD-011 no NaN/Inf reaches audio graph.
MOD-012 feedback graph is detected or made explicit.
MOD-013 MotionShaper phase-locks to transport.
MOD-014 swing/offset behaves predictably.
MOD-015 multiband modulation recombines without unacceptable artifacts.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
45. ACCEPTANCE TESTS — NATIVE SYNTHS
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

SYNTH-001 oscillator pitch accurate across MIDI range.
SYNTH-002 note-off/release stable.
SYNTH-003 voice stealing deterministic.
SYNTH-004 unison does not exceed defined CPU budget at quality target.
SYNTH-005 wavetable import persists.
SYNTH-006 modulation matrix saves.
SYNTH-007 MPE expression is per note.
SYNTH-008 microtonal scale loads correctly.
SYNTH-009 granular engine handles asset relink.
SYNTH-010 multisample zones map correctly.
SYNTH-011 offline render is repeatable within tolerance.
SYNTH-012 preset mutation never alters factory source preset.
SYNTH-013 hardware mapping changes logical macros, not hardcoded UI pixels.
SYNTH-014 web/native project uses same synth patch schema.
SYNTH-015 quality fallback does not change project data.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
46. ACCEPTANCE TESTS — EFFECTS / MASTERING
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

FX-001 EQ response matches designed coefficients within tolerance.
FX-002 dynamic EQ sidechain triggers correct band.
FX-003 linear-phase mode reports latency.
FX-004 compressor attack/release fixtures pass.
FX-005 saturator oversampling reduces aliasing according to benchmark.
FX-006 delay tempo sync follows tempo map.
FX-007 delay mode transition does not create unsafe spikes.
FX-008 reverb tail survives stop/export as configured.
FX-009 convolution relinks user IR.
FX-010 limiter true peak target verified with oversampled measurement.
FX-011 mastering assistant returns editable chain.
FX-012 assistant reject leaves project untouched.
FX-013 loudness meter meets chosen standard implementation tolerance.
FX-014 A/B comparison supports gain matching.
FX-015 reference track excluded from export.
FX-016 codec preview is disabled when codec unavailable/unlicensed.
FX-017 master dither applied once at final appropriate conversion.
FX-018 stem-aware mastering clearly identifies generated stem dependency.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
47. IMPLEMENTATION ROADMAP
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

PLUGIN PHASE A
Host abstraction, internal native module interface, plugin descriptor/state model.

PLUGIN PHASE B
VST3 scanner/host desktop + crash isolation.

PLUGIN PHASE C
CLAP scanner/host + parameter/note-expression integration.

PLUGIN PHASE D
macOS/iOS AUv3 adapters.

PLUGIN PHASE E
PDC, sidechain, multi-output, preset indexing, freeze.

PLUGIN PHASE F
Universal modulation engine.

PLUGIN PHASE G
Core native mix effects.

PLUGIN PHASE H
Wave Synth + Sampler integration.

PLUGIN PHASE I
MotionShaper + CharacterRack + advanced creative FX.

PLUGIN PHASE J
Mastering Hub + assistant.

PLUGIN PHASE K
Portable PXM/WASM extension research.

PLUGIN PHASE L
LV2 adapter and additional ecosystem support where justified.

AAX:
Do not put into host roadmap unless Avid provides an appropriate authorized path.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
48. CURRENT SDS REPOSITORY MIGRATION
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Current SDS built-in synth/drum/effect demonstrations should become views over
real modules.

Existing `audioEngine`:
- keep as prototype backend only;
- move synthesis algorithms into native module interfaces;
- move mixer DSP into graph nodes;
- replace direct component calls with Command/AudioGraph services.

Existing rack:
- become plugin/native module container.

Existing rear panel:
- show audio, MIDI, modulation and sidechain ports.

Existing mapper:
- map hardware controls to logical parameters/macros.

Existing mixer:
- host plugin/native inserts and sends.

Existing AI:
- manipulate exposed module parameters/commands, not raw component state.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
49. CLEAN-ROOM RULES
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Allowed inspiration:
- published workflow concepts;
- public manuals;
- public specifications;
- open-source standards under their licences;
- independent DSP theory;
- measurements/research conducted lawfully;
- user feedback.

Do not copy:
- proprietary code;
- factory presets;
- wavetables;
- impulse responses;
- protected samples;
- interface artwork;
- product-specific text;
- proprietary hardware model names for Poietek native products;
- undocumented protocols;
- patented algorithm implementations without review/licensing.

"Analog-inspired" is different from claiming an exact model of a named device.

#################### 19_CONNECTED_STORAGE_FABRIC_GIT_FORGES_BYOC.txt ####################

POIETEK / STUDIO DAW STATION (SDS)
MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 1.4 ADDENDUM

19 — CONNECTED STORAGE FABRIC, GIT FORGES & BRING-YOUR-OWN-CLOUD
===============================================================

PURPOSE

Poietek must never assume that every creator owns the same cloud service—or any
cloud account at all.

Storage is a user-controlled capability.

A project may live:
- entirely on the current device;
- on an internal or external drive;
- on a NAS/home server;
- in one cloud provider;
- across several cloud providers;
- in a Git repository for structured/versioned files;
- in object storage for large media;
- in a hybrid of all of the above.

CORE PRODUCT RULE

NO CLOUD IS REQUIRED.

Local creation, editing, saving, recording and export must remain available even
when the user never creates or connects a cloud-storage account.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. STORAGE SETUP EXPERIENCE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

On first use, present:

STORAGE MODE

[ Auto — Recommended ]
Poietek recommends locations based on what is available and connected.

[ Local Only ]
Keep all project data on this device unless I explicitly move or back it up.

[ Choose Locations ]
Set locations separately for projects, media, backups, models, cache and exports.

Auto is a RECOMMENDATION MODE, not permission to upload private work silently.

Before first use of any remote destination:
- show provider;
- show what will be uploaded;
- show visibility;
- show whether team members will have access;
- request explicit confirmation.

After a user approves a policy, Poietek can reuse it as their default.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. DEFAULT RECOMMENDATION LOGIC
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Always available baseline:

PROJECT STATE:
local durable database/project folder.

AUDIO/VIDEO WORKING MEDIA:
fast local storage.

CACHE / WAVEFORMS / PROXIES:
local only by default.

AUTOSAVE:
local.

If a connected provider exists, Poietek may RECOMMEND:

PROJECT BACKUP:
connected cloud drive or user-selected remote target.

BULK AUDIO/VIDEO BACKUP:
provider with large-object/resumable-upload capability.

STRUCTURED SOURCE / DEVICE PROFILES / SCRIPTS:
Git forge, if user intentionally enables it.

AI MODELS:
local model cache or a dedicated model hub/cache target.

IMPORTANT:
"Recommended" is computed from capability, not provider brand.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3. STORAGE LOCATION PICKER
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Every project has a Storage page.

PROJECT ROOT:
[ Browse / Change ]

AUDIO RECORDINGS:
[ Inherit Project ] [ Browse ]

VIDEO:
[ Inherit Project ] [ Browse ]

SAMPLES:
[ Inherit Project ] [ Browse ]

AI MODELS:
[ Application Default ] [ Browse ]

WAVEFORM / RENDER CACHE:
[ Fastest Local ] [ Browse ]

BACKUPS:
[ None ] [ Add Destination ]

FINAL EXPORTS:
[ Inherit Project ] [ Browse ]

LICENCES / CONTRACTS:
[ Inherit Project ] [ Encrypted Location ] [ Browse ]

The user can change locations later.

Poietek performs a safe move/copy operation with verification rather than merely
rewriting file paths.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4. PROVIDER DETECTION
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

NATIVE DESKTOP/MOBILE CLIENT

May detect:
- mounted local volumes;
- removable drives;
- user-accessible mounted cloud folders;
- configured Poietek storage connectors;
- NAS/network mounts exposed by the OS.

WEB CLIENT

Must not pretend it can discover arbitrary applications installed on a device.

Web can know about:
- providers the user connected through OAuth;
- provider connectors enabled in Poietek;
- directories/files explicitly granted through browser APIs where supported;
- browser-local storage.

This distinction must be explicit.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
5. ADD / INSTALL STORAGE PROVIDER
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

If the ideal provider adapter is not available:

Show a non-blocking option:

"Add another storage provider"

Categories:
- Cloud Drive
- Object Storage
- Git / Source Control
- NAS / WebDAV
- Model Hub
- Custom Server

Provider card actions:

[ Install Connector ]
if a Poietek provider module is not installed.

[ Connect Account ]
if connector is installed but authorization is absent.

[ Configure ]
for S3/WebDAV/NAS/custom endpoints.

[ Skip ]
always available.

Core creation must never be blocked because the user refuses to install a
storage connector.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
6. STORAGE CONNECTOR ARCHITECTURE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

StorageProvider interface:

connect()
disconnect()
reauthorize()
getCapabilities()
getQuota()
list()
stat()
upload()
uploadResumable()
download()
downloadRange()
delete()
move()
copy()
createFolder()
watchChanges()
getVersions()
createShareReference()
revokeShareReference()

ProviderCapabilities {
  folders
  versioning
  resumableUpload
  multipartUpload
  rangeRead
  streaming
  shareLinks
  teamAcl
  changeFeed
  contentHashes
  gitSemantics
  modelRegistry
  objectStorage
  webDav
}

Do not hard-code vendor quota/pricing into project files.
Provider limits change and should be queried or maintained as updateable adapter
metadata.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
7. STORAGE CLASSES
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

LOCAL WORKING STORAGE
Best for:
recording, realtime playback, current assets, cache.

PERSONAL CLOUD DRIVE
Best for:
backup, documents, project archives, optional media.

OBJECT STORAGE
Best for:
large immutable WAV/video/stem objects, team media and scalable remote assets.

GIT FORGE
Best for:
structured text, source code, schemas, presets, mappings, scripts, documentation,
issues and change review.

MODEL HUB
Best for:
AI models, model cards, datasets, adapters and evaluation assets.

APPLICATION BACKEND
Best for:
accounts, permissions, comments, collaboration metadata and notifications.

These are different jobs.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
8. INITIAL PROVIDER ADAPTER CANDIDATES
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

GENERAL CLOUD / DRIVE
- Google Drive
- Microsoft OneDrive
- TeraBox
- Dropbox
- Box
- Nextcloud
- generic WebDAV
- other providers after API/security verification

OBJECT STORAGE
- generic S3-compatible endpoint
- AWS S3
- Cloudflare R2
- Backblaze B2
- Wasabi
- Google Cloud Storage
- Azure Blob
- self-hosted MinIO-compatible service
- future Poietek-managed storage

GIT / SOURCE CONTROL
- GitHub
- GitLab
- Bitbucket
- Gitea
- Forgejo
- self-hosted Git

MODEL / DATA HUB
- Hugging Face Hub
- self-hosted model registry
- future compatible registries

LOCAL / NETWORK
- internal disk
- external SSD/HDD
- removable USB storage
- NAS mount
- SMB/NFS exposed by operating system
- user-selected folder

All third-party integrations remain subject to current API terms, quotas and
authentication capabilities.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9. GITHUB / GIT FORGES
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

GitHub and similar services should be first-class Poietek integrations, but
Poietek must use them intelligently.

Excellent Git candidates:
- device profiles;
- MIDI maps;
- schemas;
- themes;
- presets represented as structured text;
- plugin/extension code;
- workflow scripts;
- documentation;
- ADRs;
- project manifests;
- release metadata;
- tutorial project structure;
- issue/feature proposals;
- test fixtures.

Poor default candidates:
- raw multitrack WAV;
- constantly changing video;
- render cache;
- huge proxies.

GitHub documents that Git LFS stores pointer files in the Git repository while
the actual large objects are managed separately, and GitHub imposes plan-based
per-file limits. Therefore Git LFS is an optional advanced bridge, not Poietek's
universal media store.

Poietek's internal versioning remains independent of Git.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
10. OPTIONAL GIT PROJECT BRIDGE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Export a structured Poietek project representation to Git:

/poietek-project
  project.manifest.json
  arrangement.json
  device-profiles/
  mappings/
  presets/
  workflows/
  metadata/
  rights-drafts/
  docs/
  README.md

Large binary assets remain referenced by content hash and storage locator.

This makes an open tutorial or development project inspectable on GitHub without
requiring raw studio masters inside the repository.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
11. HUGGING FACE ROLE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Hugging Face is primarily a model/data/application ecosystem, not merely a generic
cloud disk.

Current official Hub documentation describes:
- model repositories;
- dataset repositories;
- Spaces;
- Git-based versioning;
- Xet-backed large-file handling;
- newer non-versioned Storage Buckets for mutable object storage.

Potential Poietek uses:
- discover optional audio/MIR models;
- host Poietek-compatible open models;
- host model cards/evaluation results;
- publish research datasets when licensing permits;
- test AI demos in Spaces;
- maintain model versions;
- download user-selected models into a local cache.

Never automatically download or execute a model because it exists on a model hub.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
12. STORAGE LOCATOR MODEL
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

An Asset has identity independent of location.

Asset {
  assetId
  contentHash
  mediaType
  metadata
}

AssetReplica {
  replicaId
  assetId
  providerId
  locator
  state
  byteLength
  verifiedHash
  availability
  accessClass
  lastVerified
}

Example:

assetId: vocal_take_04
hash: SHA256...

replicas:
- Local SSD / available
- TeraBox / synced
- External SSD / disconnected

The project references assetId, NOT a single provider URL.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
13. REDUNDANCY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

User-selectable policies:

WORKING
1 local copy.

SAFE
1 local + 1 verified backup.

IMPORTANT
1 local + 2 independent remote/removable copies.

ARCHIVE
2 verified durable copies; local working copy may be evicted only after user
permission and verification.

Poietek shows Storage Health:
Protected
At Risk
Missing
Uploading
Provider Offline
Needs Reauthorization

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
14. ASSET RESOLUTION
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

When opening an asset:

1. Look for verified local replica.
2. Look in local cache.
3. Look at mounted/removable storage.
4. Query preferred connected provider.
5. Query other authorized replicas.
6. If none available, show Missing Asset resolver.

Never replace missing content with a fake sound.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
15. PROVIDER OUTAGE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

If cloud provider becomes unavailable:
- project still opens;
- locally cached media works;
- new recordings remain local;
- sync operations queue;
- user receives a non-destructive warning.

If a remote-only asset is unavailable:
display placeholder and all known replicas.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
16. TEAM ACCESS
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Personal cloud does not automatically equal team storage.

Access classes:

PRIVATE_OWNER
TEAM_SHARED
PUBLIC
APP_MANAGED
EXTERNAL_SHARED_LINK

Before choosing a user's personal Drive/TeraBox file as team media, verify whether
collaborators can actually access it.

If not:
- copy/upload to an approved team destination; OR
- let collaborator provide another replica; OR
- keep project private to owner.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
17. SECURITY / AUTHORIZATION
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Tokens:
- stored in secure native credential storage where available;
- never in project JSON;
- never committed to Git.

Scopes:
request minimum required provider scope.

Disconnecting provider:
revokes or deletes local token according to provider capability.
Does NOT delete user files unless explicitly requested.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
18. ENCRYPTED STORAGE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Optional application-level encryption for selected remote assets.

Encryption metadata:
algorithm
key reference
chunking
nonce/IV data
version

Keys remain separate from provider credentials.

This can allow a user to store encrypted project backups on a generic provider.

Do not implement custom cryptography casually; use mature libraries and security
review.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
19. PROJECT PORTABILITY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

"Export Complete Portable Project"

Options:
- project metadata only;
- project + currently used media;
- project + all versions;
- include proxies;
- include licences/documents;
- exclude third-party copyrighted libraries that cannot legally be repackaged.

A portable archive must not depend on provider login if the chosen assets were
included.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
20. USER DEFAULTS
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Global defaults:
Project Location
Recording Location
Video Location
Sample Library Locations
Cache Location
Model Cache
Backup Policy
Preferred Backup Provider
Preferred Team Provider
Preferred Git Provider
Preferred Model Hub

Project settings can override global defaults.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
21. "AUTO — RECOMMENDED" EXAMPLE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Scenario:
User has:
- local SSD;
- Google Drive connected;
- GitHub connected;
- no TeraBox;
- no object store.

Poietek may recommend:

Working project:
Local SSD.

Recording/video:
Local SSD.

Backup:
Google Drive.

Device profiles/workflow source:
GitHub only if this project/developer feature is explicitly enabled.

Cache:
Local SSD only.

Private audio upload to GitHub:
NOT recommended.

Scenario:
User has only local storage.

Recommended:
Local Only.

Prompt:
"Your project currently has one copy. Add a backup destination?"
[Add Storage] [Not Now]

No nagging during recording.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
22. STORAGE PROVIDER INSTALL UX
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Provider Browser:

Installed
Connected
Available
Community
Custom

Example card:

Nextcloud / WebDAV
Status: Available
Capabilities:
Files, folders, chunked upload, versions

[Install Connector]

After install:
[Connect / Configure Server]

The same architecture can support community-maintained provider adapters, but
they must be permissioned, signed/reviewed where distributed through an official
marketplace, and isolated from unrelated project data.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
23. NEXTCLOUD / WEBDAV
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Nextcloud's official developer documentation exposes WebDAV operations including
file/folder upload, download, move, copy, deletion, version handling and chunked
uploads.

This makes Nextcloud/WebDAV particularly valuable because users can attach:
- a hosted Nextcloud account;
- a self-hosted server;
- some compatible NAS/cloud systems.

A generic WebDAV adapter increases Poietek's independence from consumer-cloud
vendors.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
24. ACCEPTANCE TESTS
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

STO-001 Create project with no account/network/cloud.
STO-002 User chooses custom local project folder.
STO-003 User chooses different recording folder.
STO-004 User chooses external drive.
STO-005 Removing external drive shows missing/offline asset, not corruption.
STO-006 Reconnect resolves matching assets by content hash.
STO-007 Auto mode falls back safely to Local Only.
STO-008 Auto mode never uploads before first remote-storage consent.
STO-009 Connected cloud can be selected as backup.
STO-010 Cloud failure does not block recording.
STO-011 Upload queue resumes.
STO-012 duplicate asset does not upload twice when content hash matches.
STO-013 user can add a new connector after project creation.
STO-014 user can decline connector installation.
STO-015 project continues after connector removal.
STO-016 credentials never appear in project export.
STO-017 personal remote asset is not assumed team-readable.
STO-018 team storage permission is validated.
STO-019 Git export contains structured project state.
STO-020 huge WAV is not committed to ordinary Git by default.
STO-021 Git LFS use is explicit.
STO-022 model-hub assets require model licence/trust validation.
STO-023 complete portable export opens without cloud credentials.
STO-024 changing storage policy safely migrates selected replicas.
STO-025 deletion requires explicit choice between removing a replica and deleting
        the canonical asset.
STO-026 provider quota/status is presented without hardcoded stale assumptions.
STO-027 cache eviction never deletes the sole canonical copy.
STO-028 encrypted remote backup can be restored with authorized key.
STO-029 web client does not claim it detected arbitrary installed desktop apps.
STO-030 native client lists only accessible mounted locations.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
25. VERIFIED REFERENCE SOURCES — AUGUST 2026
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

GitHub Git LFS:
https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/working-with-files/managing-large-files/about-git-large-file-storage

Hugging Face Hub:
https://huggingface.co/docs/hub/en/repositories
https://huggingface.co/docs/hub/index

Nextcloud WebDAV:
https://docs.nextcloud.com/server/stable/developer_manual/client_apis/WebDAV/index.html
