#################### 20_OPEN_RESOURCES_THIRD_PARTY_RESEARCH_FIREBASE_WEB_PROTOTYPE.txt ####################

POIETEK / STUDIO DAW STATION (SDS)
MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 1.4 ADDENDUM

20 — OPEN RESOURCES, THIRD-PARTY RESEARCH & FIRST WEB PROTOTYPE BACKEND
======================================================================

PURPOSE

Poietek should deliberately reuse high-quality public standards, open-source
libraries, open research and model ecosystems rather than reinvent every low-level
component.

At the same time, proprietary/closed products can be studied for published
workflow and capability inspiration.

Nothing enters the production codebase merely because it is available online.

Every dependency/model/resource must pass:
- technical review;
- licence review;
- security review;
- maintenance/health review;
- platform review;
- provenance/data-rights review where applicable.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. RESOURCE CLASSIFICATION
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ADOPT
Licence/architecture are suitable and project approves direct dependency.

EVALUATE
Promising; prototype/benchmark before commitment.

REFERENCE ONLY
Study algorithms/API/workflow, but do not include code/assets.

LICENCE REVIEW REQUIRED
Potentially useful but licence obligations may conflict with project distribution.

VENDOR / COMMERCIAL OPTION
Could be licensed later.

RESEARCH
Paper/prototype not suitable for production yet.

DEPRECATED / ARCHIVED
Do not build critical future architecture around it without a maintained fork.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. OPEN RESOURCE REGISTRY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Create a repository file:

/docs/resources/RESOURCE_REGISTRY.yaml

Entry:

id
name
category
source
version
upstreamStatus
licence
modelLicence?
datasetLicence?
patentNotes?
purpose
classification
securityNotes
platforms
lastReviewed
decision
replacementCandidates

Keep code licence, model licence and training-data licence separate.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3. CURRENT HIGH-VALUE OPEN RESOURCES
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

CROSS PLATFORM

Tauri
Use case:
shared web UI packaged for desktop/mobile, native plugins.
Current repository uses MIT/Apache-2.0 licensing where applicable.
Status: EVALUATE / strong candidate.

COLLABORATION

Yjs
Use case:
CRDT structured collaborative state and local-first ecosystem.
Current project states MIT licensing.
Status: EVALUATE / strong candidate.

SOURCE SEPARATION

Demucs
Use case:
music source-separation research/benchmark.
The Meta repository states MIT for the code but was archived in January 2025.
Pretrained-model rights must be reviewed independently.
Status: REFERENCE / EVALUATE MAINTAINED FORKS OR RETRAINED MODELS.

AUDIO TO MIDI

Spotify Basic Pitch
Use case:
polyphonic-ish audio-to-MIDI/pitch-bend research/prototype.
Official repository states Apache-2.0.
Status: EVALUATE.
Still review model/data dependencies.

MUSIC INFORMATION RETRIEVAL

Essentia
Use case:
music analysis/classification/beat/key/audio descriptors.
Official licensing documentation states AGPLv3 for non-commercial open use with
commercial licensing options; official pretrained models have their own
noncommercial/proprietary licensing conditions.
Status: LICENCE REVIEW / potentially commercial licence.

MEDIA

FFmpeg
Use case:
decode/encode/transcode/media probing.
Build configuration changes licensing obligations.
Status: EVALUATE WITH BUILD-SPECIFIC LICENCE REVIEW.

Timeline interchange:
OpenTimelineIO-style standards/libraries should be evaluated for video timeline
interchange.

PLUGIN STANDARDS:
VST3 / CLAP / AUv3 / LV2 per Document 18.

STORAGE:
Git, WebDAV, S3-compatible protocols per Document 19.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4. GITHUB AS RESEARCH SOURCE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Use GitHub for:
- open-source libraries;
- DSP implementations;
- test fixtures;
- device mapping examples;
- protocol libraries;
- UI frameworks;
- build tools;
- standards implementations;
- plugin SDK examples;
- issue discussions describing edge cases.

Research procedure:
1. record exact repo/commit/tag;
2. inspect licence;
3. inspect activity/maintenance;
4. inspect security advisories;
5. inspect transitive dependencies;
6. classify;
7. prototype behind adapter;
8. add tests;
9. document decision.

Do not paste code from a repository before licence classification.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
5. HUGGING FACE AS MODEL/DATA RESEARCH SOURCE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Hugging Face currently provides versioned repositories for models, datasets and
Spaces and documents Xet-backed large-file handling; it also offers storage
buckets.

Poietek use cases:
- discover source separation models;
- pitch/transcription models;
- speech/music embeddings;
- tagging/classification;
- transcription/captioning;
- visual generation/research;
- publish Poietek-compatible open models;
- benchmark alternatives;
- maintain model cards.

MODEL IMPORT GATE

Before enabling a model:
- model licence;
- code licence;
- dataset/training provenance if known;
- commercial-use permissions;
- derivative restrictions;
- attribution;
- architecture compatibility;
- model size;
- inference memory;
- offline capability;
- latency;
- quality benchmark;
- malicious/custom-code risk.

Never enable "trust remote code" automatically.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
6. CLOSED / PRIVATE PRODUCT INSPIRATION
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Proprietary tools are legitimate sources of high-level product research through:
- public documentation;
- product demos;
- publicly available UI;
- reviews;
- patents as legal/technical research;
- user feedback;
- published APIs;
- documented interoperability.

Examples already covered in the Poietek library:
DAWs
pitch editors
DJ software
synth/effect suites
video/VFX editors
creative platforms

Clean-room rule:
Research team writes capability requirements.
Implementation team works from requirements/open specifications, not copied
private code/assets.

Do not solicit leaked source code, stolen SDKs, cracked products, confidential
documents or bypassed vendor protections.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
7. PATENTS
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Patent publications can inspire research but publication is not permission.

For novel/high-risk implementation areas:
- polyphonic note editing;
- advanced source separation;
- DVS protocols;
- time stretching;
- proprietary hardware transfer;
- ML audio transformations;

create:
PriorArtRecord
PotentialPatentConcern
FTOReviewStatus

Avoid implementing a known active claim solely because its patent text is public.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
8. FIREBASE FIRST-WEB-PROTOTYPE OPTION
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The creator has access to a Firebase hobbyist/no-cost setup and may use it for the
first Poietek Web prototype.

Design Firebase as an adapter, not the canonical backend.

FirebaseBackendAdapter:
Authentication
Firestore
Hosting
App Check
Crashlytics/Performance where desired
Cloud Messaging later

MEDIA STORAGE:
Do NOT require Firebase Cloud Storage for the Spark prototype.

Current Firebase documentation states that as of 3 February 2026 access to Cloud
Storage for Firebase requires the Blaze billing plan, even though no-cost usage
may still exist after upgrading.

Therefore the first no-billing prototype can use:
- local IndexedDB/OPFS for media;
- Firestore for metadata/collaboration prototypes;
- external BYOC storage connectors from Document 19;
- optional Supabase/object storage later.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9. FIREBASE SPARK WEB VERTICAL SLICE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Goal:
Test browser UX, account sync and lightweight collaboration without making
Firebase responsible for the realtime audio engine or large media.

Suggested:

Firebase Hosting:
web UI.

Firebase Authentication:
optional sign-in.

Firestore:
users
project index
team membership
comments
lightweight project sync envelope
presence-ish metadata where cost/behavior is acceptable

Local:
actual project state cache
audio/video media
waveform cache
offline operation

StorageProvider:
Google Drive / OneDrive / TeraBox / WebDAV / user-selected provider for remote
asset replicas.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
10. BACKEND ABSTRACTION
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

BackendServices {
 AuthService
 ProjectDirectoryService
 CollaborationRelay
 NotificationService
 CommunityService
 AnalyticsService
 StorageRegistry
}

Implement adapters:

FirebaseAdapter
SupabaseAdapter
SelfHostedAdapter

This allows a future migration from Firebase without rewriting the DAW.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
11. FIREBASE VS SUPABASE PROTOTYPING
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Firebase strengths:
- quick web/mobile onboarding;
- mature Firebase tooling;
- no-cost Spark usage for many development scenarios;
- Hosting/Auth/Firestore integration.

Supabase strengths:
- relational PostgreSQL foundation;
- SQL;
- open-source/self-host options;
- Realtime/Auth/Storage ecosystem.

Poietek should avoid ideological dependence on either.

Prototype choice may be Firebase because the creator already has an account.
Canonical architecture remains provider-neutral.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
12. OFFLINE FIRST WITH FIREBASE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Do not interpret Firestore offline cache as the entire Poietek offline system.

Poietek needs its own:
LocalProjectRepository
AssetStore
CommandLog
SyncQueue
MigrationManager

Reason:
project media and durable DAW state must remain portable even if the backend
provider changes.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
13. RESOURCE MARKETPLACE / DISCOVERY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Future in-app Resource Browser can search approved sources:

Open Source
Models
Datasets
Device Profiles
Plugins
Presets
Templates
Tutorials

Each result displays:
source
author
licence
version
platform
size
trust
permissions
last update
review status

Installation is always explicit.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
14. CLOSED-RESOURCE FEATURE RESEARCH DATABASE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Maintain:

FeatureReference {
 sourceProduct
 publicSource
 capability
 problemSolved
 userBenefit
 implementationConstraints
 cleanRoomRequirement
 patentReview?
 PoietekDesignDecision
}

This ensures Poietek uses competitors as research rather than becoming a collage
of copied screens.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
15. AI MODEL PROVIDER ABSTRACTION
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ModelSource:
Bundled

Community Registry

id

Model download and model execution are separate permissions.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
16. SECURITY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Never execute downloaded repository/model code inside the main DAW automatically.

Use:
sandbox
WASM where applicable
worker/process isolation
hash validation
signed releases
allowlist/review
network permissions
filesystem permissions

Model files are treated as untrusted external content.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
17. RESOURCE LOCKFILE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Every Poietek release should generate an SBOM/resource lock:

Third-party code
Models
Plugin SDKs
Media codecs
Datasets used in distributed components
Fonts/assets
Build tools

Include versions and licences.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
18. FIREBASE COST GUARD
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

For Spark prototype:
- conservative Firestore writes;
- batch/debounce project metadata;
- do not write mixer meters/playhead every animation frame;
- do not store large waveforms as Firestore documents;
- do not sync PCM/video through Firestore;
- cache locally;
- surface quota errors cleanly.

If quota unavailable:
app remains locally usable.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
19. ACCEPTANCE TESTS
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

RES-001 Every dependency has licence metadata.
RES-002 Model licence stored separately from code licence.
RES-003 archived project warning exists for critical dependency.
RES-004 unreviewed model cannot execute automatically.
RES-005 remote custom code disabled by default.
RES-006 Firebase can be removed without changing project schema.
RES-007 web app opens existing local project while Firebase offline.
RES-008 Firebase quota error does not corrupt project.
RES-009 Firestore is not used for WAV/video binary data.
RES-010 Spark prototype works without Firebase Cloud Storage.
RES-011 user can select BYOC remote media provider.
RES-012 closed-product research has public source reference.
RES-013 no proprietary source/assets enter repo through research notes.
RES-014 licence-incompatible library is marked Reference/Licence Review.
RES-015 SBOM/resource lock generated for release.
RES-016 model hash is checked after download.
RES-017 user can delete cached model without deleting project.
RES-018 AI-disabled app remains usable.
RES-019 backend provider adapter integration tests pass.
RES-020 local-only mode requires no Firebase/Supabase account.

#################### 21_UI_UX_SYSTEM_FRONTEND_ARCHITECTURE.txt ####################

POIETEK / STUDIO DAW STATION (SDS)
MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 1.5 ADDENDUM

21 — UI/UX SYSTEM, FRONTEND ARCHITECTURE & DESIGN STRUCTURE
==========================================================

PURPOSE

This document defines the complete front-end architecture for Poietek/SDS across:
- desktop;
- tablet;
- mobile;
- web;
- touch;
- mouse/keyboard;
- accessibility;
- multiple displays;
- beginner/pro modes.

The UI must remain one coherent product even though it contains many highly
specialized creative workspaces.

The front end is NOT the source of truth for project state.
React renders and edits canonical domain state through typed commands/services.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. FRONTEND PRINCIPLES
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1. DOMAIN FIRST
UI components never own durable project truth.

2. WORKSPACE BASED
Users move through purpose-built workspaces rather than one giant screen.

3. PROGRESSIVE DISCLOSURE
Advanced functionality exists without overwhelming beginners.

4. DEVICE RESPONSIVE
Phone, tablet, desktop and web render the same project with different layouts.

5. COMMAND DRIVEN
Buttons, shortcuts, MIDI controls, AI and menus invoke the same command layer.

6. ACCESSIBLE
Every core function has non-pointer access.

7. NON-DESTRUCTIVE
UI always exposes undo/history and version implications.

8. HONEST CAPABILITIES
Do not display controls for unavailable platform features as though they work.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. FRONTEND LAYERS
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Layer A — App Shell
Routing, top-level layout, account/session, platform capability context.

Layer B — Workspace Manager
Docking, tabs, windows, panels, splits, multi-monitor.

Layer C — Design System
Typography, spacing, controls, meters, waveform colors, focus, themes.

Layer D — Feature Workspaces
Arrangement, Mixer, Rack, Sampler, MIDI, Video, DJ, Rights, Community, etc.

Layer E — Inspectors
Contextual property editing.

Layer F — Command / Interaction Layer
Menus, shortcuts, gestures, MIDI, context commands.

Layer G — View Models
Derive UI-specific state from canonical project/domain state.

Layer H — Platform Adapters
Native dialogs, file pickers, windowing, share sheets, device permission UI.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3. RECOMMENDED FRONTEND MONOREPO STRUCTURE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

/apps
  /studio-web
    /src
      main.tsx
      app.tsx
      routes/
      service-worker/
  /studio-native
    /src
      main.tsx
      shell/
      native-bridge/

/packages
  /ui
    /components
    /primitives
    /icons
    /forms
    /menus
    /dialogs
    /tooltips
    /meters
    /waveform
    /timeline
  /design-system
    /tokens
    /themes
    /typography
    /motion
    /accessibility
  /workspace
    /layout
    /docking
    /floating
    /windows
    /workspace-presets
  /feature-arrangement
  /feature-sampler
  /feature-piano-roll
  /feature-score
  /feature-mixer
  /feature-rack
  /feature-hardware
  /feature-dj
  /feature-video
  /feature-ai
  /feature-rights
  /feature-community
  /feature-storage
  /feature-settings
  /feature-learning
  /frontend-state
  /commands-client
  /platform-capabilities

RULE:
Feature packages can depend on domain contracts.
They must not reach directly into backend/vendor SDKs.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4. APP SHELL
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

AppShell owns:
- selected project;
- top-level navigation;
- workspace layout;
- notifications;
- command palette;
- global modals;
- account/session indicator;
- sync indicator;
- storage health;
- platform capability banner;
- update status.

AppShell does NOT own:
- tracks;
- clips;
- MIDI notes;
- mixer parameters;
- rights metadata;
- collaboration truth.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
5. WORKSPACE MANAGER
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Workspace types:
ARRANGE
MIX
RACK
SAMPLE
STEMS
MIDI
SCORE
PITCH
VIDEO
VFX
DJ
HARDWARE
MAPPING
COMMUNITY
RIGHTS
PUBLISH
LEARN

Each workspace describes:
id
title
icon
minimumSize
preferredAspect
phoneMode
tabletMode
desktopMode
supportsDetach
supportsMultiInstance
capabilityRequirements

WorkspaceLayout {
  panels[]
  splitTree
  activePanel
  detachedWindows[]
  displayAssignments[]
}

Layouts can be saved:
Songwriting
Beatmaking
Mixing
Mastering
Video
DJ Live
Learning
Custom

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
6. DESKTOP UX
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Top menu.
Toolbar.
Left browser.
Central workspace.
Right inspector.
Bottom transport/status.

All regions resizable/collapsible.

Desktop-specific:
- right click;
- hover;
- keyboard shortcuts;
- multi-window;
- multi-display;
- detachable mixer/rack/piano roll;
- command palette;
- drag/drop from OS.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
7. TABLET UX
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Tablet emphasizes:
- touch;
- split workspace;
- floating compact inspector;
- large pads/faders;
- Apple Pencil/stylus support where available;
- gesture zoom/pan;
- docked transport.

Avoid simply shrinking desktop UI.

Typical tablet:
Left: browser/pads.
Right: arrangement/editor.
Bottom: transport.
Inspector as slide-over.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
8. PHONE UX
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

One primary task at a time.

Bottom navigation:
Create
Mix
Perform
Library
Project

Context drawer:
Inspector.

Persistent mini transport.

Phone workflows:
- capture recording;
- quick sample;
- chop;
- drum programming;
- simple arrangement;
- review/comments;
- collaboration;
- mixer;
- quick export;
- DJ compact mode.

Complex graph/routing remains accessible through focused editors rather than
trying to display the entire desktop rack at once.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9. RESPONSIVE BREAKPOINT LOGIC
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Do not rely only on pixel width.

Use:
viewport
pointer type
hover capability
touch
display density
orientation
safe areas
window count
platform capability

Example:
A 13-inch touch Windows device can use a hybrid layout.
An iPad with keyboard/trackpad can expose additional desktop-style interactions.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
10. DESIGN TOKENS
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Tokens:
spacing
radius
border
surface
elevation
text
accent
warning
error
success
focus
waveform
MIDI note
clip type
automation
meter zones
routing cable types

Never hard-code random colors across components.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
11. THEMES
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Built in:
Dark Studio
Light Studio
High Contrast
OLED
Low-light Stage

User/community:
token-based only.

Theme cannot:
run script
alter permissions
hide warnings
fake sync/security state.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
12. INPUT SYSTEM
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Unified input mapping:

Keyboard
Mouse
Trackpad
Touch
Stylus
MIDI
HID
Game controller later
Voice command
Accessibility switch control

All invoke commands.

Example:
PlayCommand
can be invoked by:
Space bar
transport UI
MIDI CC
hardware transport
touch button
AI command

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
13. KEYBOARD SHORTCUT SERVICE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ShortcutBinding {
  commandId
  keyChord
  context
  platform
}

Contexts:
Global
Arrangement
Mixer
Piano Roll
Sample
Video
DJ

Allow custom bindings and import/export.

Conflict resolver required.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
14. COMMAND PALETTE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Search:
commands
workspaces
settings
tracks
devices
assets
collaborators
help

Examples:
"Split clip"
"Open mastering"
"Reconnect missing audio"
"Invite collaborator"
"Change buffer"
"Show vocal stem"

AI natural-language interpretation can call the same command registry.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
15. MENUS
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Desktop menus:

File
Edit
View
Project
Transport
Audio
MIDI
Sampler
Sequence
Arrange
Mix
Video
Effects
Hardware
Collaborate
Rights
Publish
Community
Window
Help

Menu item descriptor:
commandId
label
shortcut
enabledWhen
visibleWhen
submenu
helpId

Menus must be generated from command registry, not duplicated hand-written logic.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
16. CONTEXTUAL INSPECTOR
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Inspector adapts to selection.

Track:
name
routing
mixer
input
contributors

Audio clip:
start/end
gain
fade
warp
pitch
asset info
rights

MIDI note:
pitch
velocity
duration
expression

Plugin:
parameters
automation
modulation

Video:
transform
speed
color
effects

Contributor:
role
assignment
credit status

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
17. FRONTEND STATE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Separate:

DOMAIN STATE
Canonical project.

SESSION STATE
Current unsaved UI operation, playback, recording session.

UI STATE
Selected objects, open panels, zoom, scroll.

PRESENCE STATE
Remote users/cursors.

DEVICE STATE
Connected MIDI/audio/controllers.

SYNC STATE
offline, queued, syncing, conflict.

Do not put all of these in one global store.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
18. RECOMMENDED FRONTEND STORE SPLIT
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ProjectReadModel
Canonical-derived.

UIWorkspaceStore
Local user preference.

SelectionStore
Ephemeral.

TransportViewStore
Fast playback display.

DeviceViewStore
Connected hardware status.

PresenceStore
Ephemeral remote collaboration.

NotificationStore
Toast/activity.

ModalStore
Transient.

Avoid triggering full React project rerenders at audio-rate.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
19. VIEW MODEL PATTERN
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Example:

MixerChannelViewModel {
  id
  name
  meterDisplay
  faderValue
  pan
  mute
  solo
  insertLabels
  sends
  automationState
  hardwareBinding
}

Meters are fed from realtime telemetry, not written into canonical project state.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
20. REACT PERFORMANCE RULES
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Do not update the whole component tree at 60/120 Hz.

Use:
fine-grained subscriptions
canvas/WebGL for dense waveforms
off-main-thread analysis
requestAnimationFrame for visual meters
shared memory/ring buffers where platform architecture permits
virtualization for huge track/plugin lists

Audio engine never depends on React render timing.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
21. TIMELINE UI ARCHITECTURE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Timeline layers:
ruler
tempo/signature
markers
tracks
clips
automation
comments
playhead
selection
drag previews

Use one coordinate transform service:
time <-> pixels
ticks <-> pixels
frames <-> pixels

Avoid feature components implementing separate timeline maths.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
22. WAVEFORM RENDERING
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

WaveformPeakCache provides multi-resolution peak data.

Frontend requests level based on zoom.

Very zoomed out:
summary peaks.

Zoomed in:
higher-resolution peaks.

Sample editor:
raw sample-level view only when necessary.

Large sessions must not decode full WAV repeatedly.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
23. CANVAS / GPU
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Use DOM for:
forms
menus
buttons
accessibility structure.

Use Canvas/WebGL/WebGPU where appropriate for:
waveforms
spectrogram
meters
large piano roll
video scopes
dense node graphs.

Always retain accessible alternate controls.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
24. DRAG / DROP
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Drag types:
asset
clip
track
plugin
preset
rack module
device mapping
modulation source
contributor
storage destination

Typed drag payloads.

Drop target checks:
compatibility
permissions
project state
platform capability.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
25. TOUCH / GESTURES
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Pinch:
zoom.

Two-finger pan:
timeline.

Long press:
context menu.

Drag handle:
precise clip move.

Fine mode:
temporary precision multiplier.

Never make destructive actions dependent on hidden gestures only.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
26. ACCESSIBILITY ARCHITECTURE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Every control:
label
role
value
state
description
keyboard behavior.

Audio-specific accessibility:
spoken meter values on request
keyboard parameter entry
MIDI controller navigation
track navigation commands
focusable timeline items
non-color state indicators

Video:
captions/transcripts.

Motion:
reduced-motion preference.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
27. LEARNING UX
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

HelpDescriptor {
  id
  short
  explanation
  example
  tutorialId
  documentationRef
}

Tooltip:
one sentence.

Learn More:
full explanation.

Show Me:
highlights control and demonstrates safely.

Tutorial can create a disposable branch/fork.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
28. ONBOARDING
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Step 1:
Choose experience:
New
Some experience
Professional

Step 2:
Audio/MIDI setup optional.

Step 3:
Storage:
Auto
Local
Custom

Step 4:
Optional accounts/providers.

Step 5:
Choose first goal:
Make beat
Record song
Mix
DJ
Video
Learn

No forced account before local creation.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
29. EMPTY STATES
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Example Sample workspace:
"No sample loaded."
[Import] [Record] [Use Demo]

Mixer:
"No tracks."
[Add Track]

Collaboration:
"Project is local only."
[Invite / Enable Team] [Stay Local]

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
30. ERROR UX
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Errors include:
human-readable summary
technical detail expandable
retry
repair
report
safe fallback

Examples:
Missing asset
Plugin failed
Cloud unavailable
MIDI disconnected
Audio device lost
Database migration failed

Never silently substitute fake data.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
31. FRONTEND SECURITY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

No raw OAuth secrets in UI state.
Sanitize community HTML/markdown.
CSP for web.
Safe URL handling.
No arbitrary extension UI script without sandbox.
Confirm external links when required.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
32. FRONTEND TESTING
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Unit:
formatters
view models
command predicates.

Component:
controls
inspectors
dialogs.

Accessibility:
keyboard
ARIA
focus.

Visual regression:
core workspaces.

E2E:
open project
edit clip
save
offline
reopen
switch layout
connect device
invite collaborator.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
33. CURRENT SDS REPO MIGRATION
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Current:
src/components/daw
src/components/workspaces
src/components/rack
src/components/mapper

Future:
packages/feature-*
packages/ui
packages/workspace

Current App.tsx:
becomes AppShell.

Current MasterState:
split into domain/transport/session stores.

Current workspace switching:
becomes WorkspaceManager.

Current cascading menu:
retain visual concept; rewrite against CommandRegistry.

Current floating windows:
retain; move into workspace package.

Current Tailwind:
can remain initially, but design tokens should become centralized.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
34. FRONTEND ACCEPTANCE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

FE-001 same command works menu/shortcut/hardware.
FE-002 no canonical project object lives only in component local state.
FE-003 phone can create/open/save project.
FE-004 tablet split layout usable.
FE-005 desktop multi-panel persists.
FE-006 theme cannot hide warnings.
FE-007 selection changes do not modify project.
FE-008 playback meters do not flood project sync.
FE-009 timeline maintains consistent coordinate transform.
FE-010 accessibility tree exposes transport/mixer.
FE-011 offline UI remains fully navigable.
FE-012 missing backend does not prevent local project page.
FE-013 detached workspace uses same project state.
FE-014 workspace layout can differ by device.
FE-015 help content works without network when bundled/cached.

#################### 22_BACKEND_SERVICE_API_DATA_ARCHITECTURE.txt ####################

POIETEK / STUDIO DAW STATION (SDS)
MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 1.5 ADDENDUM

22 — BACKEND, SERVICE LAYER, API & DATA ARCHITECTURE
====================================================

PURPOSE

This document defines the backend architecture that supports:
accounts, teams, sync, collaboration, storage connectors, community, rights,
publishing, notifications, search and AI jobs.

Critical distinction:

THE BACKEND IS NOT THE AUDIO ENGINE.

Realtime creative playback/recording lives on the client/native engine.
Backend services support persistence, synchronization and network features.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. BACKEND PRINCIPLES
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1. LOCAL FIRST
Backend outage does not stop local project work.

2. PROVIDER ABSTRACTED
Firebase/Supabase/self-hosted are adapters.

3. SERVICE BOUNDARIES
Do not make one giant API server.

4. EVENT DRIVEN WHERE USEFUL
Uploads/transcodes/notifications/search indexing happen asynchronously.

5. IDEMPOTENT
Retrying a sync/upload operation should not duplicate state.

6. AUDITABLE
Permissions, release approvals, rights changes and monetary operations logged.

7. LEAST PRIVILEGE
Services only access required data.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. BACKEND SERVICE MAP
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

IdentityService
AccountService
TeamService
ProjectDirectoryService
ProjectAccessService
SyncRelayService
CollaborationService
AssetMetadataService
StorageConnectorService
UploadSessionService
MediaJobService
NotificationService
MessagingService
CommunityService
SearchService
RightsService
ReleaseService
MarketplaceService
PaymentLedgerService
AIJobService
TelemetryService
ModerationService
AdminService

Not all required for first release.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3. FIRST WEB MVP BACKEND
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Required:
Identity
Project directory
Project membership
Sync metadata
Comments
Basic presence
Storage connector registry
Notifications minimal

Optional:
Firebase adapter.

Not required initially:
Marketplace
Payments
Public feed
Distribution
Heavy media processing
Blockchain

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4. SERVICE CONTRACTS
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Each service has:
interface
request DTO
response DTO
errors
auth policy
audit policy
events
rate limits
version

UI never calls provider SDK directly if a service abstraction exists.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
5. API GATEWAY / EDGE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Responsibilities:
authentication
request ID
version routing
rate limit
CORS
CSRF where relevant
input size
observability

Do not put business logic entirely in gateway.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
6. REST / RPC / WEBSOCKET
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

HTTP:
accounts
projects
teams
rights
storage config
release metadata.

Realtime:
WebSocket/WebRTC/provider realtime for:
presence
comments
structured collaboration relay.

Bulk media:
direct provider/object-store upload sessions, not API server buffering.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
7. API VERSIONING
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

/api/v1/

Breaking changes:
new version.

Nonbreaking:
add optional fields.

Client sends:
appVersion
schemaVersion
platform.

Server can reject dangerously incompatible writes but should preserve local data.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
8. IDENTITY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

User {
 id
 displayName
 email?
 createdAt
 profile
}

External identities:
Firebase
Supabase
Google
Microsoft
Apple
GitHub
etc.

Canonical internal user ID remains provider-independent.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9. AUTH
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Support:
guest/local-only
registered account
OAuth
passkey later
MFA later

Backend tokens:
short-lived.

Refresh:
secure mechanism.

Native:
secure credential storage.

Web:
secure cookies or provider-recommended secure token pattern.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
10. TEAM / ACCESS MODEL
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Team
ProjectMembership
Role
Permission

Permissions:
project.read
project.edit
asset.download
asset.upload
comment
invite
manage_members
rights.edit
release.approve
publish
billing.manage

Never trust frontend role labels.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
11. PROJECT DIRECTORY SERVICE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Stores project discovery metadata, not full media.

ProjectDirectoryEntry {
 id
 title
 owner
 team
 modifiedAt
 thumbnailRef?
 syncRevision
 schemaVersion
 visibility
 storageHealthSummary
}

Local project can exist without directory entry.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
12. SYNC RELAY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Structured updates:
receive
validate auth
persist update/log
broadcast to authorized peers

Large assets:
reference AssetReplica and UploadSession.

Sync relay does not parse PCM.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
13. COLLABORATION
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Presence:
ephemeral.

Comments:
persistent.

CRDT updates:
persistent enough for convergence/recovery.

Named snapshots:
persistent.

Activity:
append-only user-facing record.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
14. ASSET METADATA
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Canonical server metadata:
asset ID
hash
size
type
owner/team
replica references
availability
licence/provenance metadata

Actual bytes may live elsewhere.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
15. STORAGE CONNECTOR SERVICE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Stores connector metadata and encrypted authorization references.

Provider credentials never exposed to collaborators.

Connector operations can be delegated to:
client
backend worker
provider direct upload

depending on provider/API.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
16. UPLOAD SESSION
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

UploadSession {
 id
 assetId
 provider
 uploadLocator
 parts
 bytesUploaded
 checksum
 expiresAt
 state
}

Resumable.
Idempotent.
Recoverable.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
17. MEDIA JOBS
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Async jobs:
transcode
proxy
waveform
thumbnail
stem separation
AI analysis
loudness
preview

Job {
 id
 type
 inputs
 outputAssetIds
 state
 progress
 logs
 capability
 costClass
}

Local execution can satisfy same job contract.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
18. JOB SCHEDULER
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Queue priorities:
interactive
background
maintenance

Per-user/team quotas.

Cancelable where safe.

No cloud job required if local worker can run it.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
19. NOTIFICATIONS
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Events:
collaborator invite
comment
mention
sync conflict
release approval
asset missing
storage quota
marketplace later

Delivery:
in-app
push
email optional

User preferences required.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
20. MESSAGING
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Project chat
direct message
group

Message {
 id
 thread
 author
 body
 attachments
 created
 edited
 moderationState
}

Attachments use Asset references.

Do not embed giant base64 files in messages.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
21. COMMUNITY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Separate public social data from private studio data.

Community DB:
posts
profiles
follows
reactions
public comments
public media refs

Private Project DB:
members
assets
comments
rights

Never accidentally join private records into public feed.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
22. SEARCH
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Search domains:
projects
assets
people
community
plugins
device profiles
help

Local search:
works offline.

Server search:
indexes authorized remote/public content.

Permission filters happen before results are returned.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
23. RIGHTS SERVICE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Stores:
contributors
roles
entities
agreements refs
split proposals
approvals
release validation

Changes audited.

No automatic legal determination.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
24. RELEASE SERVICE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Quick Export remains local.

ReleaseService applies only to commercial/publish workflow.

Checks:
metadata
credits
rights declarations
split approval
licences
artwork
destination requirements.

Connectors to distributors/platforms later.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
25. PAYMENT / LEDGER
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Future only.

Never calculate balances with floating-point currency.

Use integer minor units / decimal types.

Immutable ledger entries.

External payment processor handles cards.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
26. AI BACKEND
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

AIProviderGateway:
routes to provider/local.

Cloud requests:
permission
data classification
cost
provider terms
redaction where possible

Store AI operation metadata separately from provider secrets.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
27. FIREBASE ADAPTER
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Possible first web prototype:

Firebase Auth -> IdentityAdapter
Firestore -> ProjectDirectory/comments/light sync metadata
Hosting -> web deployment

Do not let Firebase document shape become domain model.

Adapter maps:
Canonical DTO <-> Firestore document.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
28. SUPABASE ADAPTER
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Possible alternative:
Auth
Postgres
Realtime
Storage

Again:
Postgres tables map to domain DTO.
Project schema not equal DB schema.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
29. SELF-HOSTED BACKEND
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Future:
Postgres
Redis-compatible cache
object storage
WebSocket relay
job workers
search

Can support studios/enterprise/education.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
30. DATABASE SPLIT
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Relational:
users
teams
permissions
rights
releases
billing.

CRDT/log:
project collaborative structured updates.

Object storage:
media.

Search index:
derived.

Cache:
ephemeral.

Do not put everything into one database technology.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
31. POSTGRES CONCEPTUAL SCHEMA
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

users
teams
team_members
projects
project_members
project_snapshots
assets
asset_replicas
comments
threads
messages
contributors
rights_entities
split_proposals
split_approvals
releases
storage_connectors
notifications
audit_events

Later:
community_posts
follows
marketplace_items
orders
ledger_entries

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
32. AUDIT LOG
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

AuditEvent {
 id
 actor
 action
 objectType
 objectId
 timestamp
 sourceIp? privacy-sensitive
 deviceId?
 metadata
}

Audit required:
permissions
rights
release
billing
moderation.

Creative edit history can use separate project activity stream.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
33. EVENT BUS
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Domain events:
ProjectCreated
MemberInvited
AssetUploaded
AssetAvailable
CommentAdded
ReleaseSubmitted
SplitApproved
StorageQuotaLow

Consumers:
notification
search
analytics
jobs.

Use at-least-once semantics with idempotent consumers.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
34. BACKGROUND WORKERS
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Worker pools:
media
AI
notifications
search
maintenance

Workers must not share mutable in-process state with API.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
35. CACHE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Use cache for:
sessions
rate limits
public metadata
expensive search facets

Never treat cache as canonical rights/ledger storage.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
36. RATE LIMITS
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Per:
IP
account
team
endpoint
job type

Separate live collaboration limits from heavy job limits.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
37. SECURITY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Input validation.
Prepared queries/ORM safety.
Authz on every object.
SSRF protection for storage connectors.
Webhook verification.
Secret rotation.
Audit.
Dependency scanning.
Encryption.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
38. WEBHOOKS
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Future provider webhooks:
storage changes
payments
distribution
GitHub
model hub

Verify signatures.
Deduplicate event IDs.
Queue processing.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
39. OBSERVABILITY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Metrics:
request latency
errors
sync lag
job queue
upload success
realtime connections
DB health
provider errors

Tracing:
request ID across gateway/service/worker.

Logs:
structured.

No project media in logs.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
40. BACKEND TESTS
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

BE-001 unauthorized project cannot be read.
BE-002 team role enforced.
BE-003 duplicate upload request idempotent.
BE-004 sync reconnect recovers.
BE-005 provider credential never returned to peer.
BE-006 audit event created for rights approval.
BE-007 comment permission enforced.
BE-008 private project not indexed publicly.
BE-009 job retry does not duplicate output asset.
BE-010 backend outage does not corrupt local client.
BE-011 Firebase adapter can be swapped in test.
BE-012 Supabase adapter can satisfy same contract.
BE-013 storage webhook replay deduplicated.
BE-014 API version compatibility tested.
BE-015 asset hash mismatch rejected.
BE-016 release cannot silently change approved splits.
BE-017 public post cannot expose private asset URL.
BE-018 message attachment permission checked.
BE-019 rate limit does not affect local engine.
BE-020 logs contain no provider tokens.

END DOCUMENT 22

23 — COMPLETE SYSTEM ARCHITECTURE, RUNTIME TOPOLOGY & REPOSITORY STRUCTURE
=========================================================================

PURPOSE

This document shows how every major Poietek subsystem fits together.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. SYSTEM CONTEXT
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

USER
  |
  v
POIETEK CLIENT
  |
  +-- Local Project Repository
  +-- Audio/MIDI/Video Engine
  +-- Hardware Layer
  +-- Plugin Host
  +-- Local AI
  +-- Storage Router
  |
  +---- optional network ----+
                            |
                            v
                     BACKEND SERVICES
                            |
       +--------------------+----------------------+
       |                    |                      |
       v                    v                      v
 Collaboration        Identity/Teams        Community/Rights
       |
       v
 Remote Storage / Provider Connectors
       |
       +-- Drive Providers
       +-- Object Storage
       +-- Git Forges
       +-- Model Hubs

Core project operation never requires network path.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. LAYERED ARCHITECTURE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

L0 Platform
OS, browser, device APIs.

L1 Native/Runtime Adapters
filesystem, audio, MIDI, windows, keychain.

L2 Core Engines
audio, timeline, MIDI, video, plugin, AI.

L3 Domain
project, assets, tracks, clips, rights, members.

L4 Application Services
commands, undo, import/export, sync, storage.

L5 UI
workspaces, inspectors, menus.

L6 Network Services
identity, collaboration, community.

L7 External Providers
cloud, Git, model hubs, publishing.

Dependencies should point inward.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3. HEXAGONAL / PORTS & ADAPTERS PRINCIPLE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Domain defines ports.

Adapters implement:
Firebase
Supabase
IndexedDB
SQLite
WebAudio
NativeAudio
WebMIDI
NativeMIDI
Google Drive
TeraBox
GitHub
Hugging Face
etc.

Business logic never imports vendor SDK directly.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
5. PACKAGE DEPENDENCY RULES
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

domain:
depends on nothing vendor-specific.

commands:
depends on domain.

project:
depends on domain/commands.

feature UI:
depends on domain contracts and app services.

connectors:
depend on interfaces, never reverse.

native engines:
implement engine contracts.

server:
depends on domain DTOs, not React.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
6. DOMAIN MODULES
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Project
Asset
Track
Clip
Timeline
Mixer
Device
Mapping
Plugin
Member
Contribution
Rights
Release
Community reference
Storage policy

Each module has:
entities
value objects
commands
events
validation
serialization.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
7. COMMAND BUS
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

UI/HARDWARE/AI
     |
     v
CommandBus
     |
     +-> validate
     +-> execute
     +-> update state
     +-> undo record
     +-> activity
     +-> sync operation

One mutation path.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
8. QUERY / READ MODEL
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

UI should not mutate objects directly.

Query examples:
getTracks()
getMixerChannels()
getSelectedClip()
getStorageHealth()
getContributorStatus()

Use optimized read models.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9. REALTIME AUDIO THREAD BOUNDARY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Realtime thread:
audio graph
DSP
sample scheduling
meters output.

NOT ALLOWED:
network
database
React
JSON serialization
filesystem blocking
AI inference unless specifically realtime-safe
plugin scanning

Control thread sends lock-free/bounded commands.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
10. TRANSPORT
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

One canonical transport clock.

Consumers:
audio
MIDI
automation
video
DJ
lighting
visuals

No workspace-specific setInterval clocks.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
11. ASSET PIPELINE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Import
-> hash
-> metadata
-> local store
-> waveform
-> analysis
-> project reference
-> optional backup/sync

Derived assets:
proxy
stem
preview
freeze
thumbnail

Each maintains lineage.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
12. PROJECT OPEN
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1 read manifest
2 migrate schema
3 open local DB
4 resolve assets
5 restore graph
6 create missing plugin placeholders
7 restore UI layout
8 start engine
9 optionally connect sync

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
13. PROJECT SAVE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Commands already persist incrementally.

"Save" creates/marks checkpoint/snapshot.

Avoid one giant blocking save.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
14. CRASH RECOVERY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Journal latest transactions.
On restart:
detect unclean shutdown
offer recovered state
validate asset writes
reopen plugins safely disabled if suspected crash.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
15. NETWORK DISCONNECT
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Sync service moves to queued mode.
No domain change.
No transport interruption.
No modal blocking.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
16. STORAGE DISCONNECT
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

If local replica exists:
continue.

If remote-only:
show unavailable placeholder.

Recording never targets a disappearing remote network path directly unless
explicitly configured and validated; default staging is local.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
17. PLUGIN PROCESS TOPOLOGY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Main UI process
  |
  +-> Audio Engine
        |
        +-> Plugin Host Process Pool
              +-> Plugin A
              +-> Plugin B

Shared memory for audio.
IPC for parameters/state/UI.

Crashes isolated.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
18. VIDEO TOPOLOGY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Decode worker
GPU compositor
timeline engine
proxy/cache
render worker.

Audio sync driven by canonical timebase.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
19. AI TOPOLOGY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

AI Orchestrator
  |
  +-> Local Model Runtime
  +-> Cloud Provider
  +-> Rule/DSP Analyzer

Returns structured commands/results.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
20. HARDWARE TOPOLOGY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Physical Device
-> Platform Adapter
-> Device Profile
-> Logical Mapping
-> Command/Engine Target

Output feedback reverses:
State
-> logical feedback
-> profile translation
-> physical device.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
21. SYNC TOPOLOGY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Local Command Log
-> Sync Queue
-> CRDT/Operation Provider
-> Remote Relay
-> Other Client
-> Merge
-> Local Domain

Assets:
Local Asset
-> Storage Router
-> Replica upload
-> AssetReplica metadata
-> Other client fetch/cache.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------

25. SERVER DEPLOYMENT
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Start modular monolith if small team.

microservices.

Split services only when:

justify it.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Recommended early server:

  identity
  teams
  projects
  collaboration
  storage
  rights
  notifications

One DB.
One deployment.

Workers separate.

Later:
split community/media/search/payments.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Do NOT start with 30 network services because diagram looks impressive.

Audio product complexity is already high.

Keep boundaries in code first.

42. SYSTEM ACCEPTANCE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

SYS-001 domain imports no Firebase/Supabase SDK.
SYS-002 UI imports no raw database SDK.
SYS-003 audio thread performs no network I/O.
SYS-004 command bus is canonical mutation path.
SYS-005 one transport clock drives all time domains.
SYS-006 provider adapter can be swapped.
SYS-007 server can run without community module.
SYS-008 local app can run without server.
SYS-009 plugin crash isolated.
SYS-010 migrations tested with fixtures.
SYS-011 app can recover unclean shutdown.
SYS-012 storage connector passes common contract suite.
SYS-013 event schemas versioned.
SYS-014 client/server errors use stable codes.
SYS-015 performance budgets exist and are measured.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
44. V1.5 MASTER SYSTEM RULE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Any new feature must answer all six:

1. Where is its canonical data?
2. Which service owns it?
3. How does it work offline?
4. How is it persisted/migrated?
5. How is it exposed in UI?
6. What happens when dependency/provider/device is unavailable?

If any answer is missing, architecture is incomplete.

#################### 25_BRAND_NAMING_PRODUCT_TAXONOMY_IP_CLEARANCE.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 1.6 ADDENDUM

25 — BRAND, NAMING, PRODUCT TAXONOMY & IP CLEARANCE
===================================================

STATUS
Working brand recommendation: POIETEK.
Legacy prototype/codename: Studio DAW Station (SDS).

IMPORTANT
This document is product/brand architecture, not legal trademark clearance.
Before public launch, conduct professional trade mark/domain/company-name
clearance in intended territories and relevant Nice classes.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. WHY SDS SHOULD BECOME A CODENAME
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

"Studio DAW Station" is understandable but:
- long;
- generic/descriptive;
- narrowly implies a DAW;
- under-represents video, AI, DJ, collaboration, publishing and community;
- is less memorable as a platform brand.

Use SDS only where helpful for migration/history:
"Poietek — formerly SDS prototype."

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. POITEK VS POIETEK
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Preliminary public research found existing "Poitek Technology" usage and a
poitek.com business presence. Therefore "Poitek" should NOT be treated as
cleared.

"Poietek" is more distinctive in preliminary public web searches and preserves
the intended poiesis/techne-inspired origin more clearly.

No web search substitutes for trade mark clearance.

RECOMMENDED WORKING SPELLING:
POIETEK

Possible pronunciation guide for internal branding work:
"poy-eh-tek" or "poi-eh-tek".
Choose one official pronunciation before launch.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3. MASTER BRAND
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

POIETEK

Meaning narrative:
Creative making + craft + technology.

Brand promise:
One creative environment from idea to finished work.

Do not make the public tagline overly technical.

Possible positioning:
"Create without changing worlds."
"From idea to release."
"Your creative system."
"Make. Shape. Perform. Publish."

Taglines require separate clearance/research before public adoption.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4. PRODUCT FAMILY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

POIETEK STUDIO
Main creative application:
audio, MIDI, sampler, video, AI, collaboration.

POIETEK LIVE
DJ/live performance/show-control mode or product surface.

POIETEK BRIDGE
Hardware/device connectivity, mapping and optional bridge utilities.

POIETEK HUB
Creator community, collaboration/discovery and marketplace.

POIETEK LEARN
Tutorials, courses, forkable demos and education.

POIETEK CLOUD
Optional managed sync/storage/compute services.
Never imply cloud is required.

POIETEK SDK
Developer extension ecosystem.

POIETEK MOBILE
If a separate store listing is needed, though product should remain one
cross-platform project ecosystem.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------------
6. REPOSITORY RENAMING STRATEGY
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Do NOT mass-rename everything before codebase stabilization.

Stage 1:
README identifies Poietek as working product name.

Stage 2:
package names become @poietek/*.

Stage 3:
user-visible "Studio DAW Suite/SDS" strings migrate.

Stage 4:
repository may be renamed after redirects/remotes are planned.

Stage 5:
bundle IDs/application IDs chosen only after final brand clearance.

Maintain migration map:
old identifier -> new identifier.

Never change serialized project identifiers merely for cosmetic branding.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------

------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Check:
.com
.app
.studio

.music
.dev
social handles
GitHub organization
package registries
app stores

A domain being available does not mean trademark clearance.
A trademark being available does not mean domain availability.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Professional counsel should identify final classes, but likely categories may
include:

Do not file blindly across classes without actual intended use.

9. BRAND DESIGN SYSTEM
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

must work at 16px app icon and large format.

distinct from product UI typography if useful.

simple silhouette.

short, low-distraction identity.

optional later; avoid making startup audio mandatory.

logo must remain legible in dark environment.

------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Clear.
Creative.

Not corporate jargon-heavy.
Not patronizing.
Never imply AI replaces creators.

#################### 31_PROJECT_SCHEMA_V1.txt ####################

POIETEK IMPLEMENTATION BATCH — VERSION 1.7

31 — PROJECT SCHEMA v1
======================

STATUS: BUILD CONTRACT
Companion machine-readable file: project.schema.v1.json

CORE DECISIONS
- ULID-style durable IDs.
- schemaVersion = 1.0.0.
- project data contains references, not live AudioBuffer/MIDIPort/DOM objects.
- SHA-256 content identity for binary assets.
- asset replicas are separate from canonical asset identity.
- plugin state may have a frozen-render fallback.
- project settings include PPQ and storage policy.
- extension data is namespaced for forward compatibility.

MIGRATION RULES
1. Never mutate an old project in place without recovery copy.
2. v1 readers ignore unknown extension namespaces but retain them.
3. Future v2 migrations are explicit functions with fixtures.
4. Removing fields requires migration plus compatibility policy.
5. Every project write validates against schema in development/test.

NORMALIZED VS EMBEDDED
The JSON archive is intentionally self-describing enough to move between clients.
Runtime databases may normalize rows/documents for performance.

BINARY RULE
Audio/video/model/plugin-state bytes are stored in AssetStore. Project JSON stores
asset IDs and content hashes only.

FIRST FIXTURE REQUIREMENTS
- blank project
- one-audio-track project
- MIDI project
- missing-asset project
- plugin-missing project
- collaboration project
- old-schema migration fixture

DEFINITION OF DONE
A project created on Web can serialize, reload, validate, and open in a native
test client without vendor-specific fields becoming mandatory.

#################### 32_API_CONTRACT_V1.txt ####################

POIETEK IMPLEMENTATION BATCH — VERSION 1.7

32 — API CONTRACT v1
====================

Companion machine-readable file: openapi.v1.yaml

SCOPE
This API is for network services only. Local audio/MIDI/timeline operations are
not HTTP APIs.

RULES
- /api/v1 version boundary.
- bearer identity abstracted from Firebase/Supabase.
- project directory != entire project file.
- CRDT sync update endpoint carries structured update bytes.
- asset bytes upload through provider upload sessions, not API server buffering.
- rights/release approvals use authenticated endpoints and audit.
- all mutating operations need idempotency support in implementation.

NEXT
Generate TypeScript/Rust clients from the contract after route semantics stabilize.

#################### 33_DATABASE_SCHEMA_V1.txt ####################

POIETEK IMPLEMENTATION BATCH — VERSION 1.7

33 — DATABASE SCHEMA v1
=======================

Companion file: database.v1.sql

DESIGN
- Postgres is the canonical relational reference for a future provider-neutral backend.
- Firebase prototype may map the same contracts to Firestore.
- Binary media is never stored in relational tables.
- CRDT update bytes may be retained in an update log and compacted into snapshots.
- asset_replicas allow multiple remote/local locations per canonical asset.
- rights approvals and audit events are separate from casual project history.

FIREBASE MAPPING
users/{userId}
projects/{projectId}
projects/{projectId}/members/{userId}
projects/{projectId}/comments/{commentId}
projects/{projectId}/syncUpdates/{updateId}
assets/{assetId}
assets/{assetId}/replicas/{replicaId}

Do not write meters/playhead/PCM to Firestore.

RLS/SECURITY
Production Postgres/Supabase implementation must add row-level or service-level
authorization policies using project membership and explicit permissions.

#################### 34_SYNC_CRDT_PROTOCOL_V1.txt ####################

POIETEK IMPLEMENTATION BATCH — VERSION 1.7

34 — SYNC / CRDT PROTOCOL v1
============================

GOAL
Two clients may edit structured project state offline and later converge without
putting large binary media into the CRDT.

IDENTIFIERS
projectId: ULID
clientId: random stable installation ID
actorId: account ID or local anonymous principal
updateId: ULID
assetId: ULID
contentHash: sha256:...

DOCUMENT PARTITION

ProjectCoreDoc
- title
- tempo/signature/key maps
- track ordering
- lightweight track metadata
- bus structure
- project member display references
- storage policy refs

TrackDoc:{trackId}
Audio track:
- clip references/timing/gain/fades/operations
MIDI track:
- clips/notes/controllers
Video track:
- clip references/transforms/effects metadata

CommentsDoc
- comments
- anchors
- resolved state

RightsDraftDoc
- contributor assignments
- draft metadata
- proposed splits
NOTE: approvals are server-audited records, not CRDT last-write-wins.

Workspace state is per-user/local by default and NOT in project CRDT.

PRESENCE
Ephemeral awareness channel:
user
device
workspace
selection
cursor
playhead
typing
lastSeen
Never persisted as project truth.

BINARY ASSETS
CRDT stores:
assetId
contentHash
duration/metadata
availability state
Never WAV/video bytes.

ASSET SYNC
1. command references local assetId.
2. local AssetStore hashes media.
3. SyncQueue advertises hash.
4. server/provider checks existing replica.
5. client creates resumable upload session if needed.
6. upload completes and checksum verifies.
7. AssetReplica metadata syncs.
8. other clients fetch on demand according to cache policy.

UPDATE ENVELOPE

{
  "protocolVersion": "1",
  "projectId": "...",
  "clientId": "...",
  "updateId": "...",
  "actorId": "...",
  "docId": "track:...",
  "baseCheckpoint": "...",
  "createdAt": "...",
  "encoding": "yjs-v1",
  "payload": "<binary>"
}

IDEMPOTENCY
(projectId, clientId, updateId) unique.

LOCAL TRANSACTION
User command
-> Domain validation
-> local DB transaction
-> CRDT transaction where collaborative
-> append local sync queue
-> UI success
Network happens later.

CONFLICT TYPES

CRDT-MERGEABLE
- MIDI note changes
- clip positions
- names
- comments

HUMAN-REVIEW
- split percentages
- release approval
- destructive asset replacement
- project ownership

BINARY CONFLICT
Never merge PCM bytes.
Keep both asset versions and update clip/reference explicitly.

DELETE
Use tombstone semantics in collaborative structured state.
Physical media garbage collection only after:
- no project reference;
- no snapshot retention requirement;
- no pending sync;
- retention grace period;
- explicit user policy.

CHECKPOINTING
Server/local compactor can create:
Checkpoint {
  revision
  stateVector
  compactedUpdateAsset/ref
  createdAt
}

Clients older than checkpoint fetch checkpoint + newer updates.

OFFLINE
No network:
- all commands commit locally.
- queue displays Pending.
- assets remain local.
Reconnect:
- exchange state vectors;
- send missing updates;
- upload/fetch assets;
- recompute Storage Health.

PERMISSION CHANGE
If user loses edit permission while offline:
- preserve their local branch.
- server rejects unauthorized pushes.
- offer Save As Local Copy / Fork where policy allows.
Never delete local work.

SCHEMA MIGRATION
CRDT document version is separate from Project schema version.
Migration must occur before joining a collaborative document version that cannot
be interpreted safely.

FIREBASE PROTOTYPE
Firestore can store:
- project directory
- compact update records/chunks within safe document limits
- comments/member metadata
But do not design around unlimited Firestore update documents.
Prototype may use a provider-specific relay, then move to dedicated sync service.

YJS PROVIDER
Yjs is the first candidate, not a permanent public file-format dependency.
Wrap it behind CollaborativeDocument interface.

ACCEPTANCE
CRDT-001 A and B edit different tracks offline; converge.
CRDT-002 A/B edit same MIDI clip different notes; converge.
CRDT-003 duplicate update ignored.
CRDT-004 network drops mid-update; recovery.
CRDT-005 asset upload interrupted; resumes.
CRDT-006 binary conflict preserves both.
CRDT-007 rejected unauthorized sync preserves local work.
CRDT-008 comment anchors survive clip move.
CRDT-009 checkpoint + updates reconstruct same state.
CRDT-010 delete tombstone converges.
CRDT-011 rights split conflict requires approval.
CRDT-012 project remains open with remote media unavailable.

#################### 35_AUDIO_ENGINE_DETAILED_DESIGN_V1.txt ####################

POIETEK IMPLEMENTATION BATCH — VERSION 1.7

35 — AUDIO ENGINE DETAILED TECHNICAL DESIGN v1
===============================================

FIRST TARGET
A reliable real-audio vertical slice, not a complete Pro Tools competitor.

THREAD / PROCESS MODEL

UI THREAD
- React
- commands
- views

CONTROL THREAD / SERVICE
- graph mutation
- asset decode requests
- scheduling lookahead
- automation preparation

REALTIME AUDIO THREAD
- fixed-size callback
- reads immutable/prepared graph state
- no network
- no blocking filesystem
- no JSON
- no React
- no plugin scan
- bounded allocation policy

WORKERS
- waveform peaks
- hash
- analysis
- offline render
- transcode

AUDIO BACKEND CONTRACT

interface AudioBackend {
  open(config)
  close()
  start()
  stop()
  getDeviceInfo()
  getClock()
  submitGraphMutation(...)
  schedule(...)
  getMeters(...)
  renderOffline(...)
}

WEB V1
WebAudio AudioContext.
AudioWorklet for custom scheduler/DSP when required.
Decoded AudioBuffer exists only in runtime cache.

NATIVE LATER
Rust/C++/platform backend behind same contract.

CLOCK
Canonical sample clock.
Project musical position derived from:
sample position -> seconds -> tempo map -> ticks/bars.

PPQ default: 960.

SCHEDULING
Use lookahead scheduling from audio clock.
Never setInterval as timing authority.

For Web V1:
- UI/control timer may wake scheduler.
- events scheduled against AudioContext.currentTime sufficiently ahead.
- scheduler window dynamically sized.
- underrun telemetry.

GRAPH V1

Master
  <- Track buses
Track
  SourceNode
  ClipGain
  TrackGain
  Pan
  Inserts later
  Sends later

V1 minimum:
multiple audio clips
mute
solo
gain
pan
master gain

CLIP PLAYBACK
Clip defines:
assetId
timeline start/duration
source offset/duration
gain
fades

At playback:
resolve assetId -> decoded runtime buffer
calculate overlap with transport
create/schedule source
apply gain/fade
connect graph.

SEEK
Stop scheduled sources.
Increment transport generation.
Rebuild schedule from new position.
Old generation callbacks ignored.

LOOP
Transport loop is authoritative.
Scheduler splits events crossing boundary.

MUTE/SOLO
Calculate audible set before scheduling/mixing.
Do not merely hide UI.

GAIN
Store dB canonical.
Convert:
linear = 10^(dB/20)

PAN
V1 equal-power stereo pan where backend supports.

FADES
Sample/time-based gain ramps.
Prevent clicks.
Fade handles persist in project.

IMPORT
File picker
-> stream/read
-> SHA-256
-> copy/reference into local AssetStore
-> metadata decode
-> waveform worker
-> project Asset
-> AudioClip.

ASSET STORE WEB
IndexedDB for metadata.
OPFS preferred for large local media when available.
Fallback Blob/IndexedDB with capability warning.

RUNTIME AUDIO CACHE
LRU decoded buffers.
Key: contentHash + decode settings.
Never serialized.

WAVEFORM
Generate multi-resolution peak pyramids:
min/max per block.
Store derived cache keyed by source hash/version.

RECORDING V2 OF ENGINE
Input backend
-> ring buffer / MediaStream strategy on web
-> local staging
-> finalize asset
-> hash
-> clip.
Recording must not depend on remote upload.

OFFLINE RENDER
V1 can use OfflineAudioContext for supported native WebAudio graph.
Render same canonical graph.
Third-party/native plugin host later gets its own render adapter.

ERRORS
ASSET_MISSING
DECODE_FAILED
DEVICE_LOST
SCHEDULER_UNDERRUN
RENDER_FAILED
UNSUPPORTED_FORMAT

DEVICE LOSS
Pause/mute safely.
Preserve project.
Offer new output.

METERS
Realtime thread writes compact peak/RMS telemetry.
UI samples at animation rate.
Meters never enter project/sync state.

PERFORMANCE BUDGET INITIAL PLACEHOLDERS
These are engineering targets to benchmark, not public promises:
- no avoidable audio underruns in 30-minute V1 fixture.
- UI actions must not block callback.
- waveform generation off main thread.
- project with 16 stereo clips opens from local store without decoding all clips at once.

TEST SIGNALS
sine 1kHz
impulse
silence
pink noise
stereo phase fixture
known fade fixture.

UNIT TESTS
dB conversion
tempo/tick conversion
clip overlap
fade curves
solo/mute set
loop scheduling.

INTEGRATION
two clips overlap
seek
loop
save/reopen
missing file
decode failure
offline render.

MIGRATION FROM CURRENT src/audio/engine.ts
1. freeze existing implementation as PrototypeWebAudioBackend.
2. define AudioBackend interface.
3. move AudioContext ownership into adapter.
4. remove SamplePad AudioBuffer serialization.
5. add AssetRuntimeCache.
6. route MPC/sample views through commands.
7. replace setInterval sequencers with transport scheduler.

NOT IN V1
VST
time stretch
pitch correction
comping
buses/sends
multichannel
DVS
stem separation.

Those follow only after core transport/import/save/render is stable.
