#################### 112_RECORDING_INPUT_PREFLIGHT_GAIN_STAGING.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.5

112 — RECORDING INPUT PREFLIGHT & GAIN STAGING
==============================================

BEFORE RECORD

Poietek can run "Check My Level".

1 Select input.
2 Identify source type.
3 Performer plays/sings expected loudest section.
4 Observe for sufficient time.
5 Measure peaks/noise/dynamics.
6 Recommend hardware/input change.
7 Retest.
8 Arm recording.

IMPORTANT

A digital fader cannot undo clipping that already occurred in the analogue
preamp/converter.

When clipping originates before Poietek:
recommend lowering hardware/console preamp/input gain.

POIETEK CONSERVATIVE CAPTURE DEFAULT

A configurable engineering starting profile can prefer observed peaks around or
below -12 dBFS, advise caution as observed peaks approach -6 dBFS, and issue a
critical headroom warning near -1 dBFS.

These are Poietek defaults, NOT universal recording standards.

The engine adapts after measuring:
crest factor
noise floor
source consistency
converter/device behavior.

24-bit workflows normally allow healthy headroom; recording unnecessarily close
to 0 dBFS is not a quality goal.

REMOTE CONSOLE

If a verified digital desk exposes remote preamp gain:
Poietek can propose:
"Reduce CH 7 preamp by 4 dB".

Default:
[Preview] [Apply] [Ignore].

Phantom power and large preamp changes require safety permissions.

END DOCUMENT 112

#################### 113_LOUDNESS_TRUE_PEAK_STANDARDS_ENGINE.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.5

113 — LOUDNESS, TRUE-PEAK & STANDARDS ENGINE
============================================

CURRENT STANDARDS BASIS — 11 AUGUST 2026

ITU-R BS.1770-5 is the current in-force ITU recommendation for algorithms that
measure programme loudness and true-peak audio level.

EBU R128 v5 uses programme loudness plus Loudness Range and Maximum True Peak
descriptors and recommends -23 LUFS for its broadcast programme context.

EBU mode includes:
Momentary: 400 ms
Short Term: 3 s
Integrated: start-to-stop programme measurement.

IMPLEMENTATION RULE

Poietek MUST NOT:

take RMS
rename it LUFS

or:

take ordinary sample peak
rename it True Peak.

StandardsLoudnessAnalyzer is a separate tested engine.

VALIDATION

Use authoritative test material such as the EBU loudness test set and appropriate
standards-conformance tests.

MEASUREMENTS

LUFS-I
LUFS-S
LUFS-M
LRA
dBTP.

UI

Peak meter
True Peak meter
Momentary
Short-term
Integrated
Loudness range.

END DOCUMENT 113

#################### 114_AUDIO_FAULT_QUALITY_DETECTION.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.5

114 — AUDIO FAULT & QUALITY DETECTION
=====================================

CHECKS

HARD CLIPPING
samples at/over representable digital limit.

NEAR CLIPPING
frequent peaks too close to ceiling.

TRUE-PEAK RISK
inter-sample peak risk from standards analyzer.

DC OFFSET
non-zero mean.

POLARITY / PHASE
negative correlation
channel polarity anomalies
mono compatibility.

NOISE
broadband noise floor
hiss
room/air-conditioning
preamp noise.

HUM
50/60 Hz family plus harmonics.
Region-aware but detect rather than assume.

DROPOUT
unexpected digital silence/discontinuity during active programme.

CLICK/POP
transient anomaly detection.

SIBILANCE
spectral/context detector.

PLOSIVE
low-frequency transient detector.

RUMBLE
low-frequency persistent energy.

HARSHNESS / RESONANCE
advisory spectral features, not universal artistic faults.

STEREO
left/right imbalance
excessive anti-correlation
unexpected mono channel.

SPECTRAL

AI can compare spectral features against:
the same project's reference tracks
user-chosen reference
source-class ranges.

Never say:
"Correct EQ is X".

Say:
"Compared with your reference, 2–5 kHz is 4 dB more prominent."

END DOCUMENT 114

#################### 115_AI_RECOMMENDATION_SAFETY_AUTOFIX.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.5

115 — AI RECOMMENDATIONS, SAFETY & AUTO-FIX
===========================================

AI ROLE

Translate measurements into useful production advice.

EXAMPLE

Instead of:
"True Peak: -0.1 dBTP."

Assistant:
"Your master is very close to full scale. Spotify's current guidance asks for
more true-peak margin; preview a limiter ceiling change before export."

ACTION MODES

ADVICE ONLY
No mutation.

PREVIEWABLE COMMAND
Build non-destructive project command.

MANUAL HARDWARE ACTION
"Lower microphone preamp."

REQUIRES MORE MEASUREMENT
"Play the loudest chorus first."

NO SILENT MASTERING

AI may not automatically:
change preamp
enable phantom
insert compressor
EQ vocal
limit master
normalize
change sample rate

without policy/user approval.

AUTO MODE

User may enable bounded helpers:
Prevent digital master overload.
Record safety track.
Auto input trim on verified digital preamp.
Delivery conformity pass.

Every automatic action:
bounded
logged
undoable when technically possible
visible.

#################### 117_DELIVERY_LOUDNESS_PLATFORM_PROFILE_REGISTRY.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.5

117 — DELIVERY LOUDNESS / PLATFORM PROFILE REGISTRY
===================================================

COMPANION
profiles/audio-target-profiles.json

DO NOT SERIALIZE INTERNET PLATFORM TARGETS AS PERMANENT PROJECT LAW.

Services can change playback normalization behavior.

Profile Registry:
id
provider/context
version/date
loudness behavior
true-peak recommendation
source reference
verification.

CURRENT EXAMPLE

Spotify's artist guidance currently states:
Normal playback normalization: -14 LUFS.
Recommended master True Peak: below -1 dBTP.
For masters louder than -14 LUFS, guidance recommends below -2 dBTP to reduce
additional encoding distortion.

This is a DELIVERY PREVIEW, not a rule that every artistic master must be exactly
-14 LUFS.

APPLE

Apple Digital Masters emphasizes delivering high-quality source masters and
provides mastering/audition tools that can detect peaks/clipping after AAC
encoding.

Poietek should invoke/replicate only documented compatible checks and should not
invent an Apple loudness target where Apple has not stated one.

BROADCAST

EBU R128 profile.

FUTURE PROFILES

podcast
film
cinema
broadcast networks
social video
game assets
user/custom.

#################### 121_TECHNICAL_STANDARDS_VS_STYLE_NORMS_ARCHITECTURE.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.6

121 — TECHNICAL STANDARDS VS STYLE NORMS
========================================

CORE DISTINCTION

Not every genre has a formal industry standard.

Formal standards can define:
colour spaces
HDR transfer functions
loudness measurement
broadcast targets
cinema packaging
caption formats
timecode
interchange
delivery codecs/containers.

Genres/styles usually define tendencies, conventions and audience expectations.

Examples:
drill
jazz
classical
ambient
Afrobeats
metal
film score
documentary
horror
anime
music video.

These are not one fixed LUFS/EQ/colour rule.

POIETEK THEREFORE HAS TWO REGISTRIES.

STANDARDS REGISTRY

Formal/versioned requirements.

STYLE REFERENCE REGISTRY

Adaptive reference distributions.

AI must label each recommendation:

REQUIRED BY TARGET
TECHNICAL BEST PRACTICE
REFERENCE NORM
CREATIVE OPTION.

Never use:
"industry standard"
for a subjective genre tendency.

END DOCUMENT 121

#################### 122_MUSIC_GENRE_TAXONOMY_REFERENCE_PROFILES.txt ####################

packages/content-intelligence/src/GenreTaxonomyProvider.ts

of its tag system.

Poietek can use:

COPYRIGHT

reference audio without permission.

GENRE MIX

AI compares against more than one profile.

#################### 123_AI_MUSIC_STYLE_MIX_MASTER_ASSISTANT.txt ####################

kick/bass interaction
spectral masking
stereo width
crest factor
dynamic density
reverb time
transient balance.

MASTER ASSIST

Formal delivery requirements remain separate.

The style assistant may say:
"Your master is more dynamic than the selected contemporary references."

The delivery assistant may separately say:
"Your true peak fails the chosen platform profile."

CREATIVE INTENT LOCK

User may lock:
do not reduce distortion
keep vocal dry
preserve sub weight
do not widen master
retain cassette noise.

AI must respect these locks.

END DOCUMENT 123

CREATIVE

Tempo/style           Within reference
Sub-bass              Heavy — intentional lock
Vocal level           Slightly lower than references
Visual contrast       Cinematic/high

TECHNICAL

Audio clipping        Pass
True peak             Pass
Web export            Pass
BT.709 tags            Pass
Broadcast loudness    Needs adjustment
Captions              Missing
Rights manifest       Pass

RECOMMENDATIONS

1 Required:
Create/review captions for broadcast target.

2 Required:
Preview broadcast loudness conformity render.

3 Creative:
Vocal sits below your reference set.
[Preview +0.8 dB]
[Keep]

4 Creative:
No change to sub-bass because "Keep heavy sub" is locked.

measured fact

#################### 132_TUNING_REFERENCE_AND_MUSICAL_PITCH_STANDARDS.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.7

132 — TUNING REFERENCE & MUSICAL PITCH STANDARDS
=================================================

COMPANION
schemas/tuning-reference.schema.v1.json

ISO 16

ISO 16:1975 specifies standard musical pitch with A on the treble stave at
440 Hz and states a 0.5 Hz tuning accuracy. ISO lists this edition as current
after confirmation in 2022.

POIETEK PROFILE

ISO A440:
A4 = 440 Hz.

USES

instrument tuner
session-wide reference
sample/key detection
pitch editor
hardware synth tuning
orchestral/session preparation
stem mismatch detection.

IMPORTANT

A=440 Hz is a tuning reference.
It is NOT a universal radio-broadcast technical requirement.

ALTERNATIVES

Historical practice.
Ensemble-specific reference.
Creative/custom tunings.
Microtonal systems.

Poietek stores tuning reference as explicit project/session metadata.

AI CAN WARN

"Vocal stem is approximately 18 cents below the session's A440 reference."

It should not retune automatically unless user approves.

END DOCUMENT 132

#################### 133_RADIO_TV_BROADCAST_RELEASE_PROFILES.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.7

133 — RADIO / TV BROADCAST RELEASE PROFILES
===========================================

RADIO/BROADCAST READINESS MAY INCLUDE

loudness
true peak
sample/format requirements
duration
channel layout
metadata
identifiers
rights
file naming/package
station/network-specific rules.

EBU R128

Current EBU R128 v5 recommends programme loudness of -23 LUFS and uses Loudness
Range and Maximum True Peak descriptors for its broadcast ecosystem.

ITU-R BS.1770-5

Current in-force ITU recommendation defines the measurement algorithms for
programme loudness and true-peak audio level.

ATSC A/85:2026

Current ATSC A/85 revision was approved 8 July 2026 and covers audio loudness
practice for digital television and contemporary broadcast/streaming workflows.

POIETEK RULE

No single profile called "Radio Standard".

Instead:
BBC/UK profile
EBU regional profile
specific broadcaster profile
US/ATSC-oriented profile
station/client profile
etc.

Profiles are updateable and cite their source/version.

END DOCUMENT 133

EVM networks
Bitcoin evidence formats
other reviewed ledgers.

CHAIN SWITCH

Future evidence can use another network.
Old receipts remain verifiable against their original network.

NO DEFAULT CROSS-CHAIN BRIDGE

Moving tokens/funds between chains is not required for creative interoperability
and introduces materially higher security risk.

Keep payment-bridge functionality separate and disabled by default.

#################### 143_ORGANIC_ALTERNATIVE_TUNING_ARCHITECTURE.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.8

143 — ORGANIC & ALTERNATIVE TUNING ARCHITECTURE
===============================================

GOAL

Make tuning a first-class creative system.

Poietek can offer:

Standard
Historical
Organic/Harmonic
Microtonal
Custom.

"Organic" is a user-facing creative category, not a scientific health claim.

A REFERENCE PITCH

A4=432 Hz means:
the note A4 is assigned 432 Hz.

The remainder of the tuning follows from the temperament/mapping.

Do not confuse:
reference pitch
temperament
frequency content of a finished song.

CORE OBJECTS

TuningProfile
SessionTuningMap
TrackOverride
InstrumentTuningCapability
AudioRetuneOperation.

PROJECT PORTABILITY

Every project records:
reference note
reference Hz
temperament
mapping
profile/version.

No hidden global tuning preference.

#################### 144_A432_ALTERNATIVE_REFERENCE_POLICY.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.8

144 — A432 & ALTERNATIVE REFERENCE-PITCH POLICY
===============================================

A432

Poietek supports:
A4 = 432 Hz
with 12-TET or another selected temperament.

UI PRESET:

Organic / Alternative
A432.

SCIENCE LABEL

Do not advertise:
healing frequency
universal natural frequency
biological resonance
superior health effect

as established fact.

Small human studies have reported some differences between 432- and 440-Hz
listening conditions, but the evidence is limited/mixed and does not establish
A432 as a universal health standard.

Therefore:

Creative preference:
YES.

Research mode:
YES.

Medical claim:
NO without appropriate validated evidence/regulatory basis.

COMPARE MODE

[Listen A440]
[Listen A432]
[Level Match]
[Blind A/B]
[Choose]

Level matching matters because louder playback can bias preference.

END DOCUMENT 144

#################### 145_HARMONIC_SERIES_JUST_INTONATION.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.8

145 — HARMONIC SERIES & JUST INTONATION
=======================================

This is the strongest technical meaning for an "organic harmonic" mode.

Natural acoustic resonators generate harmonic/partial relationships.

Just intonation uses intervals represented by simple frequency ratios.

Examples:
Unison       1/1
Octave       2/1
Perfect fifth 3/2
Perfect fourth 4/3
Major third  5/4
Minor third  6/5.

Poietek can:

show ratios
show cents difference from 12-TET
retune synth/MIDI notes
generate reference tones
analyse chord beating
preview adaptive tuning.

LIMITATION

Fixed just intonation is key/context dependent.

A ratio ideal for one harmony can become awkward after modulation.

ADAPTIVE JUST

Detect chord/context.
Choose bounded rational offsets.
Protect melodic continuity.
Avoid sudden pitch jumps.
Keep user-set maximum retune cents.

This becomes a Poietek creative engine, not a universal correctness rule.

END DOCUMENT 145

#################### 146_HISTORICAL_MICROTONAL_TEMPERAMENTS.txt ####################

IMPORTANT

A historical pitch reference and historical temperament are separate controls.

A415 + 12-TET
and
A415 + meantone
are different systems.

#################### 147_MIDI_SCALA_MICROTUNING_INTEROPERABILITY.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.8

147 — MIDI, SCALA & MICROTUNING INTEROPERABILITY
================================================

CURRENT MIDI ECOSYSTEM

MIDI Tuning Standard provides fine tuning through MIDI System Exclusive messages.

MIDI 2.0 preserves the MIDI Tuning Standard and adds per-note pitch/tuning
mechanisms.

Poietek should support multiple delivery methods because device/plugin support is
not universal.

ORDER OF PREFERENCE

MIDI 2.0 per-note pitch
MIDI Tuning Standard
host/native tuning API
Scala SCL/KBM
MPE/per-channel pitch-bend fallback
render/freeze audio.

SCALA

Support both:
SCL scale
KBM keyboard mapping.

Do not claim complete Scala compatibility if KBM mapping is ignored.

CAPABILITY NEGOTIATION

Each synth/device profile declares:
MTS
MIDI 2
MPE
Scala
host API
none.

Poietek never assumes a plugin obeys microtuning merely because MIDI reaches it.

END DOCUMENT 147

#################### 148_AUDIO_RETUNING_AND_SAMPLE_INTEGRITY.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.8

148 — AUDIO RETUNING & SAMPLE INTEGRITY
=======================================

A project can contain audio recorded or sampled at different references.

EXAMPLE

Instrument stem:
A440.

Session:
A432.

Poietek can detect approximate stable pitched content and report the reference
difference.

USER OPTIONS

Keep original.
Pitch-shift preview.
Render tuned copy.
Change project reference.
Mark source as intentionally detuned.

NON-DESTRUCTIVE

Original asset remains unchanged.

AudioClip stores tuning/pitch operation.

FORMANTS

For vocals/instruments, pitch shift can optionally preserve formants where the
selected algorithm supports it.

PERCUSSION

Do not force harmonic retuning onto unpitched/percussive sources.

AI asks when classification confidence is low.

END DOCUMENT 148

#################### 149_TUNING_HEALTH_AI_ASSISTANT.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.8

149 — TUNING HEALTH & AI ASSISTANT
==================================

DETERMINISTIC ANALYSIS

fundamental/pitch estimate

AI EXPLAINS.

EXAMPLE

Piano stem:
approximately +31.8 cents relative to current reference.

Recommendation:
"This stem appears closer to an A440 reference than the project's A432 setting."

ENSEMBLE

"Strings are centered near A442 while project is A440."

Never auto-retune acoustic musician monitoring without user/engineer permission.

#################### 150_TUNING_AUTOMATION_AND_REGIONAL_MAPS.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.8

150 — TUNING AUTOMATION & REGIONAL MAPS
=======================================

COMPANION
schemas/session-tuning-map.schema.v1.json

Most projects use one tuning.

Advanced projects can change tuning by region.

Examples:

Intro:
A432 / just.

Verse:
A432 / 12-TET.

Bridge:
19-EDO.

Outro:
adaptive just.

MIDI instruments can follow region maps when supported.

AUDIO

Existing recordings do not automatically retune when crossing a map boundary.
Audio operation must be explicit.

LIVE

Region tuning changes must be preloaded and validated for connected hardware.

NO SURPRISE PITCH JUMPS

Live Mode can lock tuning profile against accidental edits.

END DOCUMENT 150

151 — ORGANIC MODE FOR SOUND DESIGN
===================================

"ORGANIC" SHOULD INCLUDE MORE THAN A432.

Harmonic-series oscillator ratios.

Resonance-aware synthesis.

RULE

Organic is aesthetic vocabulary.

Example:

Organic Pitch:
A432 / Just Intonation.

Organic Timing:
Humanized ±8 ms.

Organic Drift:
0.04% slow random pitch drift.

Nothing is secretly changed because a user selects the word "organic."

#################### 154_PUBLIC_RELEASE_TUNING_COMPATIBILITY_ADVISOR.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.9

154 — PUBLIC RELEASE TUNING COMPATIBILITY ADVISOR
=================================================

IMPORTANT

Public release does not automatically require A440.

A440 is the current ISO standard musical-pitch reference, which makes it a strong
interoperability default for instruments, sessions and collaborators.

Poietek therefore uses ADVISORY logic.

EXAMPLE

Project:
A432.

Destination:
No explicit tuning requirement.

RESULT:
PASS.

Advisory:
"Your project uses A432. Keep the original. A separate A440 compatibility master
is available if a collaborator/client system specifically expects standard pitch."

STRICT TARGET

If a client/session/ensemble explicitly requires A440:

NOT READY for that target.

Actions:
Preserve original project.
Create separate A440 compatibility version.
Review audible quality.
Export both when appropriate.

NO FEAR WARNING

Poietek must not tell users:
"Radio will reject A432"
unless the selected destination actually documents such a requirement.

END DOCUMENT 154

#################### 155_DUAL_MASTER_ORIGINAL_AND_COMPATIBILITY_EXPORT.txt ####################

155 — DUAL MASTER: ORIGINAL + COMPATIBILITY EXPORT
==================================================

USE CASE

Creator wants A432 master but client/collaborator asks for A440 compatibility.

EXPORT SET:

01 ORIGINAL
SongName_MASTER_A432.wav

02 COMPATIBILITY
SongName_MASTER_A440_COMPAT.wav

03 METADATA
SongName_TUNING.txt/json

Both reference the same release lineage but different render variants.

RIGHTS

No new copyright ownership is inferred from compatibility rendering.

HASHES

Each render receives:
Asset ID
SHA-256
Rights Manifest link
tuning profile
render provenance.

QUALITY

Compatibility retune must be auditioned.
Do not label it lossless if an audio pitch-shift operation was required.

#################### 159_TUNING_HISTORY_EVIDENCE_NOTES.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.9

159 — TUNING HISTORY & EVIDENCE NOTES
=====================================

CURRENT VERIFIED HISTORY

ISO 16:1975 specifies A=440 Hz and remains current after its 2022 review.

Historical research and contemporary 1939 reporting show that A440 emerged through
decades of international negotiation and an International Standards Association
conference held at BBC Broadcasting House in London on 11–12 May 1939.

Before standardization, pitch varied materially between locations, periods and
ensembles.

PRODUCT POLICY

Poietek does not encode unsupported claims that one family, individual or secret
group recently changed the musical standard.

Historical notes can include:
verified source
date
confidence
alternative interpretations where serious scholarship differs.

CREATIVE FREEDOM

Historical uncertainty or politics does not affect a user's right to choose A432,
A415, custom pitch or another temperament in Poietek.

END DOCUMENT 159

#################### 163_REALTIME_PITCH_SHIFT_TEMPO_PRESERVATION.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 3.0

163 — REAL-TIME PITCH SHIFT & TEMPO PRESERVATION
================================================

COMPANION
packages/player/src/PlaybackTuning.ts
packages/player/src/TunedMediaBackend.ts

A432 -> A440

Ratio:
440 / 432 = 1.0185185...

Pitch offset:
approximately +31.76665 cents.

A440 -> A432:
approximately -31.76665 cents.

PLAYER REQUIREMENT

Change pitch while preserving:
tempo
duration
video synchronization.

NAIVE RESAMPLING

Changes pitch AND speed.

Do not use naïve resampling for normal community tuned playback.

HIGH-QUALITY MODE

Use a time-preserving pitch-shift algorithm.

QUALITY TIERS

REALTIME PREVIEW
lower CPU/latency.

HIGH QUALITY
better offline/server/local derivative render.

CREATOR RENDERED
creator supplies a dedicated alternate master.

PREFERRED ORDER

Creator alternate rendition
High-quality cached Poietek rendition
Realtime pitch shift
Original fallback.

LIMITATIONS

Pitch shifting a finished master can alter:
transients
stereo image
formants/timbre
phase
codec artifacts.

Player must never promise alternate playback is identical to a separately mastered
version.

END DOCUMENT 163

#################### 164_VIDEO_PLAYER_TUNING_AND_AV_SYNC.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 3.0

164 — VIDEO PLAYER TUNING & AUDIO/VIDEO SYNC
============================================

VIDEO DURATION MUST STAY FIXED.

If the community video player's audio is retuned:
pitch changes
tempo/duration remain fixed.

This preserves:
lip sync
cuts
captions
chapters
timecode-aligned metadata.

Do not change audio playback speed from 432 -> 440 for a normal music video.

SPECIAL CASE

If a creator intentionally wants:
slower/faster visual playback with pitch,
that is a separate creative playback mode.

PLAYER PIPELINE

Video decode
+
audio decode
-> tuned audio DSP
-> common media clock
-> output.

SYNC MONITOR

Measure:
audio/video drift
buffer underrun
retune processor latency.

Apply latency compensation.

END DOCUMENT 164

POIETEK CURRENT IMPLEMENTATION-READINESS BENCHMARK

#################### v2_1_OFFICIAL_REFERENCE_NOTES_2026-08-11.txt ####################

Official sources reviewed 11 August 2026:

PRS for Music:
- Online member account sign-up uses a membership number (CAE/IPI).
- Nexus exposes recording ISRC, work ISWC, PRS Tunecode, and writer IPI data.
- Registered PRS works receive a unique tune code.

PPL:
- recording rightsholder members register new recordings;
- performers claim performances on recordings already in PPL repertoire.

The MLC:
- eligible members can register individual works, bulk works, or CWR;
- writer IPI can be used in writer search/registration workflows.

SoundExchange:
- creators/rights owners register and manage repertoire/royalty information;
- official materials describe ISRC-rich repertoire submissions and DDEX/flat-file routes.

DDEX:
- RIN captures project/session/contributor/recording/work data and supports
  proprietary identifiers by namespace;
- RIN can link messages to audio using URI/hash;
- RDR-N exchanges recording/music-video contributor and rights-claim data;
- production implementations require an implementation licence.

C2PA:
- current Content Credentials specification is 2.4 and is designed for
  signed media provenance/history.

Stripe Connect:
- separate charges and transfers can split a platform payment across multiple
  connected accounts, subject to geographic/processor rules.

Apple / Google Play:
- current policies govern digital goods, creator content, tips/gifts and
  alternative billing by storefront/territory. Commerce must be policy-routed.

#################### v2_1_docs__71_CONTRIBUTOR_PASSPORT_SOCIETY_IDENTIFIER_ABSTRACTION.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.1

71 — CONTRIBUTOR PASSPORT & SOCIETY IDENTIFIER ABSTRACTION
==========================================================

COMPANION
schemas/contributor-passport.schema.v1.json
packages/identity/src/ExternalIdentifierVault.ts

CORE IDEA

Poietek gives every person/company a stable internal Party ID.

External identifiers are attached behind it:
PRS/MCPS membership/CAE/IPI
PPL account/member identifiers
The MLC identifiers
SoundExchange identifiers
ISNI
publisher/label identifiers
other societies.

The everyday UI shows the creator profile, not a wall of codes.

EXAMPLE

Maya Jones
Producer · Performer

Verified:
PRS ✓
PPL ✓

Behind the scenes:
Poietek Party ID -> verified external identifiers.

EXPORT/REGISTRATION resolves only the identifiers required for that destination.

SECURITY

Raw external IDs:
encrypted at rest where sensitive;
masked in normal UI;
never embedded in public media unless the identifier is intended/public for that
purpose;
never shared with unrelated collaborators.

DDEX FIT

DDEX RIN supports standard identifiers and proprietary identifiers expressed with
a namespace plus the identifier. Poietek can therefore preserve provider-specific
IDs without making them its own canonical identity.

IDENTIFIER VERIFICATION

self_declared
format_checked
provider_confirmed
admin_verified.

A self-declared identifier is not presented as provider-verified.

ACCEPTANCE
ID-001 one Party links multiple societies.
ID-002 company and person identities distinct.
ID-003 contributor profile remains stable if provider ID changes.
ID-004 public UI masks private external IDs.
ID-005 registration export resolves exact provider-required identifier.

#################### v2_1_docs__72_PRODUCTION_SESSION_MULTI_DEVICE_TEAM_ACCESS.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.1

72 — PRODUCTION SESSION, MULTI-DEVICE TEAM ACCESS & GUEST CLAIMING
=================================================================

COMPANION
schemas/project-access-policy.schema.v1.json
packages/identity/src/ContributorSession.ts

JOIN OPTIONS

1 Contributor scans project QR on their own phone/tablet/laptop.
2 Contributor opens invite link/email.
3 Producer searches an existing Poietek profile.
4 Contributor creates a guest identity on the studio device.
5 Guest identity is claimed later from their own device/account.

OWNER / ADMIN CONTROLS

Role.
Permissions.
Access mode.
Expiry.
Concurrent sessions.
Devices per contributor.
Project-only or reusable team membership.
Revoke device.
Suspend contributor.
Allow/deny export.
Allow/deny rights editing.
Allow/deny financial visibility.

ACCESS MODES

ONE_SESSION
Valid for the current production session.

TIME_LIMITED
Ends at specified time.

PROJECT_LIFETIME
Access continues for this project.

TEAM_REUSABLE
Contributor can join future projects in the same team when invited/allowed.

ORGANISATION
Company/studio account policy.

UNLIMITED
No project-level device/session cap, subject to system/security/product plan.

IMPORTANT

"Unlimited" does not mean unauthenticated.
Every active device/session still has identity, token, audit and revocation.

STUDIO-DEVICE GUEST

Contributor enters:
display name
email/phone optional
role
society IDs optional.

Poietek creates:
Temporary Party
Session contribution record.

Later:
Claim this contribution
-> authenticate on contributor's own device
-> prove invite/claim token
-> merge temporary Party into permanent Party
-> preserve original audit history.

CREDITS SURVIVE ACCESS REVOCATION

Removing access must never remove:
credits
approved rights
session history
registration evidence.

FINALIZATION

When a recording is selected as final:
Poietek asks contributors with unresolved metadata/rights to review.
Remote contributors receive review tasks.
Owner cannot silently impersonate another contributor's approval.

ACCEPTANCE
TEAM-001 QR join works.
TEAM-002 studio-device guest can claim later.
TEAM-003 owner can revoke one device.
TEAM-004 revoked user retains credits.
TEAM-005 unlimited access still records sessions.
TEAM-006 contributor can use several permitted devices.
TEAM-007 rights approval requires correct Party identity.

#################### v2_1_docs__73_UNIVERSAL_RIGHTS_MANIFEST_EMBEDDED_METADATA.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.1

73 — UNIVERSAL RIGHTS MANIFEST & DOCUMENT-LIKE EMBEDDED METADATA
================================================================

COMPANION
schemas/universal-rights-manifest.schema.v1.json

APPLIES TO

audio
video
images
samples
presets
plugins
skins/themes
clips
templates
documents
other creator content.

THREE DISTINCT LAYERS

CREDIT
Who contributed.

RIGHT
Who owns/administers/licences what.

PAYMENT
Who receives money under a transaction/agreement.

Do not automatically give every contributor ownership or payment simply because
they are credited.

DOCUMENT-LIKE INSPECTOR

Selecting a file can show:

File properties
Creation date/time
Content hash
Author/creator
Credits
Rights
Identifiers
Licence
Society registrations
Provenance
Commerce policy
Payment beneficiaries
Version/history.

EMBEDDING

Project standard metadata for common tools.
Use media-specific standard tags.
Use C2PA Content Credentials for signed provenance where supported.
Use DDEX structures for relevant music-industry exchanges.
Embed a compact/public Poietek rights projection where compatibility is proven.
Keep the complete canonical manifest separately by content hash.

WHY EXTERNAL CANONICAL COPY

Transcoders/distributors can strip unknown metadata.
A file must not be the sole copy of a legal/rights record.

PRIVACY

Never embed:
banking info
tax data
private contracts
OAuth tokens
private society credentials
private addresses.

ACCEPTANCE
META-001 standard media remains valid.
META-002 common metadata remains readable.
META-003 Poietek renders complete rights document.
META-004 public embed excludes private fields.
META-005 manifest version is linked to exact content hash.

#################### v2_1_docs__74_FINAL_MASTER_RIGHTS_SYNC_REGISTRATION_PIPELINE.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.1

74 — FINAL MASTER RIGHTS SYNC & REGISTRATION PIPELINE
=====================================================

COMPANION
packages/rights/src/SocietyConnector.ts

DO NOT REGISTER EVERY EXPORT

A rough bounce is not a release.

TRIGGERS

Mark as Final Master
Create Release Candidate
Approve Final Video
Publish Marketplace Product.

PIPELINE

1 Freeze project/release metadata snapshot.
2 Resolve contributors.
3 Resolve external identifiers.
4 Check missing credits.
5 Check rights claims/splits.
6 Request outstanding contributor approvals.
7 Generate Rights Manifest version.
8 Generate DDEX/CWR/bulk/portal packages as applicable.
9 User/authorized owner approves submissions.
10 Connector submits/exports/hands off.
11 Save receipts and assigned external IDs.
12 Embed approved public metadata into final deliverables.
13 Generate commerce policy.
14 Publish/distribute only when required gates pass.

PRS/MCPS

PRS assigns a unique tune code to registered works, and Nexus exposes links among
work/recording metadata such as tunecode, ISWC, ISRC and writer IPI.
Poietek should store these returned identifiers against the work/recording.

PPL

New recordings are registered by recording-rightsholder members; performers claim
performances on existing PPL repertoire. Poietek routes users according to their
capacity rather than submitting the wrong claim type.

THE MLC

Member workflows currently support individual, bulk and CWR registration routes.

SOUNDEXCHANGE

SoundExchange Direct lets creators/rights owners search/claim/manage repertoire;
SoundExchange also documents DDEX and flat-file repertoire submissions for
registered rights owners.

SUBMITTED != ACCEPTED

Store both states separately.

ACCEPTANCE
FINAL-001 unresolved dispute blocks automated release.
FINAL-002 missing IPI produces warning where needed.
FINAL-003 returned Tune Code stored.
FINAL-004 performer is not treated as PPL recording rightsholder automatically.
FINAL-005 submission receipt immutable.

#################### v2_1_docs__75_DDEX_C2PA_OPTIONAL_BLOCKCHAIN_PROVENANCE.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.1

75 — DDEX, C2PA & OPTIONAL BLOCKCHAIN PROVENANCE
================================================

DDEX RIN

Capture studio creation metadata:
sessions
contributors
roles
projects
recordings
musical works.

RIN can link messages to audio files using URI and hash values.

DDEX RDR-N

Designed for exchanging sound-recording/music-video contributor information and
rights claims in licensing-company workflows.

C2PA

Use Content Credentials for cryptographically signed provenance/history across
supported media.

POIETEK MANIFEST

Human-facing and application-facing canonical rights graph that maps into these
standards.

BLOCKCHAIN

Optional evidence anchor only.

Store:
hash of approved manifest
timestamp/receipt
network identifier.

Do not store:
private contracts
personal data
bank data
unreleased media
revocable credentials.

A blockchain transaction is not proof that:
the copyright claim is legally valid;
the collecting society accepted it;
the contributor was actually paid.

Every manifest amendment creates a new hash/version.

DDEX LICENSING

Any production implementation of DDEX standards must follow DDEX's current
implementation-licence requirements.

ACCEPTANCE
PROV-001 RIN export links exact content hash.
PROV-002 C2PA verification reported clearly.
PROV-003 old blockchain anchor remains immutable after rights amendment.
PROV-004 no private data written to public chain.

#################### v2_1_docs__76_ROYALTY_STATEMENTS_RECONCILIATION_CORRECT_PATHWAYS.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.1

76 — ROYALTY RECONCILIATION & CORRECT-PATHWAY ENGINE
=====================================================

GOAL

Users should not need to understand every royalty pathway to use Poietek.

Poietek can infer a recommended pathway from:
territory
role
right type
content type
society memberships
distribution choice
licence/use.

EXAMPLE

UK songwriter:
composition registrations -> relevant work society/publisher route.

UK recording rightsholder:
PPL repertoire route.

Performer:
PPL performer claim route.

US mechanical rightsholder:
The MLC route when applicable.

US digital sound-recording performance:
SoundExchange route when applicable.

These are examples, not universal legal determinations.

PATHWAY ENGINE

Input:
Party
RightsGraph
Territory
Release
Usage.

Output:
Required
Recommended
Optional
Not applicable
Needs legal review.

ROYALTY STATEMENT IMPORT

Normalize society/distributor/marketplace statements.

Match:
ISRC
ISWC
Tune Code
provider IDs
metadata.

MATCH STATES

exact_identifier
high_confidence
possible
unmatched
conflict.

Never auto-allocate ambiguous money.

CORRECT-PAYMENT GOAL

Embedded metadata improves matching but cannot itself guarantee payment.

Correct routing requires:
approved rights;
valid registrations;
correct identifiers;
usage reports;
provider processing;
ledger reconciliation;
payout account;
tax/KYC where applicable;
dispute resolution.

ACCEPTANCE
PATH-001 performer/rightsholder routes differ.
PATH-002 unknown territory returns review, not invented advice.
PATH-003 unmatched royalty line stays unmatched.
PATH-004 original statement retained immutable.

#################### v2_1_docs__77_MARKETPLACE_COMMERCE_LEDGER_AUTOMATIC_SPLITS.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.1

77 — MARKETPLACE COMMERCE, LEDGER & AUTOMATIC SPLITS
====================================================

COMPANIONS
schemas/commerce-policy.schema.v1.json
packages/commerce/src/AllocationEngine.ts
packages/commerce/src/PaymentRail.ts
database/rights-commerce-ledger.v1.sql

POIETEK ECOSYSTEM MAY SELL

songs
albums
videos
clips
samples
sample packs
presets
plugins
themes/skins
templates
tutorials
licences
other digital creator goods.

ONE SALE

Buyer pays once.

Transaction records:
product
rights manifest version
commerce policy version
gross
tax
processor fee
platform fee
refund reserve if needed
distributable amount
beneficiary allocations.

AUTOMATIC SPLIT

Use approved CommercePolicy.

Example only:
Creator A 5000 bps
Creator B 2500 bps
Company C 1500 bps
Platform 1000 bps.

Actual splits come from approved agreement/policy, never AI inference.

INTEGER MONEY

All ledger calculations use minor units and deterministic rounding.

PAYMENT PROCESSOR

A marketplace rail such as Stripe Connect can support splitting a platform charge
into transfers to multiple connected accounts.

Still required:
seller onboarding;
identity/KYC as required by processor/law;
payout account;
refund/chargeback reconciliation;
tax handling;
territory availability.

HISTORICAL IMMUTABILITY

Order stores CommercePolicy version.
Changing tomorrow's split does not rewrite yesterday's sale.

DISPUTE

Freeze affected unpaid allocation.
Do not delete ledger history.
Resolve through adjustment entries.

ACCEPTANCE
SELL-001 shares total exactly 10000 bps.
SELL-002 £/$0.01 rounding deterministic.
SELL-003 refund creates reversal/adjustment.
SELL-004 old sale retains old policy.
SELL-005 missing payout account holds beneficiary balance rather than paying wrong person.

#################### v2_1_docs__78_TIPS_DONATIONS_FANS_LISTENERS_CREATOR_SUPPORT.txt ####################

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.1

78 — LISTENER SUPPORT, TIPS, GIFTS, DONATIONS & CREATOR ECONOMY
==============================================================

SUPPORT TYPES

Purchase
Tip
Gift
Membership/subscription
Pay-what-you-want
Crowdfunding/campaign later
Charitable donation only where legal/platform requirements are met.

IMPORTANT TERMINOLOGY

A payment to an artist is generally a tip/gift/support payment unless it meets the
legal requirements for a charitable donation.

Do not market creator tips as tax-deductible donations.

FAN EXPERIENCE

Listen/watch/view.
Follow.
Like/comment.
Buy.
Tip.
Gift.
Subscribe/support.
Purchase licences/content.
Attend/participate in creator community.

RIGHTS-AWARE SUPPORT

Tip to one creator:
payment policy determines recipient.

Purchase collaborative song:
approved CommercePolicy determines sale allocations.

No rights claim should be inferred from a fan payment.

MOBILE STORE ROUTING

CommercePolicyRouter checks:
platform
storefront/territory
product
digital vs physical
tip/gift vs content purchase
current store rules.

On Apple platforms, creator content is treated as UGC/creator content and digital
purchases are subject to App Review payment rules; optional monetary gifts have a
separate rule when 100% goes to the receiver and are not connected to digital
content/services.

On Google Play, digital goods/services generally use Play Billing unless an
eligible current alternative-billing program applies; peer-to-peer/tax-exempt
donation categories are treated differently.

Never hard-code one global mobile checkout.

ACCEPTANCE
FAN-001 tip never grants copyright.
FAN-002 purchase licence is explicit.
FAN-003 app-store rail selected by current policy.
FAN-004 blocked/refunded payment updates ledger.

POIETEK MASTER DEVELOPMENT LIBRARY — VERSION 2.1

79 — MANAGED SERVICES, MONETIZATION & FREE-PROVIDER ABSTRACTION
==============================================================

OWNER-SIDE PROVIDERS

During prototype/early operation, Poietek's operator may combine:
Supabase
Firebase
object storage
GitHub
Hugging Face
other free/open services
local/self-hosted workers.

END USERS SHOULD NOT NEED THOSE ACCOUNTS.

User sees:
Poietek account
Poietek project
Poietek cloud/sync status
Poietek marketplace.

Provider routing is infrastructure.

EXCEPTIONS

Users/sellers may need to onboard directly or indirectly with:
payment/payout processor;
collecting society;
distributor;
regulated identity/tax service

when the external organisation legally requires the account/mandate.

FREE-TIER ROUTER

ServiceRouter selects provider using:
capability
quota
cost
latency
privacy
region
availability
licence.

NEVER "FREE FOREVER"

Free provider terms/quotas can change.
Production architecture must support paid migration and provider replacement.

MONETIZATION OPTIONS

Free local core.
Optional subscription for managed cloud/team services.
Storage/compute tiers.
AI compute credits.
Marketplace commission.
Transaction/platform fee.
Premium collaboration/team administration.
Creator storefront tools.
Enterprise/studio/education plans.
Optional paid support.

DO NOT CHARGE USERS FOR THEIR OWN RIGHTS

Poietek may charge for:
software/service/automation/marketplace infrastructure.

It should not imply the fee creates the copyright or royalty entitlement.

SUSTAINABILITY

The user experience remains simple even if Poietek internally uses many providers.

ACCEPTANCE

BIZ-002 user can export project/data.
BIZ-003 subscription cancellation does not erase local project.
BIZ-004 marketplace fees disclosed.
BIZ-005 provider cost tracked per capability.

80 — END-TO-END CREATOR -> RELEASE -> SALE -> PAYMENT WORKFLOW
==============================================================

1 PROJECT OWNER CREATES PROJECT

Access policy:
private/team.
Device/session rules.

2 CONTRIBUTORS JOIN

Existing account
QR/link
studio-device guest
claim later.

3 CONTRIBUTIONS RECORDED

Party
role
track/clip/session
date/time
credit suggestions.

4 IDENTIFIERS RESOLVED

Poietek Party ID
-> verified PRS/PPL/MLC/SoundExchange/etc identifiers when applicable.

5 RIGHTS AGREED

Credit.
Rights.
Payment terms.

Separate objects.

6 CONTRIBUTORS APPROVE

No silent AI determination.

7 FINAL MASTER / FINAL CONTENT CREATED

Hash generated.
Rights Manifest version frozen.
C2PA/provenance generated where enabled.
DDEX packages generated where appropriate.

8 REGISTRATIONS

Correct pathway engine prepares:
work registration
recording registration
performer claims
rights claims
distribution metadata.

Provider confirmation stored.

9 PUBLISH

Poietek marketplace
external distributor
private delivery
licensing.

10 SALE/USE

Internal Poietek sale:
CommercePolicy version -> ledger allocations.

External royalties:
statements imported/synced -> matched/reconciled.

11 PAYOUT

Eligible internal balance -> approved payout rail/account.

External society royalties:
paid by the relevant organisation under its own process;
Poietek tracks/reconciles the statement/payment.

12 AUDIT

Every important step has:
actor
timestamp
manifest version
policy version
receipt
hash.

DESIRED USER EXPERIENCE

The creator sees:

Credits complete ✓
Rights approved ✓
PRS work: Accepted ✓
PPL recording: Accepted ✓
SoundExchange: Submitted
Marketplace split: Ready ✓
Payout accounts: 3/3 ready ✓
Release: Ready to publish

The technical complexity remains behind Poietek.

END DOCUMENT 80

#################### v2_2_OFFICIAL_BLOCKCHAIN_REFERENCES_2026-08-11.txt ####################

Official/current technical sources reviewed 11 August 2026:

Ethereum:
- Smart contracts expose block.timestamp as the current block epoch timestamp.
- Solidity documentation warns that block.timestamp can be influenced to some degree,
  so it should not be treated as a precision trusted clock.
- Account abstraction is intended to make smart-contract wallets easier to use and can
  support programmable recovery/batching and gas sponsorship.

EIP-712:
- Standard typed structured data hashing/signing.
- Includes domain separation; protocol must handle replay protection appropriately.

ERC-1271:
- Standard method for smart-contract wallets to validate signatures.

ERC-1155:
- Multi-token standard; useful only for optional tokenized digital-asset/licence research.
- Token ownership must not be confused with legal copyright ownership.

OpenZeppelin Contracts 5.x:
- Provides EIP712, SignatureChecker, MerkleProof, AccessControl/AccessManager,
  Pausable, Nonces and related mature primitives.

Primary references:
https://ethereum.org/developers/docs/smart-contracts/anatomy/
https://ethereum.org/roadmap/account-abstraction/
https://docs.soliditylang.org/en/latest/units-and-global-variables.html
https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-712
https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-1271
https://eips.ethereum.org/EIPS/eip-1155
https://docs.openzeppelin.com/contracts/5.x/api/utils/cryptography
https://docs.openzeppelin.com/contracts/5.x/access-control

#################### v2_5_OFFICIAL_AUDIO_HEALTH_REFERENCES_2026-08-11.txt ####################

Official/current sources reviewed 11 August 2026:

ITU-R BS.1770-5:
https://www.itu.int/rec/R-REC-BS.1770/en
Current in-force recommendation (approved November 2023) for algorithms to measure
audio programme loudness and true-peak audio level.

EBU R128 v5:
https://tech.ebu.ch/publications/r128
https://tech.ebu.ch/loudness/
Programme loudness recommendation and EBU Mode ecosystem. Current public EBU
material describes Momentary (400 ms), Short Term (3 s), Integrated and Loudness
Range/True Peak concepts; EBU supplies a loudness test set/reference pink-noise
material.

Spotify artist loudness guidance:
https://support.spotify.com/artists/article/loudness-normalization/
Current page states Normal playback normalization at -14 LUFS following ITU 1770,
with mastering guidance below -1 dBTP; louder-than--14-LUFS masters are advised to
stay below -2 dBTP to reduce encoding distortion.

Apple Digital Masters:
https://www.apple.com/uk/apple-music/apple-digital-masters/
Apple provides high-quality-master guidance and mastering/audition tools for
testing AAC encoding, peaks and clipping.

Apple MusicUnderstanding LoudnessResult:
https://developer.apple.com/documentation/musicunderstanding/loudnessresult
Apple documents integrated, momentary, short-term and peak loudness results aligned
with LUFS/ITU-R BS.1770.

MusicBrainz genres:
https://musicbrainz.org/doc/Genre
https://musicbrainz.org/doc/MusicBrainz_API
https://musicbrainz.org/genres
MusicBrainz treats genres as part of the tag system and notes that genres are
subjective; the API exposes an all-genres resource.

#################### v2_7_OFFICIAL_RELEASE_READINESS_REFERENCES_2026-08-11.txt ####################

Primary/current references reviewed 11 August 2026:

ISO 16:1975
https://www.iso.org/standard/3601.html
Standard musical pitch: A on treble stave = 440 Hz, accuracy 0.5 Hz.
ISO page says confirmed in 2022 and current.

ITU-R BS.1770
https://www.itu.int/rec/R-REC-BS.1770/en
BS.1770-5 (11/2023) is current/in force for programme loudness and true-peak
measurement algorithms.

EBU R128 v5
https://tech.ebu.ch/publications/r128
Current page recommends average programme loudness -23 LUFS plus Loudness Range
and Maximum True Peak descriptors.

ATSC A/85:2026-07
https://www.atsc.org/atsc-documents/a85-techniques-for-establishing-and-maintaining-audio-loudness-for-digital-television/
Approved 8 July 2026. Current recommended practice for audio loudness in digital
television and hybrid broadcast/streaming workflows.

#################### v2_8_TUNING_REFERENCE_NOTES_2026-08-11.txt ####################

Primary/current sources reviewed 11 August 2026:

ISO 16:1975:
https://www.iso.org/standard/3601.html
ISO specifies standard musical pitch A=440 Hz and lists this edition as confirmed
in 2022/current.

The MIDI Association:
https://midi.org/microtuning-and-alternative-intonation-systems
Official MIDI.org material describes the MIDI Tuning Standard and Scala SCL/KBM
microtuning interoperability.

MIDI 2.0:
Current MIDI Association developer material states MIDI 2.0 preserves MIDI Tuning
Standard concepts and adds per-note pitch/tuning mechanisms.

432 Hz research examples:
PubMed PMID 31031095:
Music Tuned to 440 Hz Versus 432 Hz and the Health Effects: double-blind crossover
pilot, 33 participants. Authors explicitly described the evidence gap and called
for larger trials.

PubMed PMID 35545982:
432 Hz vs 440 Hz randomized pilot in emergency nurses.

PubMed PMID 40061232:
2025 double-blind crossover study in kickboxers reported performance/response
differences but did not establish a universal health or "natural frequency"
standard.

Poietek policy:
Support A432 as creative/research option without presenting universal healing or
biological claims as settled science.

#################### v2_9_VERIFIED_HISTORY_REFERENCES_2026-08-11.txt ####################

Verified/current references used 11 August 2026:

ISO 16:1975
Official ISO page:
https://www.iso.org/standard/3601.html
Specifies A=440 Hz. Published 1975; reviewed and confirmed in 2022.

Nature, 27 May 1939
G. W. C. Kaye, "International Standard of Concert Pitch"
Documents the International Conference of 11–12 May 1939 held at Broadcasting
House under the International Standards Association, with delegates from France,
Germany, Great Britain, Holland and Italy plus views/representation from other
standards/broadcast bodies.

Max Planck Institute for the History of Science
"Tuning the World"
Describes A440 as the result of decades of international negotiation among
performers, composers, instrument builders, scientists and state actors.

No credible evidence was found in this review supporting a claim that the current
A440 standard was changed by the Rothschild family "a few years ago".

YouTube Help — Recommended upload encoding settings
https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/1722171
Current recommendations include MP4/H.264, AAC-LC/Opus/Eclipsa audio, 48 kHz
audio sample rate, native frame rate and BT.709 for SDR. No A440 tuning-reference
requirement was found on the page.

YouTube Help — Encoding specifications for music videos
https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/6039860
Current partner specification recommends FLAC or Linear PCM, 48 kHz recommended,
24-bit recommended, stereo (plus current immersive options). No A440 tuning
requirement was found on the page.

YouTube Help — Live encoder settings
https://support.google.com/youtube/answer/2853702
Current live delivery requirements focus on codecs, bitrate, sample rate, frame
rate, keyframes and colour space.

EBU R128:
https://tech.ebu.ch/publications/r128
Broadcast loudness profile remains a separate technical delivery concern from
musical tuning reference.
